# TODO - DG Coastal Ocean Model (dg-rs)

This roadmap is driven by the full-crate review of **2026-09-25** in `REVIEW.md`, which supersedes the 2026-07-08 review (in git history). See `reports/` for the 2026-02-11 ROMS/NorKyst comparison, math evaluation, BC assessment and devil's-advocate reports.

**Current state.**
- **What is solid:** the DG core (GLL operators, strong-form DGSEM, Roe/HLL, SSP-RK3, SoA/rayon), the tidal astronomy module, and the NorKyst ingest readers. 1,024 lib + 61 integration + 59 doctests pass (`--no-default-features --features parallel,simd`).
- **What the tests don't cover:** the suite runs in under 2 s, and the physics tests only exercise easy configurations.
- **What the review found broken:**
  - Realistic bathymetry is not well-balanced: metre-per-second spurious currents within an hour. (Since fixed opt-in by the split-form DGSEM, P1.2.)
  - The cell-average workaround leaks mass. (Since fixed, P0.13.)
  - Flather-type open boundaries reflect about 1/3 of outgoing waves. (Since fixed, P0.12.)
  - Coastline-fitted meshes cannot be loaded.
  - Mode splitting is first-order and damps the barotropic mode.
  - The 3D layer is scaffolding with three blocker-level math errors. (Vertical advection ×D since fixed, P0.16; tracer constancy and the σ pressure gradient since fixed, P4.2/P4.3, 2026-09-30.)
- **Cost:** as configured, roughly ~50× ROMS core-hours for a 2D tidal run (cost-model estimate, `REVIEW.md` §5).

**Strategic direction.**
1. **Make the 2D barotropic tide model correct, then cheap, then validated.** This is where DG's fjord-geometry and accuracy-per-DOF advantages can actually beat ROMS.
2. **Then rebuild 3D on the ROMS recipe.**

"Faster than ROMS" is a claim to be *proven* with a cost-vs-error benchmark (P3.2), plausible for 2D tides at P3+ with local time stepping or an implicit free surface. It should not be assumed.

P1.4 is done: one characteristic OBC (`CharacteristicOBC` + external-state providers), NorKyst-800 boundary tides from a harmonic atlas, and a `ModelClock`. 1. **P3.1 validation: the tooling is done; the month-long run is still to do.** Heimsjø and Kristiansund lie outside the Frøya domain. The run takes ≈ 9 h at 8.2 ms/step, so it waits until P2 makes it cheaper, or is started overnight:

The station nodes differ too: at 1 km the node is 325 m away and 16.4 m deep; at 500 m, 199 m away and 7.6 m deep.
     - `land_elevation=1.5` against 5 m: 0.3 mm RMS at Mausund. The shoreline cliffs are local and do not reach the gauge.

             - **Answered 2026-09-30: not the bed, a wet/dry bug.** The hotspot is a one-node-wide wet strip between land (+4 m) and a sill node (+0.35 m) in one element. On strong floods the `WetDry` subcell reconstruction kept the sill dry under up to 0.65 m of water: it took the sill's η = B for a water level and closed the interface. The strip piled up 0.15–0.3 m and a 3–5 m/s jet pushed at the sill. Fixed in `reconstruct_subcell` (no η slope through a dry node below the water, see CHANGELOG). Hours 0–25 re-run: 0.03 m/s there at hour 24, where it was 4.73; the largest speed is the (10.6, −13.8) km pinnacle again (2.83 m/s). The `rx0` default is still open (P1.3), now without this case.

Q1 is 1.9× the observed, K1 and S2 are 12–21 % high, M4 is 5× too weak, and M2 agrees (1.026, −0.5°). NorKyst's own complex-difference RSS against the gauge is 0.14 m, 0.13 m of it from N2. The Frøya run therefore re-infers the boundary atlas's N2 and Q1 from M2/O1 with Mausund's ratios (`gauge_ratios=N2,Q1`; since 2026-09-30 `N2,Q1,P1`, after `gauge_gains=K1,O1`). Report the N2 deficiency to MET, and check other NorKyst gauges (Bergen) for it.

Sampled there at the nearest point ≥ 3 m deep (51 m away, 3.3 m deep), the model sat in a perched pocket: mean +1.38 m, M2 0.30× the gauge's, no current. The example now samples only in submerged elements (every node ≥ 3 m deep), which at 1 km is 1.29 km from the gauge in 55 m of water. There, over hours 24–72 from 2025-06-15: M2 0.997×, −8.2° (2-day fit, N2 inferred), centred RMSE 4.4 cm against the gauge's prediction (3.2 cm at the old node, 325 m away and 16 m deep). The depth-averaged M2 current there is 3.6 mm/s against NorKyst's 0.25 m/s at its point 606 m from the gauge. Each gauge now also gets a station at its nearest atlas point, but that point lies in a shoreline element too.
     - 1 km: sampled 1.2 km from NorKyst's point (21 m deep). M2 ellipse 0.056 m/s at 160° against NorKyst's 0.246 m/s at 149°.

NorKyst's point is sampled 657 m away, 11 m deep: M2 current 4.7 mm/s, 2.4 mm/s at the gauge.
     - **Answered 2026-09-27: mostly the point-sampled bed** (`scripts/norkyst_current_comparison.py`: M2 ellipses of the run and of NorKyst's hourly ū, v̄ fitted identically over hours 24–72 at every NorKyst point in the domain).
       - Peak speeds are not comparable: NorKyst's ū is the total flow (coastal current, wind), and the model's peak speed was 0.45 of NorKyst's, tides only against everything.
       - Tide against tide, the model's M2 current is 0.89 of NorKyst's where both are over 120 m deep (depth ratio 1.00), but only 0.41 within 3 km of Mausund. The 50 m DEM there has 20–30 % land (skerries), with water of median 17–20 m between them; point samples at nodes 500 m apart land on skerries (1.5–3 m nodes where NorKyst has 45–55 m) and close the sounds.
       - With the L2-projected bed (`bed=projected`, now the example's default) the ratio near Mausund is 0.63, 0.88 domain-wide (0.83 point). At the NorKyst-point station (sampled 410 m away instead of 1.2 km) the M2 ellipse is at 157° against NorKyst's 149°, lag 65° against 62°, 0.071 against 0.246 m/s. The gauge improves too: centred RMSE 4.0 against 4.4 cm (prediction), 6.3 against 6.6 cm (observations).
       - In shallow water the comparison is limited by NorKyst itself: its smoothed bed is 2–3× deeper than the DEM where NorKyst has 3–60 m, so its ū is not the child's for the same transport. The η M2 ratio of 1.11 is aliasing (a 2-day fit lumps N2 and S2 into M2, and the model's N2 is gauge-corrected).
       - 500 m, projected (fit over hours 24–50): 0.86 domain-wide, 0.88 over 120 m, 0.68 within 3 km of Mausund (1 km: 0.61 over the same hours). The model is at ≈ 0.9 of NorKyst wherever the beds agree and approaches it near Mausund as the mesh refines. But at the NorKyst-point station (237 m away, 41 m deep) the M2 current drops to 0.010 m/s, in NorKyst's direction (148° against 149°). That is a local lee of islands the 500 m mesh resolves and NorKyst's 800 m grid (45–55 m deep there) does not have. The gauge at 500 m: centred RMSE 3.5 cm (prediction), 5.8 cm (observations), as with point samples.
       - [ ] Which is right near the skerries, the model's lee or NorKyst's open flow, needs observed currents (farm surveys, see above) and a coastline-fitted mesh (P1.3).
   - Re-run the Bergen NorKyst fit too: it used the truncated periods fixed in P0.15 ([PR #2](https://github.com/EmilLindfors/roms-rs/pull/2)).
2. **Shoreline cliffs (P1.6): fixed with Kartverket's topobathy model (2026-09-26).** Lone wet nodes next to land nodes at `LAND_ELEVATION` = +5 m had carried η jumps of ~1 m and 1–2.8 m/s through the tide. The topobathy model has land heights and depths in one grid (`scripts/kartverket_topobathy.sh`, `data/froya_topobathy.tif`). With it, plus land capped at 5 m and isolated wet nodes raised, the Frøya 1 km run has no cliff nodes: max |u| 0.9–1.4 m/s, and η extremes are only mm films. Centred RMSE at Mausund is 3.2 cm (point) / 3.1 cm (projected) against 4.1 cm on the old builder. Open: the sea part's vertical datum (below, P1.6).
3. **Quick checks on the Mausund sub-domain (2026-09-29).** A 22 × 19 km box around the gauge (`bbox=8.45,63.78,8.90,63.95`, `data/mausund_coast.msh`, its own atlas `data/mausund_boundary_tides.txt`; recipe in `docs/gmsh-meshes.md`) costs a quarter of Frøya: ≈ 30 min for 3 days alone on the machine. Always with `tide_transport=3` (see P1.4). Its absolute skill is below the full mesh's (3.1–4.2 cm centred against 2.3 cm), so use it for relative comparisons.
4. **Then let the error budget choose:** geometry (P1.3) if the coastline dominates the error, cost (P2, profile first) if run time does.
5. **η-based limiting is on hold.** The subcells limit the partially dry elements, and tides are smooth. Revisit only if a real run shows oscillations.

Build note: default features need the `roms-rs` conda env (`conda activate roms-rs`) for HDF5/netCDF. Otherwise use `cargo test --no-default-features --features parallel,simd`.

## Application target: currents around a salmon farm

Goal (added 2026-09-26): resolve currents at a farm site (cages ≈ 40–60 m across, nets 20–40 m deep) inside a NorKyst-driven coastal domain, and track sea-lice larvae, feed and faeces from the cages. A 3D visualisation (Bevy, separate crate) reads the model output; it is not part of `dg-rs`.

This section only orders the existing items for that goal and adds the two farm-specific ones (F.1, F.2). Everything else lives in its own priority section.

**Stage A: 2D farm-scale currents.** Depth-averaged, so it answers tidal exposure and the wake of the farm, not the surface layer.
1. P1.3 geometry (blocker): coastline-fitted meshes with elements of tens of metres at the farm and kilometres offshore. General quadrilaterals are supported in 2D since 2026-09-26, and all-quad Gmsh meshes (MSH 4.1/2.2) load since 2026-09-26 (see `docs/gmsh-meshes.md`). A coastline-fitted Frøya mesh is built from the topobathy model since 2026-09-27 (`scripts/gmsh_coastline_mesh.py`, `froya_real_data mesh=`); since 2026-09-28 without the degenerate quads that set its time step (1.84× faster), it costs ≈ 4× the 500 m grid per model hour, mostly in the local time stepping's stage dependency (see P1.3). Triangles or quad-dominant meshes and CSR are still open.
2. P1.6 shoreline cliffs and bathymetry: done at 50 m (2026-09-26: `BedRaster`, `Bathymetry2D::project`, Kartverket's topobathy model, `raise_isolated_wet_nodes`). Farm-scale meshes (tens of metres) need the service's 1 m level, which has depths only inside surveyed projects: merge it over the 50 m model where it has data (see P1.6).
3. ~~F.1 cage drag (2D form)~~ done 2026-09-26 (`CageDrag2D`, `with_cage_drag`).
4. ~~P2.5 local time stepping~~ done 2026-09-27 (`MultirateSSPRK3`: 2.4–2.8× on a 20 m farm mesh; with horizontal viscosity since 2026-09-28; see P2.5 for the follow-ups).
5. ~~P1.5 nesting of NorKyst ū, v̄, η and the P1.6 atmospheric forcing reader~~ done 2026-09-27 (`OceanModelState` with transport scaling, relaxation band and bed blending; `AtmosphereReader` + `GriddedAtmosphere2D`; gauge-corrected tides with NorKyst's residual, `with_tidal_correction`; see P1.5 for the follow-ups).
6. ~~P3.1 current validation~~ tooling done 2026-09-27: station series of η, ū, v̄ sampled at the station by evaluating the DG polynomial there (`PointLocator2D`, `Probe2D`; F.2 reuses them), tidal-ellipse fit and complex difference (`fit_tidal_ellipses`), ADCP comparison paired by time. Still to do: real current data (farm-site surveys, see P3.1).
7. ~~F.2 particle tracking in 2D~~ done 2026-09-28 (`particles::ParticleTracker2D`: RK4 on the DG velocity, random walk, walls, open exits, stranding; online or between snapshots; see F.2 for the follow-ups).
8. ~~Minor: `SpatiallyVaryingManning2D` as `BottomFriction2D` (P1.2); the vacuous tests in P1.7~~ done 2026-09-28.

**Stage B: 3D.** Lice larvae live in the upper few metres and respond to salinity, so dispersion needs the stratified surface layer.
- P4.2 (tracer constancy and 3D open boundaries: done 2026-09-30; momentum in inventory form: done 2026-09-30; 3D nesting with the NorKyst/ROMS 3D reader: done 2026-09-30; not yet run on real NorKyst output), P4.3 (balanced PGF: done 2026-09-30; vertical grid), P4.4 (GLS: done 2026-10-01, with `k`, `ψ` advected since 2026-10-01; quadratic bottom drag: done 2026-09-30), P4.5 (3D wet/dry: done 2026-09-30; vertical advection order: done 2026-09-30; 3D horizontal viscosity: done 2026-10-01, with Smagorinsky and limited Akima at fronts: done 2026-10-01; parallel, non-allocating 3D kernels: done 2026-10-01; split-form momentum advection: done 2026-10-02, P2 no longer needs viscosity to be stable), rivers (P1.6 volume sources: done 2026-10-01, `source::river`; the NVE/ROMS river-file reader is open, P5.1), then P4.6 validation (the idealised fjord estuary: done 2026-10-01; the stratified seamount: done 2026-10-01, it found the rest state unstable over slopes, fixed by the σ-pairs PGF with split-form tracer advection).
- General quadrilaterals in 3D (P1.3): done 2026-10-03, so the coastline-fitted meshes run in 3D. A 3D run on the Frøya coastline mesh is next (P1.3).
- ~~F.1 cage drag (3D form)~~ done 2026-10-01 (`Hydrostatic3D::with_cage_drag`); ~~F.2 in 3D~~ done 2026-10-01 (`ParticleTracker3D`: σ-levels, Visser's walk, sinking and settling); ~~lice larvae behaviour~~ done 2026-10-01 (`SalmonLice`: light-seeking, salinity avoidance, degree-days). All together in `examples/farm_3d.rs`.

Not needed for this goal: P0.11/P2.7 (GPU, MPI), P3.2, P5.2–P5.4, most of P6 and P7. Until Stage B is validated, the particle tracker and the visualisation can be developed against NorKyst-800 3D fields.

### F.1 Cage drag
- [x] Momentum sink from the net as a porous region: S = −½·C_d·a·|u|u per unit volume, with a (net area per volume) and C_d from the net solidity (Løland 1991; review in Klebert et al. 2013, Ocean Eng. 58). Fouling raises the solidity, so it is a per-cage parameter (`NetCage`, `NetCage::circular` with Løland's C_d and a = 4/(πR)).
  - [x] 2D (2026-09-26, `source/swe_2d/cage.rs`): integrated over the net depth only, Λ = ½C_d a |u| min(d_net, h)/h, over the cage footprint.
  - [x] 3D (2026-10-01, `physics::cage_drag`, `Hydrostatic3D::with_cage_drag`): per σ-layer, weighted by the layer's share above the net bottom; linearised like the 3D bottom drag (`−Λ̄ū` point-implicit in the barotropic pass, the shear part in `G`, `−λ_l u_l` implicit in the vertical solve). Gates in `simulation_3d.rs`: unsheared flow decays as the 2D drag (first order), only the layers inside the net slow down, wind against nets reaches `Σ H_l λ_l u_l = τ/ρ₀` to 8.7e-14.
    - [ ] `G`'s shear part `−D Σ Δσ_l λ_l (u_l − ū)` is explicit (AB3-extrapolated with the rest of `G`): at `λΔt ≥ 10` it overshoots and reverses the stopped flow by < 1 % of `u₀` (`a_strong_cage_drag_is_stable_at_long_steps`; none up to 5). Harmless at farm steps (`λ ≈ 0.005–0.02 /s` at 0.5–1 m/s, Δt ≲ 60 s), but a coarse 3D run with long baroclinic steps through a farm should keep `λΔt` below ≈ 5. The bottom drag's shear part has the same structure. Measured 2026-10-01: not the AB3 extrapolation (with `G` frozen at `tⁿ` the numbers are identical; the first step, AB1, already overshoots). It is the split itself: the pass damps the whole `ū` at `Λ̄` while the drag acts only on the netted layers. Any implicit rate `Λ_imp ≥ 0` in the pass, with `G` taking `D Σ Δσ_l λ_l u_l − Λ_imp ū` at `tⁿ`, keeps the steady balance exact; the drag-weighted mean `Λ_imp = Σ a_l λ_l (u_l·D̃)/|D̃|²`, `a_l = Δσ_l λ_l/(1 + Δtλ_l)`, `D̃ = Σ a_l u_l`, makes one pass equal the vertical solve's backward-Euler mean when nothing else acts, but the pass's per-stage implicit damping compounds to ≈ `e^{−ΛΔt}`, not `1/(1 + ΛΔt)`, so the match needs `G` and the rate chosen against that (and `dt` in `G`, which `slow_forcing_into` does not get). Not worth it at farm steps.
    - [ ] Wake turbulence: the nets produce TKE (`P = Σ λ_l |u_l|²` per unit mass, as in canopy models; Beudin et al. 2017 for vegetation in ROMS/COAWST) that GLS does not see; the wake's mixing below the cages comes only from the shear the drag makes. Since 2026-10-01 `k` and `ψ` are advected (P4.4), so net-made TKE would be carried into the wake: add it as a source of the `k` equation (and `ψ`'s, with Beudin's `c_ψ4`) per caged layer.
    - [x] A 3D farm run: `examples/farm_3d.rs` (2026-10-01), two cages in a stratified, periodic tidal channel, 3D against 2D with and without cages, the profile behind a cage, the drag force, and particles (larvae, faeces, feed) from the cages. At 3 h (≈ 0.34 m/s):
      - The depth-mean wake is the same in 3D and 2D (10.0 % deficit 50 m behind the cage in both; 5.1 against 4.4 % at 400 m), but in 3D it is a 20 % deficit over the net's 20 m, unchanged 100 m behind, with 4–12 % more current underneath.
      - The drag on the farm is 90 kN in 3D against 72 kN in 2D: the current is surface-intensified (0.37 m/s in the top layers, 0.34 depth mean) and the nets hang in the fast part. A depth-averaged model underestimates cage loads by ≈ 20 % here.
      - Feed (10 cm/s) lands ≈ 110 m down-current, faeces (3 cm/s) ≈ 290 m.
      - Larvae released over the top 5 m stay there (passive, `lice=passive`: 10/50/90 % at 0.5/2.3/4.2 m after 1 h; swimming, the default `lice=ladim` since 2026-10-01: 0.0/0.6/2.4 m, all swimming up in the 03:00 UTC June light): with no wind and a 2 °C thermocline at 10 m, GLS leaves K ≈ 1e-6 m²/s above it (1e-4 at 15 m, 2–3e-2 in the bottom boundary layer). Real sites need the wind (and the surface-wave TKE item in P4.4) before larval depth statistics mean anything. Since 2026-10-01 `farm_3d wind= waves= closure=` has both: generic GLS under 0.1 Pa gives K ≈ 2e-3 m²/s at 1 m (log-layer surface) or 1e-2 (breaking waves, z₀ₛ = 1 m), and the larvae spread to 0.4/2.7/6.1 m or 0.8/3.4/7.2 m; real sites still need the wind and `H_s` from forcing files (P4.4).
      - With `k`, `ψ` advected (P4.4, 2026-10-01) the farm is unchanged to the reported digits: wake 10.02 % at 50 m and 5.03 % at 400 m, drag 89.9 kN. At 0.34 m/s the channel's turbulence is made and dissipated where it is; advection should matter at fronts, sills and in wind-mixed layers carried into still water, not here.
      - Still to do: a real fjord site with a tide through an open boundary; the 2D and 3D undisturbed currents differ (0.31 against 0.34 m/s) because Chézy on ū is not the 3D log-layer drag on the bottom layer (the P4.4 friction-consistency item).
    - [ ] The splitter's layer-drag buffer is dense (`[element][node][level]`, `ModeSplitIntegrator` `layer_drag_rate`) and `Hydrostatic3D::layer_drag_into` zeroes all of it every step, though only the cage nodes are ever nonzero. Cheap next to a 3D step; a sparse form over `CageDrag2D::nodes` (rates per caged column) would also let the vertical solve skip the lookup.
  - [x] Cages are comparable to or smaller than an element: each node is weighted by the area of its GLL subcell inside the footprint over w·J (adaptive bisection on the signed distance), so Σ wJφ is the footprint area on any quadrilateral.
- [x] Point-implicit with the bottom friction (`ImplicitDamping2D::cages`, `SWEPhysics2DBuilder::with_cage_drag`).
- [x] Gate tests (`tests/cage_drag_test.rs`):
  - Porous band across a periodic channel driven by a body force: momentum-flux and pressure loss across the band = drag − forcing (1e-3); total drag = total forcing (1e-4).
  - Mass conserved; lake at rest over a rough bed with cages exact (1e-10).
  - Magnitude: domain-wide cage against a body force reaches the analytic u² = 2Gh/(C_d a min(d, h)) (1e-6); decay of uniform flow converges at first order.
- [ ] Follow-ups:
  - The rear net sees the cage-mean velocity; Løland's velocity reduction behind a panel (r = 1 − 0.46 C_d) is left to the resolved flow. Compare the wake with a farm survey or published flume data before trusting the default a = 4/(πR).
  - Angle dependence (C_d(θ)) and the lift term: irrelevant for a circular cage averaged over its perimeter, not for square cages in oblique flow.
  - Net deformation in strong currents (the net lifts and its projected depth shrinks, reducing d_net) is not modelled.
  - ~~A cage-layout reader (positions, radii, net depths per site), e.g. from the Fiskeridirektoratet site register, for real farms.~~ Done 2026-10-03 (`io::farm_site`, `scripts/fiskeridir_site.sh <site nr>`).
    - Sources:
      - The register (pub-aqua API, `sites?nr=`) has the site's position, capacity and species.
      - The NYTEK installation certificate (Fiskeridirektoratet's `NYTEK_FEATURE_API_OGC` feature service: layer 8 the boundary polygon, layer 7 the mooring lines) has the frame's corners and the mooring lines to the anchors. Fiskeridirektoratet does not quality-control NYTEK's coordinates.
      - Neither has the cages. `FarmSite::cage_layout` puts one cage per frame cell of about `spacing` m (bilinear in a four-cornered frame, the bounding rectangle along the longest edge otherwise), unless the file has `cage lon lat [radius net_depth]` lines.
    - Kattholmen (14042, 7800 t, about 600 m east of the Mausund gauge, NYTEK certificate 2022-12-15 by Åkerblå): a 180 × 365 m frame, so 2 × 4 cages ≈ 91 m apart at 90 m spacing.
    - [ ] The cage count and size are guessed from the frame. Check them against an aerial photo, or ask the operator, before trusting a site's numbers. A `totalelements` field exists in NYTEK's polygon layer but is empty for Kattholmen.
    - [ ] Only the frame geometry: the feed raft (`mooring … raft`) and the mooring lines are read but not modelled (drag on the raft and the lines is small next to the nets).
  - [ ] Farm runs need a spun-up tide and horizontal viscosity (found with `viz/`, 2026-09-27/28). In the fjord-farm scenario the fastest water was a jet through the cages. Drag minus no-drag at the west cage centre, 50 model minutes, P2, `examples/local_time_stepping_farm.rs` setup:
    - Abrupt start, inviscid (the example as it is): the tide switched on at full amplitude from rest sends a ≈ 110–130 mm/s surge past the farm at t ≈ 5–10 min. The drag opposes it (−5.5 mm/s at 10 min), and the deficit then *stays*: −6.9 mm/s at 50 min and −6.7 at 2 h, while the tidal current there is 2–5 mm/s (the fjord is short and closed, so the tide is nearly standing). Local and global stepping agree to 0.01 mm/s, so the scheme is not at fault.
    - The persistence comes from the model, not the ocean: without horizontal viscosity nothing removes a 50–100 m eddy but bottom friction (≈ 1e-7 /s at 90 m) and weak upwind dissipation (the largest anomaly within 300 m: 9.0 → 7.3 mm/s over 2 h). With ν = 1 m²/s (`with_viscosity`, global stepping) it decays with e-folding ≈ 1000 s ≈ L²/ν: −3.5 → −1.5 → −1.1 mm/s at 10/20/30 min (largest 4.5 → 1.9 → 1.3), and the background flow is unchanged (no-drag velocities equal to 0.01 mm/s).
    - With a 1 h ramp (`HarmonicTide::with_ramp_up`) there is no surge: flood peaks at 19 mm/s at the farm and the drag takes 0.35–0.65 mm/s (3–4 %) off it. Still inviscid, that deficit then does not decay after the flow reverses (−0.67 mm/s at 2 h).
    - Done 2026-09-28: the tide ramps up over 1 h in `examples/local_time_stepping_farm.rs` (`ramp=`) and in the viewer (`--ramp`). At 15 min, local stepping still does 2.60× less RHS work, and local and global agree to 1.6e-6 m/s.
    - To do: run farm cases with viscosity (affordable under LTS since 2026-09-28: `local_time_stepping_farm nu=1`, 2.1× the inviscid LTS cost), and choose ν (constant 1–10 m²/s or Smagorinsky with a background: at these shears Smagorinsky alone gives ν ≈ (0.1·20 m)²·|S| ≈ 5e-4 m²/s, i.e. nothing; the combination is `HorizontalViscosity2D::smagorinsky(cs).with_background(nu)` since 2026-10-02, P2.5). A farm-wake study also wants a site with real tidal currents.

### F.2 Lagrangian particle tracking
- [x] Point location: find the element holding a point and its reference coordinates (2026-09-27, P3.1: `mesh::PointLocator2D`, a bucket grid over element bounding boxes and Newton on the bilinear map). Curved (isoparametric) elements, once P1.3 has them, need the same Newton on the higher-order map.
- [x] 2D tracking (2026-09-28, `src/particles/`): `ParticleTracker2D` moves `Particle2D`s through a `ParticleVelocity2D`, sampled as the element polynomial at the particle (`interpolation_weights_into` on the stack, no allocation).
  - [x] Velocity: `SWEVelocity2D` is (hu, hv)/h of a solution, steady or linear in time between two snapshots (output files, or online: the previous and current state a `Simulation` callback hands over); `NodalVelocity2D` is any nodal field.
  - [x] Locating: every move walks the straight segment face by face from the particle's element (`particles::walk`), O(faces crossed), no bucket search after release.
  - [x] RK4 advection; horizontal random walk √(2KΔt)ξ with constant K. Each particle has its own SplitMix64 stream (tracker seed, particle id), so a run does not depend on thread count or particle order. Particles are stepped in parallel with `parallel`.
  - [x] Boundaries: walls reflect specularly (`with_reflecting` picks the tags; untagged faces reflect), other faces let the particle out at the crossing (`ParticleStatus::Exited(tag)`), periodic faces carry it across. Particles in water shallower than `with_stranding_depth` strand and float again when it deepens.
  - [x] Gates (`tests/particle_tracking_test.rs`):
    - Solid-body rotation on distorted quads (exact in Q1): one revolution returns to 7e-7 at 100 steps, rate 4.00.
    - A walk of 0.45 per step (several elements) with a flow into a corner never leaves the basin.
    - Well-mixed: a uniform distribution stays uniform in a closed basin (χ² over 64 bins 72 and 57 for steps below and above the element size); in open water the variance is 2Kt to 5 %.
    - Open exit at the crossing point, periodic wrap, stranding and refloating, reproducibility per particle.
    - Online in a running `Simulation` (uniform oscillating flow): second order in the snapshot interval (rate 2.00).
  - [x] `local_time_stepping_farm particles=N [particle_seconds=60] [kh=0.1]` releases N particles in each cage and tracks them online. 1 h from rest with 500 per cage (4 threads): the tracking costs 0.08 s against 198 s for the solver. The clouds drift 42 m with the flood and spread 38 m RMS, which is the random walk's √(4Kt) (the tidal current at the farm is mm/s, so the site says little about advection; a farm-wake study needs a site with real currents, see F.1).
- [ ] Follow-ups:
  - ~~Variable K (drift ∇K)~~ done 2026-10-03 (`particles::diffusivity`, below). Still open: for a depth-integrated tracer (∂(hc)/∂t = ∇·(hK∇c)) or a σ-layer's (`H_z`-weighted), the drift K∇h/h (Dimou & Adams 1993). The walk is right for a surface or depth-uniform concentration in water of uniform depth only, which `farm_3d`'s flat channel is and a real site is not.
  - Curved (isoparametric) elements, once P1.3 has them: the walk assumes straight faces, and `inverse_bilinear` the bilinear map.
  - ~~Particle output (positions per snapshot) for the viewer (F.3)~~ done 2026-10-03 (`io::ParticleFileWriter`, see F.3); ~~connectivity statistics between farms~~ done 2026-10-03 (online, below).
  - A higher-order time interpolation between snapshots (cubic, as for the nesting fields in P1.5) when the output interval is long against the tidal period.
- [x] Pen-to-pen connectivity statistics (2026-10-03, `particles::connectivity`). Contact zones (`ContactZone`: a circular footprint over a depth range). `ConnectivityRecorder` adds up each particle's weighted time in each zone after every tracking step (rectangle rule, `|ΔE| ≤ Δt` per passage). It counts a particle's own zone only after the particle has been `with_return_distance` beyond it. `ConnectivityMatrix` gives share, mean exposure and binomial error per (source, receiver) for any set of release groups; `contacts` gives each contact's exposure and age at first contact; `ConnectivityEnsemble` gives the spread across seeds, standard errors and the `Difference` of two ensembles.
  - Gates (`tests/particle_connectivity_test.rs`):
    - In a uniform current, every particle's exposure equals its chord time through the next cage to the step (3.92 s at Δt = 4 s, 0.95 s at 1 s).
    - A random walk's mean occupation time of a disc around its release matches the exact `Σ Δt (1 − e^{−a²/4Kt_n})` (191.7 ± 1.6 s against 192.5 s).
    - Independent particles: the spread across 40 seeds is the binomial error (0.027 against 0.028). The same layout with other seeds differs by z = 1.0, a receiver 15 m further away by z = 21.6.
  - `farm_3d particles=N layout=across|along releases= release_every= seeds=R contact_depth= connectivity=<csv> runs=cages`: larvae in R seeds (extra seeds track the larvae alone). For each batch it reports the release time, M2 phase and current at the farm, then per pair the share (mean ± spread, binomial), exposure per larva, the 10/50/90 % exposure among the larvae in contact, and the median age at first contact. The CSV has every seed's matrix per batch. The 9 h runs below use 200 per cage, 4 hourly batches from 2 h and 8 seeds, ≈ 650 s wall each, of which 10 s is tracking.
    - Across the flow (120 m apart): 4.3 ± 0.7 % from cage 0 to cage 1 and 2.8 ± 0.6 % back, all on the ebb, after 3–6 h. Each cage's own larvae return at 39 / 38 %: 28 % for the batch released at 2 h (back after 6.5 h), 59 % for the one near slack (back after 68 min). The channel's tide is reversible with no residual current, so the ebb brings the cloud back over the farm; a real site needs its residual current (item 3 of the plan).
    - Along the flow: 88.0 ± 0.8 % from cage 0 to cage 1 on the flood (first contact at 7 min, median exposure 225 s) and 36.4 ± 1.1 % back on the ebb. Released near slack, the larvae linger: 896 s of exposure per larva from 0 to 1.
    - The two layouts' own-cage return from cage 0 differs by 3.2 ± 0.5 % (39.2 against 36.0 %, z ≈ 6), so a few-percent layout effect is measurable with 8 seeds of 800 larvae per cage.
    - Edge flicker: without a return distance, the walk stepping out of a cage and back gave a spurious 2–3 % own-cage contact at 1 min (one step of exposure). `farm_3d` uses a cage radius.
  - [ ] The spread across seeds is about half the binomial error (0.9 against 1.7 % over all batches). The sunflower release points are the same in every seed, so the spread measures the walk's noise in one flow and one release. That is the right yardstick for comparing layouts or `kh` in the same flow; it is not the uncertainty of the release. Draw the release points per seed (and per batch) for that.
  - [ ] `farm_3d`'s periodic channel is 6 km long. The peak-to-peak tidal excursion `2U/ω` = 7.1 km, so larvae released at slack can meet the farm's periodic image. In the 4.5 h run the larvae's mean drift was 2.2 km (RMS spread 0.8 km) at 2.5 h after release, still on the flood. The 9 h runs' largest excursion was not logged: log the larvae's largest displacement before running longer or releasing at slack.
  - [ ] Zone lookup is O(particles × zones) per step (`ConnectivityRecorder::record` tests every footprint). That is fine for one site; for region-wide farm registers (hundreds of sites) use a bucket grid over the zones' boxes, as `PointLocator2D` does for elements.
  - [ ] Recording is serial after each parallel step (`FarmParticles::steps`). Records are independent per particle, so it parallelises the same way when 10⁵+ particles make it count. It was not measurable here: 10 s of tracking for 4 × 9600 larvae-seeds plus 9600 faeces and feed over 9 h.
  - [ ] Exposure is first order in the step. The exact passage time within a step (segment–circle intersection, linear in the step) would make it second order, if `UΔt` cannot be kept small against the cage.
  - [ ] Only circular footprints. Site polygons (Fiskeridirektoratet's register, F.1) and a whole-farm zone around a cage group would need polygon zones (a point-in-polygon test behind `ContactZone::covers`).
  - [ ] The matrix splits by release group only. Contact time split by the tidal phase at contact (not release) needs the contacts' times, which `Contact` keeps only as `first`. Add per-phase exposure bins if that question comes up.
- [x] Sensitivity of the connectivity to the horizontal diffusivity (2026-10-03). `farm_3d kh=0.1,0.01,0.03,0.3,1` tracks the larvae in every `K` in one run, the flow being the same (8 seeds each; 45 s of tracking in a 650 s run; the CSV gains a `kh` column). Shares over all 4 batches, mean ± standard error of 8 seeds:

  | K (m²/s) | across 0→0 | across 0→1 | along 0→1 (flood) | along 1→0 (ebb) |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | 0.01 | 73.3 ± 0.7 % | 0.0 % | 94.8 ± 0.3 % | 72.5 ± 0.3 % |
  | 0.03 | 57.9 ± 0.4 % | 0.3 ± 0.1 % | 93.1 ± 0.3 % | 54.9 ± 0.4 % |
  | 0.1 | 39.2 ± 0.4 % | 4.3 ± 0.3 % | 88.0 ± 0.3 % | 36.4 ± 0.4 % |
  | 0.3 | 26.2 ± 0.5 % | 9.5 ± 0.5 % | 79.8 ± 0.4 % | 23.5 ± 0.4 % |
  | 1 | 19.0 ± 0.5 % | 11.9 ± 0.4 % | 67.8 ± 0.6 % | 16.0 ± 0.5 % |

  - Over 0.01–1 m²/s, `K` moves every share by far more than the noise (z 9–73 against `K` = 0.1). For the return on the ebb and the cross-flow neighbour it moves them more than the layout does: the cross-flow share goes from none to 12 %, and the return to the own cage from 73 to 19 %. The connectivity at the scale of the pens is set by `K`, so `K` needs a physical basis and a site's own measurements (drifter or dye spreading) before the numbers mean much.
  - For scale: Okubo's (1971) diagram gives `K ≈ 0.0103 ℓ^1.15` cm²/s, 0.01 m²/s at ℓ = 30 m and 0.04 m²/s at 100 m. The default 0.1 is 3–10× that at the cages' scale. The model's own `nu=1` background is a numerical choice and 10–100× it.
- [x] A horizontal diffusivity that varies with the flow (2026-10-03, `particles::diffusivity`). `HorizontalDiffusivity` builds a `HorizontalDiffusivityField` from any nodal `K` (one level or σ-levels). Nodes that coincide across faces, periodic ones included (union-find over the face-node pairs), take their mean, so `K` is continuous and `∇K` is the element polynomial's gradient. `smagorinsky_2d`/`smagorinsky_3d` give `ν/Pr_t` with `ν = ν₀ + (C_s Δ)²|S|` from the depth-mean or each layer's strain. `ParticleVelocity2D`/`3D::horizontal_diffusivity` is the hook (forwarded by the Stokes wrappers). `WithHorizontalDiffusivity2D`/`3D` add a field to any flow, steady or linear in time between two (`DiffusivityInTime`). The trackers step `x += ∇K Δt + √(2(K_tracker + K_field)Δt) ξ` (Euler–Maruyama at the step's start).
  - Gates: `tests/particle_diffusivity_test.rs`. Visser's well-mixed test in 2D with `K` varying tenfold on distorted P2 quads: χ² over 64 bins 74.9 with the drift, 9540 without. In 3D the field's `K` at σ = −0.5 between two levels plus the tracker's constant spreads at `2Kt` to 1.3 %. Unit tests: continuity across faces, an exact gradient for a field in the elements' space, Smagorinsky's `K` of a linear shear.
  - `farm_3d kh=smag` (Smagorinsky's `K` of each layer's strain, the model's `cs`, `Pr_t` = 1, without the numerical `nu`; `smag+0.01` adds a constant). Each callback builds the field from the state, linear in time between two. The report gives `K_h` up-current, in the lee and over the top layer.
    - At `dx=60` P2 (Δ = 30 m, `cs` 0.2), at the end of the 9 h run across the flow, Smagorinsky's `K_h` over the top layer is 0.011 / 0.029 / 0.18 m²/s (50 / 90 / 100 %), and 0.005–0.008 100 m behind cage 0. That is Okubo's value for the scale, and an order below the default 0.1.
    - Across the flow, over all batches (z against `K` = 0.1): `smag` returns 82.5 ± 0.6 % of cage 0's larvae to it (z +62) and sends none to the other cage; `smag+0.01` 69.3 %, none; `smag+0.03` 56.8 %, 0.5 %. `smag` keeps more at home than a constant 0.01 (73.3 %) although its median is 0.011. Why is not established: the report samples `K` at two points at the end of the run only. Log `K` along the larvae's paths (mean `K` seen per larva) to tell.
    - Along the flow: `K_h` is 0.15 m²/s 100 m east of cage 0 (between the cages, in the wake's strain) at the end of the run, 0.0067 / 0.027 / 0.18 over the top layer. `smag` sends 92.8 ± 0.2 % from cage 0 to cage 1 and returns 80.0 ± 0.4 % from 1 to 0 on the ebb (88.0 / 36.4 % at 0.1); `smag+0.01` 92.1 / 66.3 %, `smag+0.03` 90.8 / 53.1 %.
    - So with the model's own closure the pens are more tightly connected to themselves and to their neighbours along the tide than with the default 0.1, and not at all across it. The 0.01 m²/s background is what most moves the result (82.5 → 69.3 % return across), and nothing on this mesh constrains it.
  - [ ] Euler–Maruyama with `K` at the step's start is weak order 1. Where `K` varies over a walk step (`√(2KΔt)` against `K/|∇K|`, e.g. at the edge of a wake), Visser's half-drift-step evaluation or the backward Itô scheme (Spivakovskaya et al. 2007) is better. The 2D gate passes at Δt = 0.5 s; measure the error against Δt before using large steps.
  - [ ] Smagorinsky's `K` follows the model's resolved strain. It is a closure for the unresolved scales, so it falls as the mesh refines (`Δ²`) while the resolved eddies take over the spreading. Check that connectivity converges with mesh refinement at the pens (plan item "smaller items").
- [x] 3D tracking (2026-10-01, `particles::ParticleTracker3D`, `Solution3DVelocity`): a σ-level per particle, RK4 on (x, y, σ) with `dσ/dt = Ω/D + w_p/D` (the 2D tracker's walk, walls, exits and stranding horizontally); `u, v` linear in σ between the layer centres, Ω from `Solution3D::w` with Ω = 0 at the surface and bed; snapshots linear in time.
  - [x] Vertical random walk with the ∂K/∂z drift correction (Visser 1997, MEPS 158) on the model's `eddy_diffusivity` at the w-points (`with_vertical_random_walk`). Gates (`tests/particle_tracking_3d_test.rs`): Visser's well-mixed test, χ² over 20 bins 7.3 with the drift against 417 without (analytic K, `K' ≠ 0` at the surface), and 5.6 against 5435 on a `Solution3D` holding a log-layer K at 20 w-points (piecewise linear, `K'` jumping at every w-point); a sheared flow with a σ-velocity followed exactly; sinking particles reach the bed at `D/w_s` and settle (`with_bed_settling`, `ParticleStatus::Settled`) or reflect.
  - [x] Sinking speed (feed, faeces): a constant vertical speed per particle (`release(.., vertical_speed)`).
  - [x] Lice larvae depth preference and salinity avoidance (2026-10-01, `particles::behaviour`): `ParticleBehaviour3D` gives each particle a swimming speed per step from what it senses (`Surroundings`: depth, T and S through the new `ParticleVelocity3D::tracers`, sampled from `Solution3D` like `u`), accrues development and kills it (`ParticleStatus::Dead`); `ParticleTracker3D::step_with`. `SalmonLice` is the IMR lice model's larva: up at 0.5 mm/s where the light `E₀e^{−0.2ζ}` reaches its stage's threshold, down where S is below its threshold (wins over light), nauplius → copepodid → dead in degree-days, survival weight `e^{−0.17 t/day}`. Presets `ladim()` (the operational LADiM `salmon_lice` IBM: light 0.01, S < U[30, 32] nauplii / U[20, 28] copepodids, 40/170 degree-days, T ≥ 5 °C) and `johnsen_2014()` (Johnsen et al. 2014, AEI 5: light 0.39 / 2.06e-5 µmol m⁻² s⁻¹, S < 20, 50/150 degree-days). `ClearSkyLight`: Skartveit & Olseth's clear sky at a site, as LADiM computes it (equal to LADiM's code to 1e-9 at whole hours).
    - Gates (`tests/particle_tracking_3d_test.rs`): under constant K, light-seeking larvae reach `c ∝ e^{−wζ/K}` above the threshold depth and uniform below (χ² 23.4 over 20 bins with 20 000 particles; 64 and 56 with the swim 5 % off), and a halocline traps them in `c ∝ e^{−w|ζ−ζ_h|/K}` (χ² 11.4); development and death to the step from a two-temperature `Solution3D`.
    - [ ] The swim is chosen at the start of each step (as LADiM), so a cue's jump is smeared over a walk step `√(2KΔt)`: the light gate's χ² is 13 / 23 / 41 at Δt = 5 / 10 / 20 s with K = 5e-3 m²/s (weak order ½ for a discontinuous drift). Keep `√(2KΔt)` well below the profile scale `K/w_s` near haloclines, or locate the crossing within the step.
    - [ ] Infectivity: LADiM weights copepodids by Skern-Mauritzen et al.'s (2020) infectivity(age, T) for infection-pressure maps; add it next to `SalmonLice::survival`. The connectivity recorder takes it as the contact weight (`ConnectivityRecorder::record(.., weight)`; `farm_3d` passes 1).
    - [ ] Light: clear sky at one site only. Use the weather readers' shortwave or cloud cover (`AtmosphereReader`) and an attenuation tied to salinity / CDOM (Aksnes et al. 2009) for fjords; a per-position sun through the mesh projection for domains wider than ≈ 1° of longitude.
    - [ ] A behaviour sees the development, not the particle, so a mixed population steps one slice per species (`farm_3d` keeps one `Vec` per kind). Pass `&Particle3D` (id, vertical speed) if per-particle traits are needed.
  - [ ] Ω at the layer centres is an average of the w-point values (`Solution3D::w`, output only); the tracker interpolates it with Ω = 0 at the ends. Keeping Ω at the w-points in the state (as the turbulence and now `eddy_diffusivity` are) would make `dσ/dt` exact to the model's continuity.
  - [ ] Stranding in 3D uses the column depth only; resuspension of settled particles (bed stress above a critical value) is not modelled.
  - [ ] Sampling cost: `Solution3DVelocity::diffusivity` runs the whole `blend` (u, v, Ω, depth and K of both snapshots) to return K, and the Visser walk calls it twice per step on top of the four RK stages' `velocity` calls (each also sampling K it discards). Split `Sample` by what the caller needs, and bracket σ once per call; measure on `farm_3d particles=N` first (tracking 1200 particles costs 0.6 s of a 163 s 3D run, so it matters only for 10⁵+ particles).

### F.3 3D visualisation (`viz/`, Bevy 0.20)
- [x] Crate `dg-viz` in `viz/` (its own workspace, so dg-rs builds and tests never compile Bevy; dg-rs without NetCDF, so no native HDF5). Runs the fjord-farm scenario of `examples/local_time_stepping_farm.rs` on a solver thread (`run_with_callback`), plays the snapshots back (rate, pause, seek; oldest dropped beyond a memory budget) as the water surface over the bed, each element as p² quads of its own nodes with the polynomial's exact normals, coloured by speed or η; walls along the mesh boundary; current arrows sampled by the element polynomial (`Probe2D` weights) on a domain grid and a fine farm grid; net cages (collar, textured net) riding η; orbit camera; `--screenshot` for headless checks (2026-09-27).
- [x] A start menu (2026-10-07, `viz/src/menu.rs`): `cargo run --release` without options opens it. It lists the live scenarios (missing data files noted; four since the fjord in 3D) and the saved runs under `output/` (snapshot files and folders of VTU frames, three folders down, newest first, with size, date and whether a VTU folder has its `run.log`), a selected snapshot's title, span, frame interval and domain read from its header, and the options model hours, start view, photo view, the Mausund gauge and saving as a snapshot file (`output/viz/<scenario>-<time>.dgsnap` for a live run, `froya.dgsnap` beside VTU frames). Enter or a double-click starts the viewer as a child process with those options (the equivalent `cargo run` line is printed); M in the viewer comes back, a closed viewer window closes the menu, a failed viewer shows its last error lines. A `.dgsnap` file or a VTU folder dropped on the window is replayed. Gates: the command for each kind of entry and option, the saved-run scan, the save path; `--menu --screenshot PATH` for headless checks.
  - [x] **The fjord farm in 3D** (asked for 2026-10-07; done 2026-10-07): `--scenario fjord3d`, and "Fjord farm in 3D" in the menu (`Scenario::fjord_farm_3d`, `viz/src/scenario.rs`).
    - The tide: M2 0.8 m through the open boundary as in 2D, into the barotropic mode (`spawn_3d` now takes the 2D scenarios' open boundary, `solver::open_boundary`; Frøya's atlas tides would work the same way). T and S (and the shear) relax to the stratification at rest in a 2 km band along the open face, 30 min on it (`Nesting3D` over `ReferenceColumns`).
    - The stratification (`scenario::fjord_profile`): S 25 at the surface over S 33 sill water, the step at 5 m and 3 m thick, and a summer thermocline, 14 °C over 8 °C at 12 m; salinity sets most of the density step (6 against 1 kg/m³). Surface-stretched σ-levels (Song–Haidvogel 5, 0.4, 10 m, as Frøya's 3D tide). LADiM larvae dive out of the brackish layer.
    - Steep walls: the analytic fjord bed has none inside the domain (r_x0 ≲ 0.05 at 40 m, no flats: 20 m at the shallowest), so `smooth_element_slopes` (pycnocline bound, 20 m free depth) changes nothing there and is a guard for other `--mesh`es; no wetting and drying. The vertical reference is `Simulation3D`'s default; the PGF and the tracer limiter (horizontal Kuzmin, as Frøya's) take the profile as their reference.
    - The rest state (no tide, the open boundary and band in place): with the limiter's reference linear between samples it drifted (4e-5 m/s within 10 min); with `TracerReferenceProfile::from_smooth_fn` (new, P4.6) 3.6e-10 m/s after 1 h (gate `the_stratified_fjord_stays_at_rest`, constant mixing without diffusion; `the_tide_enters_the_3d_fjord` for the tide). With GLS, or a constant 1e-6 m²/s (GLS's background), the halocline diffuses over the slope and drives 3.6e-5 m/s within the hour, the same to three digits, so physics, not GLS numerics: next item.
    - Cost: one global step, set in the farm's quads by the internal-wave bound `(|u| + c₁)/h`, against 8 levels of local time stepping in 2D. Model time per wall time over 15 min of tide (24 threads): the 2D mesh (20 m at the farm, 3584 elements) 9× at P1 × 8 levels, 2.2× at P2 × 12; 40 m (2016) 28× / 7×; 60 m (1488) 48× / 13×. So the 3D fjord runs on its own mesh, `tests/data/gmsh/fjord_farm_3d.msh` (`scripts/gmsh_farm_mesh.py farm=40`), at P1 on 12 levels by default (`--order`, `--sigma` override it).
    - [ ] The diffusion-driven flow at rest under GLS (3.6e-5 m/s at 1 h, still growing). A no-flux bed tilts the isopycnals where it slopes (Phillips 1970, Wunsch 1970), so some flow is real; but σ-levels of different thickness also diffuse the same profile with different truncation errors. Separate them: halve the levels' spacing and κ in turn, and compare with Phillips's boundary-layer velocity.
    - [ ] Local time stepping in 3D (the mode splitter's step for the whole mesh): the farm's 40 m quads set one step for the fjord's 450 m ones. Even two levels would let the fjord run P2 at the farm.
    - [ ] The cages at P1 on 40 m quads: two nodes across a 50 m cage. Compare the wake with the 2D run's at 20 m (or the 3D one at P2), and with the drag of `CageDrag2D` integrated over the footprint.
  - [ ] The menu starts a new process per run because `main` builds the scenario, terrain and resources before `App::run`. Building them on entering a run state (`OnEnter`, the plugins' `Startup` systems moved there, despawned on leaving) would switch runs in one window and keep the terrain between replays of one domain.
  - [ ] More of the options in the menu: threads, order, local time stepping levels, particles, sites (`data/sites/*.txt`) and named places, the terrain patches.
  - [ ] `froya_real_data` writes no clock file, so its VTU folders replay without a date (and without the gauge) unless the run's output was redirected to `run.log`. Write the `Clock:` line to `<output>/run.log` (or a small `clock.txt` the viewer also reads) at the start of a run.
  - [ ] Every viewer start logs a WGSL error from Bevy's `pbr_fragment` (`cannot find declaration of uv`, also on `main`); the frames render, but find which material's shader defs cause it (the photo view's water material, `viz/src/shaders/water.wesl`, is the likely one) before the Bevy 0.20 release.
- [ ] Salmon in the cages (pfish-bevy in `../blender-procedural-fish-addon/rust`, once the add-on has a salmon).
- [x] Particles (2026-09-28, `viz/src/particles.rs`): released continuously from the cages (`--particles N` per cage every `--release S`, random walk `--kh`), tracked on the solver thread between the callback snapshots, drawn as small octahedra riding η, coloured by age (grey when stranded); P toggles them, the HUD counts them. At the fjord farm the cloud is mostly random walk (currents < 1 cm/s).
  - [x] Particle types with their own behaviour (2026-10-01, the 3D channel below): lice larvae swimming by `SalmonLice` (`--lice ladim|johnsen|passive`, daylight at Mausund from `--start`), faeces sinking at 3 cm/s, feed at 10 cm/s, tracked in 3D on the solver thread (`viz/src/cloud_3d.rs`, Visser's walk in the model's K, bed settling) and drawn at their depth, coloured by kind (dark on the bed, grey dead).
  - [ ] Trails; a density field instead of points for 10⁵+ particles. The 3D channel releases 20 of each kind per cage per minute by default, 2400 an hour; each snapshot carries all of them (17 B each).
  - [x] ~~Snapshot sizes grow with the particles, but the budget was sized by the newest snapshot~~. Done 2026-10-05: `Playback::keep` counts the bytes held and drops the oldest beyond `--memory`, keeping two. Gate: `the_budget_counts_the_bytes_held`.
- [x] Bathymetry through the water (2026-09-28): the water is 40 % opaque by default (`--water-alpha`, - and = step it, T still cycles translucent/opaque/hidden), the HUD has a bed-depth colour scale, and the bed carries depth contours (`viz/src/contours.rs`: marching triangles over the bed's own triangles, a round interval of about ten lines, every fifth bright, drawn as ribbons a few pixels wide that follow the camera distance; B toggles them).
- [x] Frøya in the viewer (2026-09-28): `--scenario froya` runs Frøya–Smøla–Hitra as `froya_real_data mesh=data/froya_coast.msh` does (the topobathy bed projected onto the coastline mesh, NorKyst-800 atlas tides from 2025-06-15, open faces beyond the atlas walled off, a 3 h ramp); F frames the Mausund gauge. ≈ 18× faster than real time at 10 solver threads, so play it back at `--rate 15`.
  - [x] ~~Land: the islands are holes with walls down to the base~~. Done 2026-10-05 (`viz/src/terrain.rs`): `--terrain FILE|none` draws Kartverket's topobathy model (default `data/froya_topobathy.tif`) around and under a georeferenced domain: land coloured by height, the sea bed beyond the model on the model bed's depth scale under a still translucent sea at 0, L toggles it. The terrain's cells stop at the mesh boundary (inside corners of the cells across it snap onto it), and the model's bed rises in a wall along the boundary to the land there. Scenarios carry their projection (`Scenario::projection`); snapshot files carry it as `projection=local,lat,lon` metadata (`froya_real_data`, the viewer's `--save-snapshot`), and `--origin LAT,LON` places older files (`froya_real_data`'s default `bbox=` centre is 63.8,8.6). Frøya: 1183 × 892 vertices at 50 m, built in 0.1 s.
    - [x] ~~Farm scale: the 50 m model's land is coarse at a farm close-up~~. Done 2026-10-05: `--terrain coarse.tif,fine.tif` layers finer patches over the base, each its own grid aligned to the base's lines (the base's cells cut out under it, its border on the base's edges, so no cracks). A patch's land or surveyed depth replaces the coarser models, and its exact 0 (flat sea) takes the coarser depth, capped at 0. `scripts/kartverket_topobathy.sh` gives the 1 m level for cells ≤ 40 m (with a warning; 40–50 m refused); `data/kattholmen_topobathy_2m.tif` is 3 × 3 km around the farm at 2 m (1520 × 1503, one request): 17 % lidar land up to 41.6 m, 83 % flat 0, 0.04 % surveyed (all shallower than 0.75 m). Drawn at 2.2 m (1381 × 1358) beside the base's 50 m, 0.2 s together.
      - It needed a GeoTIFF fix: the service writes all-flat-sea tiles sparse (64 of 144 tiles, offset and byte count 0), which the `tiff` crate (0.9) decoded as garbage up to 10³⁵. `GeoTiffBathymetry::load` reads sparse chunks as the no-data value or 0 (Fixed in CHANGELOG).
      - Land the mesh does not resolve is drawn inside it too (where the elevation model stands 0.5 m above both 0 and the model's bed): the Frøya mesh floods every skerry around Kattholmen. Its foot is drawn down to 2 m below 0, then the model's bed is the sea floor. This hides the model's water over such land; it is a map of the coast, not of the model.
      - [ ] Several patches must not overlap (each is cut out of the base only). Cut each out of the coarser patches too if two farms' patches meet.
    - [x] ~~The farm site in the map~~. Done 2026-10-05 (`viz/src/site.rs`, `--site FILE`, repeatable): the frame as floating pipes along its sides and between its cells (`FarmSite::frame_cells`, new in `io::farm_site`), the mooring lines down to their anchors on the model's bed, a buoy where each leaves the frame, the feed raft, all riding η at the site's centre; the cages (`FarmSite::cage_layout`, 90 m cells, radius 25.5 m, 20 m nets; Kattholmen: 2 × 4) join the scenario's, so a live run drags on them and `--save-snapshot` keeps them. The close-up (F) frames the first site; the gauge trace stays at the scenario's point of interest.
      - [ ] Only the frame and its cells are drawn; the site's name is in the title only. A label in the world would help with several sites (the font has the Norwegian letters now).
    - [x] ~~The coast wall shows the coastline mesh's shoreline bed~~ (teeth under every island). Answered 2026-10-05: mostly the elevation model's own coast along a simplified coastline, not the projection (P1.6, "The bed at the coastline mesh's walls"). The part that mattered numerically, land in front of the walls of elements that are otherwise water, is `wall_land=lower`; the teeth stay where the coast really is land or cliff.
    - [x] ~~The sea outside the model is flat at 0 and meets the model's water at η~~. Done 2026-10-05: its vertices snapped onto the mesh boundary follow the model's η there (0 where dry), so the two meet within a cell instead of a step. Where the elevation model ends at the mesh boundary (Frøya's covers just the domain) there is nothing outside; there the domain keeps its walls (`Terrain::covers`), else the water sheet's edge hung above the bed as a band.
    - [ ] Snapping is brute force (each corner of the cells across the boundary against every boundary segment): 0.2 s at Frøya with 7k segments, the 2 m Kattholmen patch included. A mesh of 10⁵ boundary faces wants the segments in a bucket grid, as `PointLocator2D` has.
  - [x] ~~Bevy's default font has no ø, æ, å~~. Done 2026-10-05: the viewer bundles Fira Mono whole (`viz/assets/fonts`, SIL OFL 1.1, 175 KB) in place of Bevy's subset of it, so every text keeps its look. Names are no longer spelt in ASCII (`scenario::ascii` is gone), and the keys show ← → and ×.
- [x] Visualise the 15-day Frøya harmonic run (2026-10-03, P3.1: `output/froya_15d_k1o1/`, not tracked) in the viewer: replay instead of re-running (4 h of solver time). Done 2026-10-03 (`viz/src/replay.rs`): `cd viz && cargo run --release -- --replay ../output/froya_15d_k1o1`.
  - What it does: the frames go into the playback as the solver's snapshots would. Each frame has, per element, the 9 P2 GLL nodes in the solver's order, so no new numbering is needed. The first frame's nodes and bed are checked against the scenario built as the run was (both matched to 1 mm). The frames are parsed in parallel, and the field is linear in time between them; the HUD says so and shows the UTC date from `run.log`.
  - Cost: parsing all 6.5 GB of ASCII takes 5 s from a warm file cache (385 frames, 503 MB as snapshots), with the first frame shown at once. So the binary converter was not needed for this run.
  - [x] The spring–neap cycle at Mausund as a HUD trace (2026-10-03, `viz/src/trace.rs`, G toggles it). It plots the model's η at the close-up point, sampled from every snapshot by the element polynomial as frames arrive (live runs too), with the observations from `--gauge ../data/tide_gauges/mausund_obs.txt` and a marker at the shown time. A gauge in an element that is not fully ≥ 3 m deep is sampled at the nearest node of one that is, as `froya_real_data` does: for Mausund 198 m away in 5.3 m, the run's own station point.
    - [x] ~~Bookmarked views~~. Done 2026-10-05: keys 1 to 9 frame named places (`camera::Views::places`), listed above the keys' help. The places are a snapshot file's stations (`station=` metadata; Frøya live: Mausund), the `--site`s (in the farm close-up) and `--place NAME,X_KM,Y_KM`. Stations and places use the scenario's own framing. `--view N` starts at place N. E.g. `--place pinnacle,10.6,-13.8 --view 4` frames the fast flow over the pinnacle. Gate: `a_place_is_named_in_km`.
      - [ ] Still worth a close-up: the tidal flats wetting and drying. Find a flat the coastline mesh resolves and give it a `--place`.
    - [x] ~~The trace's live runs have no clock~~: `Scenario::clock` (Frøya's tides, the channel's `--start`, a snapshot's header) is the `RunClock` of live runs since 2026-10-03, so `--gauge` and the dates work live too.
    - [ ] Hourly frames undersample the trace (≈ 12 points per M2 cycle, peaks clipped by up to ≈ 3 % of the range). A `snapshot_minutes=10` run now replays whole (on demand, next item) and its trace has every frame. Still open: drawing the run's own `station_mausund.txt` (10-min samples) for the hourly VTU runs.
  - [x] ~~Hourly frames are only ≈ 12 per M2 cycle, and 15 days of 10-min frames (2.3k, ≈ 3 GB) do not fit `--memory`~~. Done 2026-10-05: a snapshot file replays on demand (`replay::serve`, `Playback::read_on_demand`).
    - The playback knows every frame's time from the start. It asks the reading thread for the two frames around the shown time and the next one, and holds at most four (`playback::HELD`).
    - Before serving frames, the thread reads the gauge trace's η from every frame, only the probe element's nodes (`SnapshotReader::read_eta_into`, new), and sends it in chunks of 256 frames.
    - The 15-day Frøya file (385 hourly frames, 505 MB): the HUD holds 4 frames (5 MB) where the playback held all of them, and the trace has all 15 days from the start.
    - VTU frames and a snapshot file saved again (`--save-snapshot`) still stream through the budgeted playback. Gates: `a_snapshot_file_is_served_frame_by_frame`, `the_frames_around_the_shown_time_and_the_next_are_wanted`, `an_on_demand_replay_holds_the_frames_nearest_the_shown_time`, and `eta_reads_back_in_part` in the library.
    - [x] ~~Not yet replayed: an actual 10-minute run~~. Done 2026-10-05: `froya_real_data mesh=data/froya_coast.msh lts=8 start=2025-06-14T00:00:00Z hours=48 ramp_hours=3 snapshot_minutes=10` (289 frames, 379 MB, 31 min) replays to 48 h holding 4 frames (5 MB), with the gauge trace at 10 min. The 15-day 10-minute run (≈ 4 h, 2.3k frames) is still to make.
    - [ ] A far seek shows the old frames until the new ones arrive (a few ms from the page cache; a cold disk is slower). If that shows, let `bracket` return nothing while it holds no frame on either side of the shown time.
  - [x] ~~A replay still builds the whole Frøya scenario~~: a snapshot file replay builds nothing (2026-10-03, next item).
- [x] A snapshot file (element-major f32 η, u, v plus the mesh and bed) so runs can be replayed without re-running. Done 2026-10-03: `dg_rs::io::{SnapshotWriter, SnapshotReader}` (`.dgsnap`). The header carries the mesh (vertices, quads, periodic pairs, boundary tags, rebuilt with `Mesh2D::from_quads`), the order, the nodal bed, the clock and `key=value` metadata. Frames are appended and flushed whole, so a reader can follow a run that is still writing.
  - `froya_real_data snapshot_minutes=N` writes `<output>/froya.dgsnap`, with the stations in mesh coordinates. This covers nested runs and runs without `mesh=` too.
  - `dg-viz --replay FILE` builds its scenario from the header alone, with the close-up at the point of interest or the first station. `--replay DIR --save-snapshot FILE` converts a VTU run while playing it: the 15-day run went from 6.5 GB to 505 MB.
  - [x] ~~Follow a run while it writes~~. Done 2026-10-05: an idle reading thread looks for new complete frames every second (`replay::FOLLOW`) and sends their times (`SolverMessage::Grown`); the playback extends its frames, the trace's span and samples grow with them, and the clock waits at the newest frame while the file grew within the last 30 s (`playback::GROWING_FOR`; `--screenshot` waits too). The particle file is read again when frames are added. Frøya with 10-min frames: the viewer started at 23 frames and took its 5 h screenshot at 31, "growing (last frame 1 s ago)", holding 4 frames (5 MB). Gate: `a_snapshot_file_is_served_frame_by_frame` appends two frames while serving.
  - [ ] Other scenarios selectable from the command line (nested runs, the Mausund sub-domain) still need their builders in `viz/` to run live; replaying their snapshot files needs nothing more.
  - [x] ~~The format is 2D SWE only~~. 3D runs since 2026-10-03: format version 2 (version 1 still reads) adds the σ-grid (`SigmaGrid::from_levels`) and named fields on every level to the header; `SnapshotWriter::create_3d`/`write_solution_3d` store η, ū, v̄ and u, v, T, S. `farm_3d snapshot=FILE [snapshot_minutes=10]` writes its run with cages, with the cages, the section and the periods as metadata (1.5 h at 5 min: 43.5 MB). The viewer replays it with layers, section and cages, and `--save-snapshot` now also saves live runs, 2D and 3D, from the solver thread.
    - [x] ~~Particles are not in the format~~: particle files since 2026-10-03 (`io::{ParticleFileWriter, ParticleFileReader, ParticleFrame, status_code}`, `.dgpart` beside the `.dgsnap`). Frames of varying length (position, height, status, kind, release time per particle, in release order), indexed on opening; a partial last frame is ignored. `farm_3d snapshot= particles=N` and the viewer's `--save-snapshot` (live runs with particles, and replays of files that have them) write one, and replays attach each particle frame to the field frame of the same time. The 3D channel at 10 per cage per minute: 2.2 MB of particles over 1.5 h against 81 MB of fields.
      - [ ] Connectivity from these files (offline). The statistics exist online since 2026-10-03 (F.2, `particles::connectivity`). Offline needs two things: each particle's source (the frames carry kind and release time, not the cage) and a finer time than the frames. At 5–10 min a larva crosses a 50 m cage at 0.5 m/s between two frames, so interpolate positions between frames, or record online.
    - [ ] `Simulation3D` calls back at the first step past each interval, so frames drift (300 s asked, 316 s apart in `farm_3d`). `farm_3d` and the viewer now also write the final state, but the spacing stays uneven; fixing the callback timing (P6: "`Simulation3D` still fires callbacks at the first step past each interval") would give even frames.
    - [ ] A live save writes the viewer's f32 snapshot (velocity zeroed below `H_DRY`, no salinity), not the solver state. A 3D section by salinity (or an S colouring) would want S in the viewer's `Layers` too.
- [x] 3D fields (Stage B, 2026-10-01): `--scenario channel` runs the stratified farm channel of `examples/farm_3d.rs` in 3D (`Simulation3D` on the solver thread; 72× real time at P2 × 16 levels on 22 threads, release build). Snapshots carry u, v and T on every σ-level (`solver::Layers`, ≈ 1.7 MB each). `,` and `.` step the current the water, arrows and colouring show through the depth mean and each layer (`field::ShownLayer`, `--layer mean|surface|bed|N`). A vertical section along the flow through a cage (`viz/src/layers.rs`, V cycles it, `--section speed|temperature|off`): 240 columns sampling every layer by the element polynomial, at `η + σ_l D`, coloured by the horizontal speed on its own scale (0.05 m/s steps, so the cage's wake over the net's depth, the faster water underneath and the bottom boundary layer read) or by temperature (the thermocline). The channel's close-up looks along the section from low on the second cage's side.
  - [ ] The section is one fixed line per scenario and opaque: a cross-channel section behind the cages, or a section placed with the mouse; and it hides what is behind it (T hides the water, not the section).
  - [ ] The temperature scale is fitted to the first snapshot; with surface heat fluxes (P4.4) it should follow the field like the speed scale.
  - [x] ~~Only the channel runs in 3D~~: a 3D Frøya run's snapshot file (`froya_real_data levels=N tide3d=1 snapshot_minutes=N`, P1.3) replays with its layers and section (2026-10-05, the Mausund sub-domain). The fjord in 3D runs live since 2026-10-07 (`--scenario fjord3d`).
- [x] A photo view (2026-10-05, `viz/src/photo.rs`, `viz/src/shaders/water.wesl`; R, `--photo on`). It shows wind waves riding the model's current, the sky and the sun's glint reflected, the bed through coastal water, natural land colours, haze, and the real sun of the shown instant. `--eye` stands the camera where a photographer stood. See CHANGELOG for the physics. Checked by screenshots at the fjord farm and at Kattholmen (`--replay output/froya_10min_48h/froya.dgsnap --site data/sites/14042.txt --vz 1 --eye 5.4,8.2,25,245,6`).
  - [ ] The sea is synthetic, from `--wind` and `--fetch`. Drive it from the spectral wave model (F.4) when a run has one: H_s, T_p and mean direction per node, scaling and turning the 32 waves. Take the wind from a gridded atmosphere (`AtmosphereReader`) when the run has one.
  - [ ] The waves ride the depth-mean current. A 3D snapshot has the top σ-layer's u, v (`solver::Layers`), which is faster in a stratified run and is the current the waves feel.
  - [ ] Only the sky is reflected. Land, cages and the farm frame should show in the water near the shore, either by screen-space reflections (Bevy's need the deferred path) or a planar reflection pass at mean sea level. The photo view has no shadows either (`shadow_maps_enabled: false`), and a low sun wants the islands' shadows.
  - [ ] Shallow-water optics: caustics on the bed under a few metres of water (the same wave field, its Laplacian), refraction of the bed through the waves, and surf or swash foam where the model wets and dries.
  - [ ] GPU cost has not been measured. Each sea pixel sums 32 waves × 2 flow-map phases with 3 value noises each (groups and the two phase warps). A 4000 × 2250 screenshot renders fine on this machine, but measure it before larger screens or more waves. If it is too slow, bake the waves into a 2048 m tiled height and slope texture once per frame, and keep only the flow-map lookups per pixel.
  - [ ] The wave groups' clock wraps every hour of wall time (`misc.z`, kept small for f32), so the groups jump once an hour. Make their noise periodic over the wrap.
  - [ ] Not yet tried (the screenshots start in one view or the other): R toggling back and forth, so that the chart view returns with its materials, light, clear colour and HDR off. Add an app-level test (a `MinimalPlugins` app that runs `switch` twice and checks the materials and the light).

### F.4 Surface waves: a spectral wave model (`src/waves/`)
Goal (added 2026-10-03): the sea state at the farms and along the coast, coupled to the circulation and the particles. Lice larvae live in the top metres, where the waves' Stokes drift carries them. Breaking waves mix the surface layer (GLS already takes a breaking-wave flux, P4.4). Waves raise the stress on the bed in shallow water, and they load the cages. The model is phase-averaged (SWAN/WAM-type): the action balance on the DG mesh, one nodal field per (frequency, direction) component.
- [x] Stage 1 (2026-10-03): kinematics, propagation, the basic sources, the integrated quantities.
  - `waves::dispersion`: k from σ and d (Guo's start, Newton to 1e-12), c_g, ∂σ/∂d.
  - `waves::spectrum`: log-spaced frequencies, uniform directions, JONSWAP with cos^m spreading, normalised on the grid. H_s, T_m01, T_m02, T_p (parabolic peak), mean direction, Kuik spread. Stokes drift profile, radiation stress, bed orbital velocity.
  - `waves::sources`: Komen wind input with Cavaleri–Malanotte linear growth, Komen whitecapping (δ = 1), JONSWAP bottom friction (C_b = 0.038), Battjes–Janssen breaking. Integrated over a step with frozen rates (exponential integrator, stable for the stiff surf-zone sinks).
  - `waves::WaveModel2D`: strong-form DG propagation per component at c_g e_θ + U, upwind faces, open faces bringing in a boundary spectrum and every other face absorbing. Refraction by depth and current shear, and frequency shifting by currents, by first-order upwind finite volumes in θ (periodic) and σ. SSP-RK3 with a Zhang–Shu positivity scaling per element, then the sources (Lie splitting). In parallel over components.
  - Gates (`tests/wave_model_test.rs`; unit gates in each module):
    - propagation converges at rate 3.0 at P2, along x and along the diagonal;
    - refraction over a periodic shoal keeps the total action to 3.9e-15;
    - shoaling 20 → 3 m keeps the action flux c_g N to 2e-14;
    - Snell's law on a 20 → 4 m shelf at 30° incidence: off by 2.06 / 1.03 / 0.51° at 10 / 5 / 2.5° bins (first order, the θ scheme);
    - a following current 0 → 0.6 m/s keeps σ + kU = σ₀ to 0.34 %;
    - per node: bottom friction decays at its rate, wind grows a sea from calm downwind only, breaking bounds H_s in shallow water;
    - Stokes drift, radiation stress and orbital velocity match the single-component and shallow limits exactly.
- [x] Stage 2 (2026-10-03): four-wave interactions, the diagnostic tail and a growth limiter; wind seas grow.
  - `waves::nonlinear::Quadruplets`: the DIA (Hasselmann et al. 1985; C_nl4 = 3e7, λ = 0.25, the angles from the deep-water resonance), scaled for depth by SWAN's `R(0.75 k̃ d)` (`shallow_water_factor`). Off-grid frequencies are read and scattered with weights linear in 1/σ, the only linear weights that keep energy and action both exact on the grid. In `SourceTerms::rates` the gains go to the linear input A and the losses to the rate B.
  - Diagnostic tail: `σ^(−4)` (SWAN's power for the Komen terms) above `max(2.5 σ̃, 4 σ_PM)` after every step, and above the grid for the DIA.
  - Ris's limiter (SWAN's; γ = 0.1 of the Phillips level, growth only): without it a grid to 2 Hz blew up (E* ≈ 1e96 within 14 h at 18 s steps); with it the growth curves do not depend on f_max.
  - Gates:
    - unit: the DIA keeps energy and action to 1e-13 (36×36 and 30×24); a JONSWAP's transfer has the three lobes (gain below the peak, loss at 1.3–1.5 f_p, gain at high frequencies); `R(0.5) = 4.43`.
    - `fetch_limited_growth_follows_kahma_and_calkoen` (10 m/s, 120 km of deep water, P1, 25×24, 5 s in the ci profile): f_p* 0.90–1.05× KC92's `2.18 X*^−0.27` over X* = 1.2e3–1.2e4. E* is 1.31× KC92's `5.2e-7 X*^0.9` at 1.2e3, crossing 1.0 at 3e3 and falling to 0.60× at 1.2e4 (E* ∝ X*^0.56). Converged: P2, 120 elements, 36 directions, f_max = 2 Hz or no tail move it ≤ 4.5 % (most at the shortest fetch, X* ≈ 2e3; ≤ 0.6 % beyond 1e4).
    - `duration_limited_growth_approaches_pierson_moskowitz`: from calm under 10 m/s, 0.84× PM energy and f_p* 0.123 at 96 h; dt 10/60/300 s agree to 0.3 %.
  - `examples/wave_growth.rs` prints both curves (`mode=fetch|duration`, `dia=0`, `tail=0`, resolutions).
  - Cost: the DIA is ≈ 10 % of a step in the fetch gate's configuration (2.9 s against 2.6 s without, 120 points × 600 components, release). At Frøya it was 26 % of the step, then 9 % after its tables, now 5 % after vectorising across nodes (Cost below).
- [ ] Wind-sea energy at long fetch and full development: Komen whitecapping (δ = 1, C_ds = 2.36e-5) leaves E* 0.60× KC92 at X* = 1.2e4 (growth ∝ X*^0.56 against 0.9), and under a steady wind it passes Pierson–Moskowitz after ≈ 180 h, reaching 1.5× at 1000 h (`wave_growth mode=duration hours=1000 dt=300`; SWAN warns of this for δ = 1 without retuning C_ds). The f_p curve is right, so the spectrum is too low for its peak. Try van der Westhuysen et al.'s (2007) saturation-based whitecapping with the Yan (1987) input (SWAN `GEN3 WESTH`), or ST6 (Rogers et al. 2012). Gate on the same two tests, then tighten their bounds.
- [ ] The whitecapping means (`Means::of` in `src/waves/sources.rs`) integrate over the grid only; SWAN adds the diagnostic tail beyond `σ_max`. Negligible at f_max = 1 Hz for these seas (no tail and an f⁻⁵ tail agree to 1 %), but it matters for young seas near the grid top.
- [ ] Triads (LTA, Eldeberky 1996) for the surf zone.
- [x] Second-order advection in θ and σ (2026-10-03): MUSCL, the upwind bin reconstructed linearly with van Leer's limiter (`SpectralAdvection::VanLeer`, the default; `Upwind` remains). `compute_dt` halves the spectral Courant limits for it (TVD for ≤ ½).
  - Gates, against exact steady states (`N c_g/k` constant along rays; Snell's `k cos θ` or `ω = σ + k·U` and `k_x`):
    - `a_spread_sea_refracts_to_the_exact_steady_state` (cos⁸ about 20° off the normal on the 20 → 4 m shelf): mean direction off by 0.37 / 0.072 / 0.012° at 10 / 5 / 2.5° bins (rates 2.4, 2.5) against upwind's 1.38 / 0.70 / 0.35° (rates 1.0).
    - `a_spread_sea_on_a_current_has_the_exact_doppler_shift` (Gaussian in σ, cos⁸ in θ, a following current 0 → 0.6 m/s over 500 m): mean σ off by 9.2e-3 / 2.0e-3 / 3.3e-4 (rates 2.2, 2.6) against upwind's 1.8e-2 / 9.0e-3 / 4.5e-3 (rates 1.00), refining σ and θ together.
    - The single-bin gates (Snell, the Doppler spike) stay first order with either scheme: a spike is an extremum, where the limiter falls back to first order, and it leaves its bin through faces whose speed is O(Δθ) off the bin's. MUSCL: 2.13 / 1.04 / 0.52° and 0.53 %; upwind 2.06 / 1.03 / 0.51° and 0.34 %. Swell from a parent model that is one or two bins wide behaves like this; finer bins are the cure there, not the scheme.
  - Cost: within the noise of the fetch gate (3.0–3.4 s against 2.9 s; the stencil adds two bins per face).
- [x] **Implicit refraction** (2026-10-03): `WaveModel2D::with_implicit_refraction`. Refraction set the step everywhere at Frøya (0.003 s on the coastline mesh against the geographic 0.30 s; 0.31 s against 4.2 s on the 1 km grid with a 0.14 rad/s cap, and a cap matched to the geographic step, ≈ 0.01 rad/s, bends the rays too little on the slopes that matter). Now the direction advection leaves the RK stages and is stepped at every node and frequency by backward Euler, first-order upwind in θ: a cyclic tridiagonal M-matrix (Thomas + Sherman–Morrison, no allocation), positive and conservative at any Courant number. With `SpectralAdvection::VanLeer` a deferred correction (MUSCL's flux less upwind's, of the state before the step, limited per face by its donor as Zalesak's FCT) makes the fixed point MUSCL's. Strang: half the direction step on each side of the stages.
  - Gates: unit, the cyclic solve to round-off and the action of every frequency at every node kept to 1e-13 at 10⁴ × the geographic step. `implicit_refraction_steps_past_the_turning_limit` (`tests/wave_model_test.rs`, the spread sea on a 20 → 2 m shelf over 300 m, where explicit MUSCL needs a third of the geographic step): implicit at the geographic step 0.195° off the exact mean direction against explicit MUSCL's 0.167°; 0.174° at a quarter of the step; without the correction 0.91° (explicit upwind 0.88°).
  - Measured: Lie splitting gave 0.268°; the per-row positivity scaling of the correction (one scale for all directions) 0.43°, since a single empty tail bin switched it off. The splitting error still falls about linearly with the step (backward Euler half-steps); a trapezoidal half-step would be second order but not positive at large Courant numbers.
  - [x] **Implicit frequency shifting** (2026-10-07): `WaveModel2D::with_implicit_frequency_shift` (`froya_real_data implicit=1` turns on both). As feared, in the first coupled run the tide's currents set the step. At a node 0.1 m deep (the depth floor) with 0.09 m/s of current, `∂σ/∂d U·∇d` limited the 1 km P1 wave grid to 0.6 s against the geographic 7 s, so 1,605 s instead of 110 s per model hour. The bound scales with the depth of the shallowest node carrying current, so a larger dry threshold would only have moved it. Now the frequency advection is stepped like the refraction, per node and direction: backward Euler, first-order upwind, outflow only through the grid's ends (a tridiagonal M-matrix), the same donor-limited MUSCL deferred correction, and half-steps on either side of the stages. `WaveModel2D::time_step_limits` names the bound that sets the step, and where.
    - Gates: `implicit_frequency_shifting_keeps_the_action_at_any_step` (unit; 10⁴ × the explicit step: non-negative, and each direction's action changes by exactly the outflow through the ends, to 1e-12). `implicit_frequency_shifting_steps_past_the_shifting_limit` (`tests/wave_model_test.rs`): the spread sea on a following current rising 0 → 2 m/s within one 100 m element, 49 frequencies. Explicit MUSCL is limited to 0.63 s against the geographic 1.39 s. Beyond the ramp the implicit mean σ is off the exact one by 2.1e-3 at the geographic step, against explicit MUSCL's 3.7e-3; without the correction 1.4e-2 (explicit upwind 1.3e-2). Inside the ramp every scheme is off by 3.5 %, from the ramp's spatial resolution.
    - The frequency shift shares the refraction's node passes (no extra transposes); it adds `n_dir` tridiagonal solves of `n_freq` per node and half-step, plus a strided gather and scatter of each direction's column. At Frøya the step costs about the same (110–126 against 118 s per model hour before the coupling).
  - [ ] Fusing the second half-pass into the next step's first (one pass of Δt between the stages, half-passes only at output times) would remove one pair of transposes. Measured 2026-10-08 at Frøya (1 km P1, 25 × 36): a pair is 9.6 ms of the 141 ms step (7 %), not the 15–20 % estimated; it needs the state to carry a pending half-pass (outputs, exchanges and `set_*` between steps would have to finish it first). Tiling the transposes by cache lines did not help (10.8 against 9.6 ms): they move 288 MB per pass at ≈ 30 GB/s, bandwidth-bound.
- [ ] `c_σ` from a changing depth (`∂σ/∂d ∂d/∂t`): tides shift frequencies over flats. It needs the rate of η from the circulation, or the difference of two water levels.
- [ ] Coupling (two-way, every coupling interval):
  - [x] circulation → waves (2026-10-07): `WaveCoupling2D::update_waves` (`set_water_level`, `set_currents` from the circulation's nodes onto the wave mesh's, any mesh and order; see Cost below).
  - [x] **A coupled run** (2026-10-07): `waves::CoupledWaves2D` over `Simulation::run_with_exchange` (the run in coupling intervals, `&mut` physics between them). Each interval `[t, t + Δ]` goes in turn. The waves take the level and current at `t` and step to `t + Δ`. Then the tide steps under the force linear in time between the two wave states (`WaveForce2D::between`, no jump at an exchange) and its Manning friction enhanced by the mean bed stress (Soulsby). Both are ramped.
    - Gate `breaking_waves_coupled_in_time_set_the_water_up`: the surf-zone beach, the waves on 7 P2 elements under 14, exchanging every 30 s from still water. After half an hour the setup at the wall, 5.6051 cm, is the passes' fixed point to 1.0e-4 (the passes still move by 6e-5).
    - **Frøya, 6 h** (`froya_real_data mesh=data/froya_coast.msh lts=8 hours=6 rest_hours=0 wave_coupling=10 wave_mesh=60,45 implicit=1`, swell H_s 2.5 m, T_p 10 s from 285°, no wind, from 2025-06-15): 1,022 s wall against 307 s uncoupled, 0 clips. The waves cost 125 s per model hour at the geographic 7.0 s step, 73 % of the run; the exchanges cost 0.3 %. H_s ≤ 4.1 m (at (−16.8, −14.4) km), mean 1.4 m over water ≥ 3 m deep. The largest force, 0.12 m²/s², is at (−10.7, −20.3) km in 0.5 m of water.
