    - At Mausund the waves raise the level by 3.7 mm on average after hour 2 (0.9–5.7 mm) and change the current by ≤ 4 mm/s; at NorKyst's point (the outer coast) by 6.1 mm and ≤ 1.2 cm/s. Small against the 4.4 cm centred RMSE of the tide, but this is a moderate swell without wind.
    - Two things the run found, both fixed: frequency shifting set the waves' step (implicit now, above), and `WaveCoupling2D::currents` handed the waves 75 m/s at a node 2.3 m deep. That came from the interpolated depth dipping between nodes; it is now capped at the source element's fastest wet node, gate `a_dip_in_the_depth_between_nodes_gives_no_spurious_current`.
    - [ ] The waves see the tide lagged by up to Δ (first order in the coupling interval). An extrapolated level and current (`2q(t) − q(t − Δ)`) or a second pass of the interval would make it second order; measure the lag's error at Δ = 10 / 5 / 2.5 min against the beach's or Frøya's setup first.
    - [ ] H_s, T_p and direction at the stations (the gauges, NorKyst's point, the farms) in the station files, for the validation item below and the viewer.
    - [ ] A longer coupled run at Frøya with wind and the met= weather (needs the waves' gridded wind, Forcing below), and a storm case: the setup at Mausund against the gauge's residual on a stormy day.
  - [x] waves → 2D circulation (2026-10-03): `source::WaveForce2D`, `−g ∇·(S/ρg)` per node from `WaveModel2D::radiation_stress` on the same mesh, differentiated within each element; ramped (`with_ramp`), zero on dry nodes.
    - Gates: a quadratic stress gives its exact divergence on a sheared P2 mesh (1e-14). `shoaling_waves_set_the_water_down` (`tests/wave_coupling_test.rs`): 10 s, 1 m swell shoaling over a closed basin from 20 to 3 m. The steady level follows Longuet-Higgins & Stewart's `η = −E k/sinh 2kh` to 0.27 % of its 3.3 cm range.
    - [x] Flux form (2026-10-03): the strong-form divergence with the lifted jumps to the central `½(S⁻ + S⁺)` on interior faces, so the force's total is exactly `−g ∮ S·n` (gate `the_force_conserves_momentum_across_jumps`: 1e-12 of `Σ w J |F|` for a stress that jumps at every face, sheared and periodic meshes; the element-local force misses by the jumps). Measured: the longshore force's excess over the dissipation's 9.5 → 6.8 % (the spikes at element ends gone; 4–5 % remain across the inner surf zone, resolution), the offshore residue −0.78 → −0.54 cm/s; the set-down basin unchanged. Its 2e-5 m/s residual current is the discrete pressure gradient's imbalance, not the force's form: 3.3e-6 m/s at 50 m elements.
    - [x] Surf zone (2026-10-03), on a plane beach with a wall a little offshore (1:100, 4 → 0.5 m, alongshore periodic: `Mesh2D::channel_periodic_x_with_sides`), 8 s swell of H_rms 0.6 m, Battjes–Janssen the only source, P2 at 25 m (`tests/wave_coupling_test.rs`, ≈ 4 s each in the ci profile):
      - `breaking_waves_set_the_water_up_in_the_surf_zone`, two-way (`set_water_level` each pass): set-down 0.69 cm at the breaker line, setup 5.6 cm at the wall; the level follows `g D ∂η/∂y = F_y` to 0.04 % of its range; the coupling lowers the setup 3.5 % against waves on still water and converges (0.15 % on the third pass). The surf slope is 0.039 of the bed's against Bowen's 0.062 for the mean κ = H_rms/D = 0.42: BJ is not saturated (κ 0.34 → 0.48 shoreward), so Bowen's slope is not a gate.
      - `oblique_breaking_waves_drive_a_longshore_current` (cos²⁰ at 20°, 72 directions, Chézy C_d = 2.5e-3): in the surf zone the current follows Longuet-Higgins's `C_d u² = g Σ (k cos θ/σ) D` (force from the dissipation alone) to 3.8 % (2.2 % at 12.5 m), peak 0.6 m/s.
      - [x] The radiation-stress force was 9.5 % above the dissipation's at element ends (the element-local derivative): fixed by the flux form above, 4–5 % across the inner surf zone at 25 m remain, falling with the mesh.
      - [ ] Seaward of the breakers the discrete refraction leaves `∂S_xy/∂y ≠ 0` and a −0.5 cm/s current at 5° bins (with the element-local force: −0.8 at 5°, −3.8 at 10°, −0.15 at 2.5°). Swell from a parent model at 10–15° bins will drive a spurious alongshore current of a few cm/s on long straight coasts; check the size at Frøya before trusting nearshore currents.
        - With the dissipation's force (`WaveForceForm::Dissipation`, 2026-10-09) there is no force seaward of the breakers; 1.3 mm/s remains there at 5° bins, carried across the breaker line by the discretisation (gate `the_dissipation_force_drives_the_longshore_current_and_no_other`).
      - [ ] No lateral mixing: the current profile is the local balance, so it jumps where the force does, and small currents near the breaker line take ≈ h/(2 C_d u) ≈ 3 h to spin up. Longuet-Higgins's mixing (or the SWE's horizontal viscosity) would smooth it; also the runup version with `WetDry` at a real shoreline (the force is zero on dry nodes, but partly dry elements are not tested).
    - [x] 3D, depth-uniform (2026-10-03): `WaveForce2D` on `Hydrostatic3D`'s barotropic module reaches every level as `F/D` through the mode splitting (`set_column_sums`); no new code. Gate `a_longshore_current_in_3d_has_the_viscous_profile_of_a_uniform_force` (`tests/wave_coupling_test.rs`, 10 σ-levels, ν = 0.01, quadratic drag 2.5e-3 on the bottom layer): each column is the exact parabola `u_b = √(F/C_d)`, `u(ζ) = u_b + (F/νD)(Dζ − ζ²/2)|_{ζ_b}^{ζ}` to 0.22 % of the fastest current at interior nodes (3.5 % on element faces, the horizontal flux coupling, as in 2D). A setup under steady waves is the 2D one to round-off (no shear at rest), so it needs no gate of its own.
    - [ ] 3D vertical structure: the force is depth-uniform, so there is no undertow and the shear comes only from the bed. Next: (a) the Stokes transport in the continuity (the Eulerian return flow under a wall: `D ū = −M_s`), which changes the 2D model too; (b) the breaking force distributed near the surface (Uchiyama et al. 2010's `B(z)`, or a roller surface stress), so the undertow profile follows (gate against the constant-ν analytic undertow, e.g. Svendsen 1984 / Stive & Wind 1986); (c) the conservative part as the vortex force and Bernoulli head with the Stokes drift (McWilliams et al. 2004; COAWST, ROMS `WEC_VF`).
  - [x] waves → bed, 2D (2026-10-03): `source::WaveCurrentFriction2D` wraps any `BottomFriction2D` law and multiplies its rate by Soulsby's (1995) `1 + 1.2 (τ_w/(τ_c + τ_w))^3.2`. `τ_w/ρ = ½ f_w U_w²` per node comes from `WaveModel2D::bed_wave_stress(n, z₀)`: `f_w = 1.39 (A/z₀)^(−0.52)`, `U_w = √2 U_rms`, `A = U_w T_z/2π` with `T_z` the near-bed velocity's zero-crossing period.
    - Gates: one component's stress is Soulsby's exactly (a 12.5 s, 1 m wave in 8 m on 2 mm: 7.4 Pa). The enhancement's limits (1, 1 + 1.2·2^−3.2, 2.2). `waves_slow_a_current_by_soulsbys_enhanced_friction`: a channel current of 0.63 m/s slows to 0.46 m/s under a 6 Pa sea, both the roots of the steady balance to 1e-7.
    - [ ] 3D: `BottomDrag3D` under waves (the same enhancement of the log-layer `C_d`, or Grant–Madsen's apparent roughness, ROMS `SSW_BBL`).
    - [ ] The maximum stress `τ_max` (with the angle between waves and current) for sediment and the cages' moorings.
  - [x] waves → GLS (2026-10-03): a surface roughness per column, `Hydrostatic3D::with_surface_roughness` / `set_surface_roughness` (`[element][node]`), from `WaveModel2D::surface_roughness(n, 0.6)` (Terray et al.'s `z₀ₛ ≈ 0.6 H_s`). It reaches the closure as `Column::surface_roughness`, through `apply_vertical_diffusion`'s new `SurfaceFields { stress, roughness }`. GLS takes the largest of its constant roughness, the column's and Charnock's.
    - Gate `each_column_feels_its_own_surface_roughness`: columns with 0.3, 1.2 and 0 m end bit for bit where lone columns whose closure has that roughness (the constant for 0) do. The rough sea mixes the top 3× more (K 1.4e-2 against 4.8e-3 m²/s at the top interior w-point, k-ω with breaking, 0.1 Pa, 2 h).
    - [ ] The breaking flux `c_w u*³` stays uniform, from the wind's u*; a wave-dependent flux (the whitecapping dissipation of the wave model, as ROMS/COAWST's `TKE_WAVEDISS`) would tie it to the sea state. The per-node dissipation diagnostic exists since 2026-10-09 (`WaveModel2D::dissipation(n, dt)`: whitecapping, friction and breaking in m²/s, times ρg the energy flux into the water): `ρ g × whitecapping` (+ breaking) per column is the flux to give GLS's surface boundary condition, interpolated as the roughness is. The same diagnostic would map where waves break for the viewer.
    - [ ] `farm_3d waves=` still takes a constant roughness; with the wave model run on the farm mesh (see the Stokes item) it should take `surface_roughness`.
  - [x] waves → particles (2026-10-03): `particles::WithStokesDrift2D` (a fixed depth) and `WithStokesDrift3D` (each particle's depth) add the drift of `waves::StokesDriftField` to any flow, steady or linear in time between wave states. The field compresses each node's spectrum to one vector per frequency (exact) and samples the exact finite-depth profile at the particle.
    - Gates: the field equals `WaveModel2D::stokes_drift` at every node and depth to 1e-12; 2D and 3D particles over still water move by exactly their depth's drift.
    - `farm_3d stokes=1.5,6,0` (a uniform 1.5 m, 6 s sea along the channel; 7.9 / 3.6 / 1.6 / 0.9 cm/s at 0 / 1 / 3 / 5 m): the larvae drift +1268 m instead of +1121 m in the hour after release, and spread 94 m instead of 44 m, from the drift's shear over their 0–2.5 m depths; the same sea across the channel (`stokes=1.5,6,90`) moves them 125 m sideways. Faeces and feed (sinking 3 and 10 cm/s) land within 2 m of where they did.
    - [ ] The vertical Stokes velocity (`−∫∇·u_s dz`) is left out, as in OpenDrift and LADiM; it matters only where the sea state changes over short distances (a lee, a shoal).
    - [ ] `farm_3d` uses a uniform parametric sea. With wind (`wind=`) it should run the wave model on the farm mesh (fetch-limited across the channel) and couple at each output interval (`StokesDrift::between`).
    - [ ] Cost: each sample sums `n_freq` exponentials over interpolated `k` and `b` (≈ 3 n_freq · n_nodes multiply–adds). It added 0.15 s to 0.55 s of tracking for 300 particles over an hour of 10 s steps (`farm_3d`). For 10⁵ particles, tabulate the profile per node on a few depths, or a Breivik et al. (2016) two-parameter profile.

Make fully dry elements absorbing like the coast, and use the shoreline subcells (`WetDry`) for the partly dry ones. Measured 2026-10-09: on the floor it is the bottom friction, not breaking, that takes the energy (Battjes–Janssen's dissipation saturates at `H_max² = (0.073 m)²`); capping friction's losses (`GrowthLimiter::RateBothSigns`) let 19 m seas pile up against Frøya's cliffs. Absorbing land would make the result independent of the floor and of the sinks' details.
  - [x] **Absorbing land** (2026-10-09, branch `feat/wave-absorbing-land`): `WaveModel2D::with_absorbing_land`, opt-in (`froya_real_data wave_land=absorbing`). A node is dry where the water is shallower than the floor. Dry nodes lose their action every propagation step. Wholly dry elements are not stepped, and their faces are coast. Gates `absorbing_land_is_the_coast_of_a_mesh_without_it` (bit for bit the mesh with the dry elements cut out and walled) and `absorbing_land_keeps_the_waves_off_it`: on a beach, floored land held H_s 2.6 m (6.9 m on a 0.01 m floor) against 1.85 m in the water; absorbing land holds 0, and the floor moves the water's H_s by 0.8 % instead of 12.6 %. In the Frøya storm under `wave_limiter=rate2` (hours 2–20): the largest H_s 9.5 m against 20.5 m, the force 1.1 against 7.4 m²/s², and MET's points within 1.5 cm. Found on the way: `with_depth_min` rebuilt η from the floored depth (fixed, CHANGELOG).

H_s bias against MET's WAM after hour 2, SWAN's sources at 7 s (floored → absorbing): west −0.087 → −0.088 m, lee −0.440 → −0.443, Frohavet −0.522 → −0.523, north +0.314 → +0.316, reef −1.229 → −1.304. WAM's at 56 s: all five within 3 mm (the reef −1.339 both). Largest H_s 9.30 → 9.33 m and 9.20 → 9.33 m (at (−13.8, −12.4) km, in the water); the largest force 1.30 → 1.26 and 1.35 → 1.28 m²/s². So under the growth-only caps absorbing land changes the farm-relevant points by millimetres; it matters where the sinks are capped (`RateBothSigns`, the 20.5 → 9.5 m above) and it makes the result independent of the floor.

The dissipation's force removes them (2026-10-09, under Validation).

  - [ ] Partly dry elements are still a nodal approximation: the dry nodes are zeroed, so the coast lies somewhere between a wet and a dry node, and the RK stages still carry the dry nodes' floored group velocity and the floored depth's gradient (the 0.8 % floor dependence on the beach). The shoreline subcells (`WetDry`'s reconstruction) would put the coast where the water ends.

Masking them saves at most ≈ 6.5 % of the source pass, ≈ 2–3 ms of the ≈ 105 ms step (and only with the DIA's lanes compacted to wet nodes): low priority.

Build boundary spectra for past dates (the June 2025 tide runs) as a sum of partitions (JONSWAP per partition, cos^m spreading narrower for the swells) at points along the open boundary; gate on the forecast days, where both the spectra and the partitions exist.

Wall time per model hour, against ≈ 38 s for the tidal circulation on the coastline mesh (LTS, SSP-RK(4,3)):

    | Mesh, order, components | Δt (sets it) | Per step | Per model hour |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | coastline (12.1k el., 19–2700 m), P2, 25 × 36 | 0.003 s (refraction) | 2.98 s | ≈ 1,170 h |
    | the same, `turning=0.14` | 0.30 s (geographic) | 2.89 s | 9.6 h |
    | the same, P1 | 0.31 s | 1.19 s | 3.8 h |
    | the same, P2, 24 × 24 | 0.30 s | 1.78 s | 5.9 h |
    | 1 km grid (2.5k el.), P2, `turning=0.14` | 0.31 s (refraction) | 0.46 s | 1.5 h |
    | 1 km grid, P2, `turning=0.0104` | 4.2 s (geographic) | 0.47 s | 404 s (8.9× real time) |
    | 1 km grid, P1, `turning=0.0062` | 7.0 s | 0.19 s | 99 s (36× real time) |
    | coastline, P2, implicit refraction | 0.30 s (geographic) | 3.31 s | 10.9 h |
    | 1 km grid, P2, implicit | 4.2 s | 0.54 s | 459 s (7.8× real time) |
    | 1 km grid, P1, implicit | 7.0 s | 0.23 s | 118 s (31× real time) |

  - Per component and node and step: 14–22 ns of propagation (three RK stages; ≈ 5–7 ns a stage) and 7–10 ns of sources (26–34 % of the step, the DIA and the transposes).
  - So: (1) refraction was the gate (implicit θ, above, done: the last three rows); (2) the coastline mesh is 10× the 1 km grid per step and 14× smaller in Δt, and local time stepping would save at most 4.5× there (steps 0.30–18 s), 1.1× on the 1 km grid; (3) the waves want their own coarser mesh, which needs interpolation between meshes in the coupling (today the force, the bed stress and the roughness assume the circulation's mesh and order). With implicit refraction a 1 km P1 wave grid costs ≈ 3× the circulation per model hour (118 s against 38 s).
  - [x] **A wave mesh of their own** (2026-10-07): `waves::WaveCoupling2D` carries every exchange between a wave model and a circulation on different meshes and orders, over `operators::MeshTransfer2D` (each target node evaluates the source element's polynomial: `PointLocator2D`, and the new `PointLocator2D::nearest` for nodes outside the source mesh).
    - Circulation → waves: η (dry nodes take their element's wet mean, or 0) and the current as interpolated transport over interpolated depth (none outside the circulation's mesh). Waves → circulation: `S/ρg` interpolated and then differentiated on the circulation's mesh (`force`, flux form kept), the bed stress and roughness (kept ≥ 0), the Stokes drift (`StokesDriftField::transferred`).
    - Gates: unit, exactness for fields the source represents, second order from P1, the same mesh is the identity (also for a field that jumps at every face), the same-mesh coupling equals the direct one to 1e-12. `shoaling_waves_on_a_coarse_mesh_of_their_own_set_the_water_down`: the set-down against Longuet-Higgins & Stewart off by 5.2 / 2.2 % at 7 / 14 P1 wave elements (0.88 % at 28), 1.0 % at 7 P2 elements (0.40 % at 14), against 0.27 % on the same mesh; the wave state is exact at its own nodes, so this is the polynomial of `S` between them. `breaking_waves_on_a_coarse_mesh_of_their_own_set_the_water_up` (two-way, 7 P2 elements of 50 m under 14 of 25 m): setup 5.793 → 5.597 cm against 5.806 → 5.604 cm.
    - Measured at Frøya (`froya_real_data mesh=data/froya_coast.msh waves=10 wave_mesh=60,45 implicit=1`, 24 threads): the waves on the 1 km P1 grid at 122 s per model hour; the coupling to the coastline mesh (108,909 P2 nodes) builds in 9 ms, and one exchange takes 176 ms (0.7 wave steps): level and currents 28, force 37, bed stress 21, roughness 38, Stokes drift 53 ms. Exchanging every 10 model minutes adds ≈ 1 %.
    - [ ] Most of an exchange is the wave model's own per-node diagnostics, serial over the nodes: `parameters` (the roughness), `bed_wave_stress`, `radiation_stress`, `StokesDriftField::new` (23 ms), and `set_water_level`'s wavenumber tables (25 frequencies × nodes, a Newton solve each). Parallelise them over nodes (`for_each_chunk` in `waves/model.rs`), and compute H_s, the stresses and the Stokes vectors in one pass over each node's spectrum when several are wanted.
    - [ ] P2 on a coarser wave mesh is the better use of nodes in the shoaling gate (1.0 % on 7 P2 elements, 21 nodes along the basin, against 2.2 % on 14 P1 elements, 28 nodes), and a P2 step at twice the element size is ≈ 1.2× P1's. Measure a 2 km P2 wave grid at Frøya (`wave_mesh=30,23,2`) against the 1 km P1 one, for cost and for H_s in the lee of the skerries.
    - [ ] From the fine circulation onto the coarse waves the transfer samples, so currents narrower than a wave element alias (a jet through a sound is seen where a wave node happens to fall). An L2 projection from fine to coarse (the fine mesh's quadrature, weights `M_c⁻¹ Pᵀ M_f` with `P` the coarse-to-fine interpolation already built) would average them and conserve the integrals. It would also replace the cap on the currents (`WaveCoupling2D::currents`, 2026-10-07): project the transport and the depth and divide the averages.
    - [ ] In the coupled Frøya run the wave step (7.0 s) is set every hour by the lowest bin, 0.04 Hz, in 150 m of water (c_g ≈ 19 m/s), where a 10 s swell has almost no energy. `f_min = 0.05 Hz` would lengthen the step by ≈ 25 %, and LTS by frequency group (Options below) would let the low bins step alone. Check H_s and T_p against a 0.04 Hz run before changing the example's default.
    - [ ] At Frøya 638 of the 10,040 wave nodes lie outside the coastline mesh, up to 3.5 km from it (water the 1 km grid keeps and the coastline mesh does not, e.g. inner lakes and fjord arms). They take the nearest level and no current; masking the wave grid to the coastline mesh's footprint would save their cost.
  - The old estimate (≈ 70× the tidal run at P2) was optimistic by ≈ 10× on the coastline mesh because of the step, not the kernel.
  - Options:
    - P1 or a coarser mesh for the waves, with interpolation between orders in the coupling;
    - local time stepping per component group (low frequencies are fastest);
    - fewer components (24 × 25);
    - implicit geographic stepping as SWAN (Gauss–Seidel sweeps).
  - [x] **Long steps for the sources: WAM's integration and propagation substeps** (2026-10-08, branch `perf/wave-substeps`). Off by default (today's bits): `WaveModel2D::with_substeps(n)`, `SourceIntegration::Implicit`, `GrowthLimiter::Rate`; `froya_real_data wave_sources=wam wave_substeps=N wave_cfl=`.
    - Measured first (temporary probe on `wave_step_bench`'s sea, a young 15 m/s sea and swell, relative H_s error after 60 min against a converged run): the propagation's time error is negligible (8e-5 at the 7 s step without sources) and SSP-RK3 stays stable to CFL ≥ 1.5. The error and the stable step are the sources'. Frozen rates are first order (1.1e-2 at 7 s, 3.8e-2 at 28 s); Ris's limiter caps the growth per step, so it is about half of it; without it frozen rates blow up at 28 s.
    - `SourceIntegration::Implicit`: the DIA implicit in each component's own energy, WAM's scheme (ECMWF IFS Cy33r1 Part VII §5.2, ecWAM `implsch.F90`), as the exponential update with `B = B_local + min(Λ_nl, 0)`. `Λ_nl` is the DIA's diagonal derivative (`Quadruplets::source_and_diagonal`, in the lanes too; gate `the_diagonal_is_the_transfers_derivative` against finite differences). Stable without a limiter to 28 s (blows up at 55 s), error 1.4e-2 at 28 s.
    - `GrowthLimiter::Rate`: Hersbach & Janssen's limiter, `ΔF ≤ C g ũ* f⁻⁴ f_c Δt`, `C = 5e-7` (ecWAM's; 3e-7 in the paper), `ũ* = max(u*, 5.6e-3 g/f_c)`, growth only. With it the result no longer depends on the step: the same to 3 digits from 7 to 109 s, stable at 164 s. Against the scheme's own converged run: 9.4e-4 at 7 s, 3.6e-3 at 55 s, 1.1e-2 at 164 s (SWAN's scheme: 1.1e-2 at 7 s).
    - Growth curves (`wave_growth integration=implicit limiter=rate substeps=`; gates `implicit_sources_keep_the_fetch_curve_at_long_steps`, `implicit_sources_grow_a_sea_whatever_the_step`): fetch-limited within the KC92 gate's bounds at a 145 s step (E* 1.08–0.61×, f_p* 0.99–1.09×, against SWAN's 1.17–0.61× and 0.96–1.08×), 1.8 % from the 18 s curve. Duration-limited: 96 h within 0.15 % of SWAN's; the young sea grows more slowly (E* 4.7e-5 against 8.1e-5 at 30 min, equal by 6 h), the limiter acting as physics as HJ99 say. SWAN's scheme moves 8–9 % between the same short and long steps.
    - Frøya (`froya_real_data mesh=data/froya_coast.msh waves=20 wave_mesh=60,45 implicit=1`, 24 threads, wall s per model hour): SWAN 58; `wave_sources=wam` 60; + `wave_substeps=4` 39; + `wave_cfl=1.0` 19 (56 s outer step, 3.0×); `wave_substeps=8 wave_cfl=1.0` 18. Over 6.3 h at a 56 s outer step CFL 1.0 matches CFL 0.5 to 1.5e-4 in total action (H_s ≤ 3.79 / 3.78 m); CFL 1.25 is stable too. Against the 7 s step over the same 6.3 h: SWAN's sources 6.461e8 total action and H_s ≤ 3.96 m, WAM's 6.442e8 and 3.96 m at 7 s, 6.473e8 and 3.79 m at 56 s.
    - `SourceIntegration::Midpoint` (exponential midpoint, second order at a node: 5.4e-3 → 1.1e-4 at 60 s) and the symmetric placement of the sources between the substeps barely helped in the domain (1.14 → 1.04e-2), where the limiter dominated; `with_time_integrator(SSPRK43)` brought nothing (the propagation's error is not the issue).

The wall time is 26 min against 37 (the waves' stepping 508 against 1,168–1,205 s).
    - **The source scheme is.** WAM's sources against SWAN's (both at 7 s) lower H_s by 12.4 % in the lee south of Frøya (9.03°E), 9.1 % at Frohavet, 4.3 % at the north point and 3.4 % on the reef, and leave the open west point alone. Against MET's WAM forecast, bias in m (SWAN → WAM scheme): lee −0.44 → −0.61, Frohavet −0.52 → −0.72, north +0.31 → +0.07, reef −1.23 → −1.35, west −0.09 → −0.09. WAM's scheme is better on the open north boundary and worse at the sheltered points, which are where farms are. Likely Hersbach & Janssen's limiter slowing the young, fetch-limited seas of the lee, as it did on the duration curve (E* 4.7e-5 against 8.1e-5 at 30 min); the implicit DIA is the other candidate.
    - **Diagnosed (2026-10-09): it is the limiter.** `froya_real_data` now takes the two parts alone (`wave_integration=frozen|implicit`, `wave_limiter=ris|rate`). At 7 s, SWAN's frozen rates with `GrowthLimiter::Rate` lower H_s exactly as WAM's whole scheme does (lee −12.4 %, Frohavet −9.1 %), and WAM's implicit DIA with Ris's limiter does not (−0.1 %, 0.0 %; the reef −2.2 %). Part of it was a bug, now fixed: the limit's `f_c` was the whole spectrum's mean frequency, the swell's in the lee, where ecWAM takes `max(f̄_windsea, f̄)` (CHANGELOG). With the fix the WAM scheme at 56 s is at −9.1 % in the lee, −6.3 % at Frohavet and −3.8 % at the north point; against MET's forecast, bias −0.57, −0.66 and +0.10 m (SWAN's scheme: −0.44, −0.52, +0.31).
    - [x] ~~The rest of the limiter's effect: ecWAM caps the losses too.~~ Tried 2026-10-09 (branch `feat/wave-limiter-losses`): `GrowthLimiter::RateBothSigns`, `|ΔF| ≤ C g ũ* f⁻⁴ f_c Δt`, with breaking on a pass of its own even in one step (`froya_real_data wave_limiter=rate2`, `wave_growth limiter=rate2`). **It does not explain the lee, and it is harmful here; opt-in, not the default.**
      - The storm (the command above with `wave_sources=wam wave_limiter=rate2 wave_substeps=4 wave_cfl=1.0`, `output/storm_steps/wam56both/`, 28 min, 0 clips): bias against MET's WAM, growth-only → two-sided cap, lee −0.57 → −0.55 m, Frohavet −0.66 → −0.64, north +0.10 → +0.11, reef −1.34 → −1.32, west −0.09 → −0.09. The lee's deficit is the growth cap's.
      - But the largest H_s rose from 8.8 to 19.5 m and the force from 1.3 to 6.5 m²/s², against the cliff coast at (−9.9, −12.4) km. At one node the cap never binds at 1–30 m deep (H_s after an hour identical, with or without the DIA). On the 0.1 m depth floor it caps the bottom friction, which is what removes the waves arriving at land nodes (Battjes–Janssen saturates at `H_max²`): a 2 m sea keeps 1.8 m over a 56 s step, against 0.17 m. ecWAM has no floored land nodes.
      - Less step-independent too (`implicit_sources_grow_a_sea_whatever_the_step` with it): 300 s against 60 s +4.1 % in E* at 6 h (growth-only +2.2 %), 0.08 % at 96 h; at 900 s, 6 h is a different sea (f_p* +189 %) and 96 h is 8 % low.
      - Gates: `the_two_sided_limit_caps_the_losses_by_the_growths_limit` (unit), `the_two_sided_limiter_leaves_breaking_whole` (the substeps' shelf: H_s in the surf zone 0.81 % off no limiter, 0.24 % of it the breaking pass's order and the rest the cap on friction; with breaking inside the cap 5.9× the H_s).
    - [ ] The lee's deficit under WAM's scheme is then the growth limiter itself (`GrowthLimiter::Rate`, C = 5e-7). How it depends on C (3e-7 as published, 1e-6) at the lee and Frohavet points, against the duration and fetch gates, would show whether the young sea's slower start is the cap or a real difference; one 25 min storm run per C.
    - [ ] Decide the scheme with observations, not the parent. The parent's 800 m grid does not resolve the skerries, so its lee is not truth either: the reef point shows how far the two differ in shallow water. A buoy or ADCP wave record at a sheltered farm (Validation below) would settle it. Until then the choice is between today's results (SWAN's scheme, 37 min for the 24 h storm) and 1.5× cheaper runs with 6–9 % lower H_s in the lees (WAM's at 56 s, 25 min). The implicit DIA with Ris's limiter is not a way out: Ris's cap is per step, so it tightens at long steps.
  - [ ] Under 1 m deep the 56 s step still has +8 % H_s bias (16 % RMS; all sources at 14 s give −5 %): the sources left on the outer step. The likely one is the DIA's shallow-water scaling (`quadruplet_scale`, large at small kd) shifting energy to low frequencies, which lowers Battjes–Janssen's mean frequency and its dissipation. Bottom friction is linear, so its exponential update does not depend on the step. Test by moving each to the substeps. Low priority: farms are not in the surf zone, and the force it feeds the circulation is what matters.
  - [ ] Each substep runs two node passes (the halves before and after its stages); the after-pass of one substep and the before-pass of the next could be one full-step pass (`R_h` for `R_{h/2} R_{h/2}`, both first order), m + 1 passes per step instead of 2m. Measure what it saves first: at Frøya the 56 s step (4 substeps) takes 380 ms, the 7 s step 118 ms.

If it is wanted later, take bottom friction out of the cap as breaking is, or make dry land absorbing first (the Wet/dry item).

    - The DIA was 43.5 ms of the step (26 %, against the fetch gate's ≈ 10 %): 116 ns per component, its gathers and scatters bounds-checking, wrapping (`rem_euclid`) and continuing into the tail at every access. Now the spectrum extended by the rows the stencil reaches and each landing's direction bins are tabulated once per node (per-thread scratch): 12.5 ms (3.5×). Gate `the_tables_give_the_direct_transfer` (the old transfer kept as the reference, bit for bit, with and without the tail and at 6 directions).
    - The implicit refraction did ≈ 8 integer remainders per bin (periodic neighbours): branches instead, 28.4 → 18.6 ms per half-pass; the cyclic solve's two Thomas sweeps share one elimination (`thomas_pair`), → 15.8 ms. `c_θ`, `c_σ` split into a frequency's factor and terms of the node and direction (`NodeRates`, computed once per node): no measurable gain on its own (the `sin_cos` were not the cost), kept since it removes 864 of 900 per node.

        - The step is node-major throughout (`waves/propagation.rs`). It transposes once in and once out, instead of around each of the two node passes (4 transposes → 2).
        - The geographic term takes 8 directions of a frequency per vector. They share the group velocity, the current and the element geometry; 36 directions are 4 full vectors and one padded one.
        - The implicit refraction takes 8 frequencies of a node per vector (`waves/implicit_lanes.rs`), the frequency shift 8 directions. The early returns become selects, and so does Sherman–Morrison against plain Thomas; the plain Thomas solve runs only when a lane needs it.
        - `benches/wave_step_bench.rs` (new, 60 × 45 P1 grid, 25 × 36 components, implicit): step 1.22 s → 0.61 s on 1 thread, 188 → 127 ms on 24. Frøya (`waves=20 wave_mesh=60,45 implicit=1`, 24 threads): 151–161 → 113 ms per step, 77–83 → 58 s of wall time per model hour.
        - Lesson: the lane solvers keep ≈ 75 KB of vector arrays on the stack. Inlined into rayon's recursive split frames, they overflowed the worker stacks (only with many threads), so their entry points are `#[inline(never)]`.
      - [x] **The DIA across nodes** (2026-10-08, bit for bit): `Quadruplets::source_lanes` (`waves/dia_lanes.rs`) runs 8 nodes per vector on their spectra interleaved (`[c][8]`). Every gather and scatter becomes a vector load or store, and an empty component is a zero transfer in its lane (the scalar transfer skips it; adding +0 keeps every sum's bits). The node pass takes 8-node chunks when the DIA acts (`SourceTerms::integrate_lanes`). Gates `the_lanes_give_each_nodes_transfer` (unit) and `the_vector_kernels_give_the_scalar_bits` (the whole step, a partial last chunk); the Frøya total action after 22 steps equals `main`'s to 18 digits.
        - `wave_step_bench` (1 thread): the DIA 127 → 47 ms (from `sources` less the new `sources_without_dia`), the sources 247 → 150 ms, the step 597 → 498 ms; 24 threads 120 → 111 ms. LLVM already vectorises the call-free loop. With `Level::new()` + `dispatch!` per frequency row (25 per node) it was 186 ms: dispatch once per node or coarser.

        - `advance` recomputes `group_velocity` (a `sinh`) per frequency and node where `WaveModel2D` caches `cg` from the same inputs, so passing it in is bit for bit (≈ 1 %). The bottom friction's `sinh(k d)` per frequency and node changes only with `set_water_level`; it could be cached there too.

      - [ ] At 24 threads the node passes alone are bound by their transposes (≈ 15 ms a half-step of refraction or shift, nearly all of it transposes). A node-major `WaveSolution` would remove the last two per step. Its callers use `component(c)` (coupling, Stokes drift, tests), so that is an API change.
    - [x] `Quadruplets::stencil` is no longer rebuilt at every node and step (2026-10-08). The DIA's tables (stencil, landing bins, tail factors, `(σ/2π)¹¹`) are cached per thread for the grid, λ and tail they belong to (`Tables::cached` in `waves/nonlinear.rs`).

Our waves break and refract on it (H_s 3.5 → 1.6 m across it at 1 km); WAM's do not. Which is right needs a measurement; this is where a coastline-resolving model should differ from the 800 m parent.

H_s against WAM over hours 2–9 (bias, RMSE in m, the parent's uniform wind → MET Nordic per node): 8.05°E −0.09, 0.09 → −0.03, 0.04 of 4.80; the reef at 8.38°E −1.09, 1.09 → −0.80, 0.81 of 4.00; the lee at 9.03°E −0.07, 0.12 → −0.23, 0.24 of 1.20; Frohavet −0.44, 0.46 → −0.49, 0.50 of 2.06; 9.00°E 64.00°N −0.05, 0.10 → −0.01, 0.15 of 3.69. The local wind closes about a quarter of the reef's deficit and sharpens the west point; in the lee the weather's sheltered wind replaces the stronger offshore mean, so the lee's deficit is the swell's way in, not the wind. The cliff-coast currents are unchanged (8–9 m/s at (−13.2, −12.1) km, below). A full 24 h run would give the numbers across the peak (≈ 66 min on an idle machine).

On the 1 km P1 wave grid the averaged slope focuses the waves (H_s up to 9.3 m against 7 m at the boundary), and they die on floored land nodes instead of reflecting. The interpolated stress's divergence then gives the tide ≈ 1 m²/s², which the depth cap allows at 8 m. Real cliff coasts reflect and pass little momentum. It claimed 27 m²/s at one node, a force of 254 m²/s² there and 127 on the circulation, and 20 m/s and η +17 m at (−15.2, −2.4) km in hour 21. Over a step the integrator can only remove the energy there is (`D ≤ E/Δt`), so the diagnostic is now the frozen-rate removal over a step, `(B_term/B) E (1 − e^{BΔt})/Δt`; `dt = 0` is the instant.

H_s bias at MET's points: reef −1.34 → −1.27 m, north +0.094 → +0.065, the rest within 7 mm.       - [x] **The root of the spike: a depth limit on the spectrum** (2026-10-10, branch `feat/wave-depth-limit`). Wave nodes in a few decimetres of water held metres of H_s, because Battjes–Janssen saturates at `Q_b = 1` (rate ≈ 6e-4/s at H_s 2 m in 0.11 m) and a 1 km P1 element has nodes 0.1 m and 140 m deep. `SourceTerms::with_depth_limit` scales each node's spectrum down after every step (after breaking, in its own pass with substeps) to `H_rms ≤ γ d`, `m₀ ≤ (γd)²/8`, shape kept: the edge of Battjes–Janssen's model (every wave breaking). Chosen over absorbing nodes below a few decimetres, which would only move the problem to the next-shallowest node. Opt-in in the library; `froya_real_data wave_depth_limit=1` is the example's default, and its waves line now reports the largest `H_s/d`. Not checked against SWAN's own very-shallow limiter (its constant was not found).

        - The storm (WAM's sources at 56 s, absorbing land, the dissipation's force, `output/storm_steps/wam56_depthlimit/`, 0 clips), against the earlier run without the limit (`wam56_dissip_step`): H_s/d ≤ 1.03 every hour (at nodes 0.12–1.73 m deep); the largest force 0.0093 against 0.109 m²/s², which sat at (−15.2, −2.4) km in hour 21, the first run's blow-up (0.0081 there and then); H_s at MET's points within 1 cm of bias but the reef (−1.27 → −1.28 m); the largest H_s 9.46 m in both (in deep water off the cliff coast); the largest current the tide's own (4.14 against 4.12 m/s at the pinnacle). Wall 1,649 s against 1,569 s, but contended for an hour (a test build); no cost measurement.

        - [x] **Deep nodes of shoreline elements.** Measured 2026-10-10: at Frøya the limit moves the farms' water by at most 5 %, at a handful of coastal nodes. The waves alone, 6 h from a storm sea at the boundary (`waves=1 wave_mesh=60,45 implicit=1 wave_sources=wam wave_substeps=4 wave_cfl=1.0 wave_hours=6 wave_sea=5,12,285`, limit off, then on against it with `wave_reference=`; `output/depth_limit/`): it binds at 26 of 10,040 nodes, all under 2 m deep (17 under 0.5 m). Over 10 m deep, 11 of 3,354 nodes move by more than 2 % and none by more than 5 %; 9 of them up, the largest +5.0 % (3.14 → 3.29 m, 27.6 m deep, (−9.9, −4.5) km) and +3.5 % at 39 m; all in or beside an element with a limited node. RMS 0.32 % (10–30 m) and 0.15 % (30–100 m). Under 1 m the limit does its work: 69 nodes, down by up to 2.80 → 0.42 m. A finer wave grid at the coasts remains the cure for these elements (the Validation item's (3)).
          - Was: in the P1 gate the limit raises H_s at the shoreline element's 20 m node from 2.46 to 2.76 m (+12 %): one linear polynomial across the element, its shallow end pulled down. Farms sit in deep water near coasts, at the deep ends of such elements. A finer wave grid at the coasts is the cure if it matters (the Validation item's (3)).

The waves line now counts the nodes at the limit by depth band (`depth_limit_bands` in `examples/froya_real_data.rs`). In the waves-alone run above it binds at 3 nodes 1–2 m deep (of 140) and the 1–3 m band moves by 1.1 % RMS, so it takes little of breaking's work there; the coupled storm's counts (with the tide's levels) are still to read. Counting its removal as breaking would need the removed variance per node from the source pass (it runs inside `advance`); worth it only if the storm shows it binding in metres of water.

---

## Priority 0 (2026-09-25 review): Confirmed Correctness Bugs — OPEN

All confirmed in code or by measurement (`REVIEW.md` "Top correctness bugs"). Each is small. Each needs a regression test that would have caught it.

### P0.12 Flather characteristic relation applied twice (BLOCKER, open boundaries) — FIXED: [PR #4](https://github.com/EmilLindfors/roms-rs/pull/4)
- [x] **The bug.** Ghost normal velocity is set to `un_ext + √(g/h)(η_int − η_ext)`, and the Riemann solver then applies the characteristic relation again.
  - Result: reflection coefficient ≈ −1/3; a forced tide arrives at ≈ 0.65 amplitude; −0.57 reflection for the `ChapmanFlather2D` defaults.
  - Affected: `Flather2D`, `HarmonicFlather2D` (`boundary_2d.rs:381,821`), `TSTOBC2D` (`tst_obc.rs:347`), `NestingBC2D` (`nesting_bc.rs:189`), `OceanNestingBC2D` (`ocean_nesting.rs:218`), `ChapmanFlather2D` (`chapman.rs:278`).
- [x] **Fix.** Ghost = external state (η_ext, u_ext) and let the upwind flux impose Flather. `OceanNestingBC2D::with_flather(false)` is already correct.
- [x] **Tests.** Outgoing-pulse reflection < 1 %; delivered progressive-tide amplitude ≥ 0.95. (`tests/open_boundary_flather_test.rs`: reflection < 1e-5, amplitude 0.997–1.001.)

### P0.13 Hydrostatic reconstruction leaks mass when B jumps across faces (BLOCKER, 2D) — FIXED (2D and 1D, [PR #6](https://github.com/EmilLindfors/roms-rs/pull/6))
- [x] **The bug.** `f_int = normal_flux(&q_int_flux)` uses the *reconstructed* state (`swe_2d.rs:494`, parallel `:1021`). Measured: relative mass change −5.5e-5 per second with cell-averaged B and slope-correlated flow; ~1e-20 with continuous B.
  - Active in `froya_real_data` (`to_cell_average` + `with_well_balanced(true)`).
- [x] **Fix.** Use `normal_flux(&q_int)`, keep F* from the reconstructed states, and add ½g(h*⁻² − h⁻²)n to the momentum components only (DG Audusse / Xing–Shu). Apply identically in serial and parallel.
- [x] **Tests.**
  - A periodic mass-rate test with cell-averaged B and u ∝ cos·cos (a uniform u hides the bug by symmetry).
  - Lake-at-rest with cell-averaged B, serial and parallel.

### P0.14 Documentation directs users to the unbalanced configuration (MAJOR, 2D) — FIXED: [PR #11](https://github.com/EmilLindfors/roms-rs/pull/11)
- [x] The `SWE2DRhsConfig::well_balanced` / `with_well_balanced` docs (`swe_2d.rs:50-53,142-153`) say to omit `BathymetrySource2D`. Doing so gives ≈ 0.6 m/s² lake-at-rest residuals with nodal B. The advice holds only for cell-constant B.
- [x] The `Bathymetry2D::linearize()` doc says "linear B is well-balanced", which is false at p = 1 (0.07 m/s² measured).
  - Doc corrected (p ≥ 2 only, face jumps need `with_well_balanced`, split form balanced for any B); `test_lake_at_rest_linearized_bathymetry`.

### P0.15 Truncated tidal constituent periods (MAJOR, tides/analysis) — FIXED: [PR #2](https://github.com/EmilLindfors/roms-rs/pull/2)
- [x] **The bug.** `TidalConstituent::{m2,k1,o1,n2,p1}` (`boundary/tidal.rs:76-113`) use 12.42 / 23.93 / 25.82 / 12.66 / 24.07 h. They feed `HarmonicAnalysis::standard()`/`norwegian_coast()` and the harmonic BCs.
  - Phase error on a 60-day fit: ~1° (M2) to ~7° (P1); >10° over a year.
- [x] **Fix.** Use `constituent_period()` (`constituent_reader.rs:156`) or Doodson speeds.
- [x] **Test.** Fit a *true-frequency* synthetic signal; the phase error must be < 0.1°. The current round-trips synthesise with the same truncated periods.

### P0.16 3D vertical momentum advection too large by a factor of D (BLOCKER, 3D) — FIXED: [PR #2](https://github.com/EmilLindfors/roms-rs/pull/2)
- [x] `apply_vertical_advection_field` divides the Ω·u flux difference by Δσ (`advection_3d.rs:641`). Ω is in m/s, so the divisor must be Hz = D·Δσ; the tracer version (`:688`) already does this.
- [x] Test: a column with known linear Ω and u(σ) at D = 200 m, compared analytically.
- [PR #2](https://github.com/EmilLindfors/roms-rs/pull/2) also moves Ω to w-points (`n_levels + 1` interface values), which covers the w-point item in P4.2.

### P0.17 UNESCO EOS pressure units (MEDIUM, latent) — FIXED: [PR #2](https://github.com/EmilLindfors/roms-rs/pull/2)
- [x] `EquationOfState::density`/`secant_bulk_modulus` (`equation_of_state.rs:144-160,281-307`) feed dbar into the bar-based formula. ρ(5 °C, 35, 1000 dbar) ≈ 1069.5 instead of ≈ 1032.3. Convert p_bar = p_dbar / 10.
- [x] Test against the UNESCO check values (ρ(35, 25 °C, 1000 bar) = 1062.53817; ρ(35, 5 °C, 0) = 1027.67547).

### P0.18 Chezy friction units (MEDIUM, 2D + 1D) — FIXED: [PR #2](https://github.com/EmilLindfors/roms-rs/pull/2)
- [x] `ChezyFriction2D` (`friction.rs:200-239`) and the 1D Chezy compute −C_D|u|u/h with dimensionless C_D. The momentum source is −C_D|u|u (currently 50× too weak in 50 m of water).
- [x] Test the magnitude, not just the sign.

### P0.19 Limiter plumbing (MAJOR, 2D) — FIXED: [PR #11](https://github.com/EmilLindfors/roms-rs/pull/11)
- [x] The parallel fused Kuzmin+positivity limiter (`limiters/swe_2d.rs:686-703`) lacks the serial dry-element branch (`:118-129`): dry cells keep momentum and negative means survive. Add the branch plus a serial-vs-parallel equivalence test with dry cells.
  - Done: serial and parallel limiters now run the same per-element kernels (`positivity_limit_element`, `kuzmin_limit_element`), in place (no `to_vec`/copy-back), and agree bitwise. `StandardLimiter2D` uses the fused kernel and the parallel versions under `parallel`.
- [x] The high-level `Simulation` API limits once per step, not per RK stage (`runner.rs:286-287`, `builder.rs:115-125`). That voids the Zhang–Shu guarantee; move limiting into the stage hook.
  - Done: `step_with_stage_hook` is now a `TimeIntegrator` method (`step` = no-op hook) and `Simulation` passes `post_process` as the hook.

### P0.20 NorKyst nesting time base and ingest (BLOCKER, nesting) — FIXED
- [x] `read_time` returns raw values and `find_bracket` clamps out-of-range times (`netcdf_io.rs:1226-1236`), so the forcing freezes at the first snapshot. Parse CF `units` and add an epoch/offset to `OceanNestingBC2D`. Error on out-of-range times.
  - Done: `OceanModelReader::time` is Unix seconds from CF `units`/`calendar` (missing units, non-Gregorian calendars and non-increasing times are errors). Time interpolation no longer clamps. `OceanNestingBC2D::with_epoch` (default: first snapshot), `check_time_coverage`, and a panic in `ghost_state` outside the file.
- [x] Remove `"h"` (bathymetry) from the SSH candidate names (`netcdf_io.rs:1068`).
- [x] `read_variable` tries i16 before f32 (`:1258`), so unpacked float fields are truncated to integers. Read by declared type.
  - Done: branches on `vartype()`, reads numerics as f64, CF unpacking with `_FillValue`/`missing_value` (netCDF default integer fills when absent).

### P0.21 Time step (CRITICAL for cost, 2D) — FIXED: [PR #11](https://github.com/EmilLindfors/roms-rs/pull/11)
- [x] `compute_dt_swe_2d` (`swe_2d.rs:582-602`, parallel `:620-650`) pairs the global minimum element size with the global maximum wave speed. Use min over elements of hₖ/λₖ with a direction-aware (anisotropic) length.
  - Done: per node, Δt = CFL/(2N+1)·4/(λ_r + λ_s) with λ_r = |u·∇r| + c|∇r|; unchanged on squares at rest. Serial and parallel share one kernel.
- [x] `froya_real_data.rs:163` uses CFL = 0.1 where ~0.5 is stable (5× cost). Also respect the DGSEM positivity bound for wet/dry runs (CFL ≤ 0.75/0.42/0.29/0.23 for N = 1–4 in current units).
  - Done: `positivity_cfl_swe_2d(N)`; with the directional dt the bound holds for any element shape. Froya now runs at it (5/12 at P2). Not yet enforced automatically by the steppers.

### P0.22 3D tracer flux leaks through coastal walls (MAJOR, 3D; P0.7 follow-up) — FIXED: [PR #12](https://github.com/EmilLindfors/roms-rs/pull/12)
- [x] The tracer kernel sets `u_ext = u_int` at physical boundaries (`advection_3d.rs:489-491`), so heat and salt cross the coastline while the mass flux is zero. Use `boundary::reflect_velocity` as for Ω and momentum.
  - Done: reflected velocity **and** the interior tracer/Hz at the ghost, so the Rusanov tracer flux is exactly zero like the volume flux (a ghost value from the BC would still leak through the dissipation term). Test: `horizontal_tracer_advection_closed_basin_conserves_inventory` (was −0.39 of the advective scale).
  - Consequence: `TracerBoundaryCondition3D` (`Hydrostatic3D::temp_bc/salt_bc`) is no longer consulted. It comes back with 3D open boundaries (P4.2).

### P0.11 Burn GPU RHS is physically wrong (BLOCKER, GPU) — DEFERRED
Decision (2026-07-09): keep `src/solver/burn/` behind the `burn` feature for now (burn-cuda is the stated GPU target).
- [ ] `src/solver/burn/rhs.rs` computes the HLL flux, then discards it. There is no surface term, so it is not solving the SWE.
- [ ] Implement the surface term plus a burn-vs-CPU equivalence test before trusting any GPU result.
- See P2.7: the review recommends custom fused f64 kernels over CSR faces rather than Burn tensor ops with fusion disabled (`REVIEW.md` §5.2).

---

## Priority 1: A Correct 2D Barotropic Tide Model (`REVIEW.md` §1, §4, §7 Phase A)

### P1.1 Non-allocating RHS and one kernel — DONE for the `Simulation` path
- [x] `compute_rhs_into(&self, state, t, out: &mut S)` plus a workspace-owning integrator. Today the production stepper allocates ~33 full-field arrays per step (`REVIEW.md` §5.3).
  - Done: `PhysicsModule::compute_rhs_into`, `TimeIntegrator::step_with_workspace` + `StageWorkspace` (the only method an integrator implements), buffer-reusing `clone_from` for the SoA solutions. A warm `Simulation` step allocates 0 B (`tests/allocation_test.rs`; was 1.3 MB per step at 24² P3).
- [x] Unify the serial (`swe_2d.rs:318-563`) and parallel (`:775-1110`) RHS into one per-element kernel. `iter` vs `par_iter` should be the only difference.
  - Done: `SWE2DRhsKernel`; bitwise serial = parallel; per-thread cached, padded scratch. Parallel RHS 1.2–1.7× faster with `_into` (false sharing between cached workspaces cost ~25 % until padded).
- [x] Route the `Simulation`/`SWEPhysics2D` path through the parallel kernel (`builder.rs:99`).
- [ ] Remaining allocations: the limiters allocate the cell-average and vertex-bound `Vec`s every stage (P2.3); the 2D tracer's BR1 diffusion allocates its whole-mesh arrays per RHS and runs serially (`tracer_2d.rs`; port it onto the per-element `br1_gradient_element`/`br1_diffusion_element` as the SWE viscosity was, 2026-09-28); the legacy `ssp_rk3_swe_2d` stepper used by the examples still clones stages (P6: move the examples to `Simulation`); `DGSolution1D`/`DGSolution2D`/`TracerSolution2D`/`Solution3D` use the allocating default `clone_from`, and `Simulation3D`/mode splitting do not use the stage workspace (P4.1).
- [ ] Use the new split to specialise hot paths without duplicating them again: e.g. a `const N` (order) generic kernel for the fixed-size matrix products, and skipping the `reference_to_physical` + `SourceContext2D` build for sources that need neither (P2.2).

### P1.2 Well-balanced, entropy-stable, positivity-preserving DGSEM
- [x] Flux-differencing volume term with the Wintermeyer et al. (2017) two-point flux and the g·hᵢ(DB)ᵢ term. It is exactly well-balanced for any nodal B (including face-discontinuous B), conservative, and fixes GLL aliasing.
  - Done ([PR #5](https://github.com/EmilLindfors/roms-rs/pull/5)): opt-in via `SWEFormulation2D::EntropyStable` (`EntropyConservative` for verification). Exact mass conservation, entropy conserved/dissipated to round-off, P2/P3 convergence 3.06/4.00 on a nonlinear manufactured solution with bathymetry.
  - Follow-up done: `examples/froya_real_data.rs` uses nodal B and `WetDry`.
- [x] Wet/dry and positivity: Zhang–Shu towards h ≥ 0 (not h_min), Kurganov–Petrova velocity desingularization below `h_dry` (1 mm). Done for the collocated (`Standard`) formulation (`solver/algorithms/wetting_drying.rs`, `limiters/swe_2d.rs`). Gates in `tests/wet_dry_2d_test.rs`: Ritter dry dam break (L1 1.5 %, first order), Thacker's paraboloid (L1 7.4 % after one period, was 9.2 %), shoreline lake at rest (|u| < 0.06 m/s where h > 1 cm, was up to the 20 m/s cap). All conserve mass to round-off with h ≥ 0.
- [x] **Shoreline well-balancing and wet/dry for the split form: `SWEFormulation2D::WetDry`.** HLL on Audusse-reconstructed states at faces, flux differencing in wet elements, and subcell finite volumes with the same flux in partially dry elements.
  - A shoreline lake at rest is kept to round-off (P1–P4, smooth/rough beds; the P1.7 gate `lake_at_rest_with_shoreline_is_exact` passes).
  - Fully wet it keeps N+1 (P2 3.06, P3 4.02) and dissipates energy.
  - `Standard` is still not balanced in partially dry elements: on a 2 % beach it settles into a stationary circulation of ≈ 5 cm/s where h > 1 cm, and 1–4 m/s in the film.
- [x] **`WetDry` accuracy on moving shorelines.** Every element the shoreline crosses uses subcell FV, which was first order: 4.8 % on Thacker (P2, 40²) against 1.3 % for `Standard`.
  - Done: a second-order subcell reconstruction of h, η, u, v. It uses a monotonized-central limiter on the non-uniform GLL subcells, end-subcell slopes from the adjacent element, and the Audusse second-order bed term.
    - It stays well-balanced at shorelines and keeps h ≥ 0 at faces.
    - With subcells forced everywhere it converges at second order (P1–P3 ≈ 1.9).
    - Thacker at 40²: P1 3.2 %, P2 1.2 %, P3 0.9 %, P4 0.7 %, against `Standard` 10 / 1.3 / 1.8 / 2.3 %.
  - Also fixed: HLL returns zero, pressure included, when both reconstructed depths are below `h_min`; the hydrostatic correction now supplies all of ½g h².
  - [ ] End subcells on physical boundaries (no neighbour element) stay first order. Take the outer slope neighbour from the boundary condition (mirror the interior node for walls). (Note 2026-09-28: a mirrored node makes h, η and the tangential velocity even across the wall, so the MC limiter gives them zero slope and only the normal velocity gains a slope; η stays first order, and with Coriolis ∂η/∂n = −f u_t/g ≠ 0 at a wall. One-sided slopes (node 0 to node 1, bounded because the subcell face lies between them) would be second order but break the balance argument at a dry end node, whose face depth must stay zero: use them only where the end node and its neighbour are wet.)
  - [ ] `outer_nodes` scales the neighbour's node distance by the affine element heights normal to the face. Revisit with per-node isoparametric factors (P1.3).
  - [ ] Any node below `h_dry` switches the whole element to subcells. Convex DG/FV blending (Hennemann–Gassner) could keep DG in the wet part, but it needs a DG part that is balanced with dry nodes present. Lower priority now that the subcells are second order.
  - [x] `WetDry` is now the default for wet/dry runs in `SWEPhysics2DBuilder`. `Standard` stays available via `with_formulation`, and `SWE2DRhsConfig` still defaults to it.
  - [ ] Retire the `Standard` wet/dry path (hydrostatic reconstruction + `BathymetrySource2D` in partially dry elements) together with the cell-average workarounds (below), once the legacy `ssp_rk3_swe_2d` users (Frøya, P6) are on `Simulation`.
- [ ] Guide the choice of `h_dry`. It should be about 1e-4 of the characteristic depth: the 1 mm default suits field scale, but it dominates the error of the 0.1 m Thacker case (7.4 % vs 4.5 %). Consider deriving it from the bathymetry range, or making it relative.
- [ ] The Chen–Noelle (2017) reconstruction instead of Audusse. It is more accurate for bed steps larger than the depth (thin films on steep subcell beds, e.g. fjord walls); it changes only B* and h* (`b* = min(max B, min η)`, `h* = min(η − b*, h)`).
- [ ] Dry fronts lag: in the Ritter test the h = 1 mm front is at 71.5 m instead of 79.8 m (P2, 1 m elements; the same on `main`). It moves only to 73 m at 0.5 m resolution, or 75 m with h_dry = 1e-5. The thin-layer relaxation plays no role. Suspects: strict Kuzmin at the front, and zeroing the momentum of elements with mean < h_dry. Revisit with η-based limiting.
- [ ] Positivity alone does not control a dry front: without a slope limiter, P1/P2 dam breaks shed a thin film that runs ahead at the 20 m/s velocity cap. Partially dry cells need limiting (next item).
- [ ] Limit η (and velocities or characteristic variables), not h, with a troubled-cell indicator (TVB-M/KXRCF). Limit h only in partially dry cells (Vater et al. 2019).
  - Today, strict Kuzmin on h with cell-average B collapses sloping elements to P0 on every flood/ebb half-cycle.
- [x] The positivity CFL is enforced: `PhysicsModule::max_cfl`, applied by `Simulation`; `SWEPhysics2D` reports `positivity_cfl_swe_2d(N)` when it has wetting/drying. Roe with wetting/drying prints a warning at build time.
- [x] Negative-mean elements are counted, not hidden: the positivity limiters and wet/dry correction return the count; `SWEPhysics2D::negative_depth_clips`.
- [x] Point-implicit friction in every RK stage (`TimeIntegrator::step_with_relaxation`, `BottomFriction2D`, `SWEPhysics2DBuilder::with_implicit_friction`). It keeps friction balances exact for any dt, is L-stable, and is first order in the friction term (13–32 % above the exact decay at dt·Λ ≈ 3; explicit flips the sign there).
  - [x] `SpatiallyVaryingManning2D` did not implement `BottomFriction2D`: the rate needs the node position. Done 2026-09-28: it holds g·n² per node (`new` from n(x, y) at the nodes, `from_nodal` from values), `damping_rate` takes the node index, and the damping checks the field against the mesh. Its explicit source goes through `add_element` only (`evaluate` has no node index and panics).
    - [ ] A roughness map from data (sediment type or a calibrated n per region, e.g. lower n over deep rock, higher over tidal flats), projected onto the nodes like the bed (`Bathymetry2D::project`). Frøya runs n = 0.025 everywhere, and friction is one of the phase suspects at Mausund (P3.1).
  - [ ] A second-order IMEX treatment (e.g. an SSP IMEX-RK) if the friction term's first-order error matters in shallow tidal flats.
- [x] The dt-dependent wet/dry momentum damping is gone. It is replaced by the point-implicit thin-layer relaxation r(h) = (h_dry/h − 1)²/τ, which is zero at h ≥ h_dry.
- [x] HLL is the default flux for wet/dry runs in `SWEPhysics2DBuilder`. `SWE2DRhsConfig::new` still defaults to Roe (low-level API).
- [ ] Retire the cell-average/`linearize` workarounds: `WetDry` now covers wet/dry with nodal B.
- [x] `SWEPhysics2DBuilder` footgun (found 2026-09-30): `with_bathymetry` without wet/dry defaults to `Standard`, which gets the bed-slope force only from an explicit `BathymetrySource2D`. Without it a sloping bed pushes nothing: a lake at rest over a linear 20→10 m slope reaches η = +10 m at the shallow end within an hour (P2, 30 km channel). Default to `EntropyStable` when a bed is set and no source carries the slope, or refuse to build.
  - Done 2026-09-30: both. Without wet/dry, a bed that is not flat now defaults to `EntropyStable` unless a source carries the slope; an explicit `Standard` over such a bed without `BathymetrySource2D` panics at `build`. The channel stays at rest to round-off (`a_sloping_bed_is_balanced_by_default`). No example changes (the only affected one, `minimal_3d`, has a flat bed). One test relied on the trap: `several_levels_conserve_mass` (LTS) got its third level from the spurious flow; with the slope applied (either formulation) it has two.
  - [ ] The low-level `SWE2DRhsConfig::new` still defaults to `Standard` with no check; a `with_bathymetry` there could warn the same way.
- [x] Move `examples/froya_real_data.rs` off the legacy `SWE2DTimeConfig` (`H_MIN` = 5 m, land as a 5 m film, cell-averaged B). It now uses `Simulation` + `WetDry` (nodal B) + `with_wet_dry` + `with_implicit_friction` on a water-only mesh, with all four sides open.
- [x] `SWEPhysics2D::post_process` built a `LimiterContext2D` every stage, whose `new` computed `mesh.h_min()` serially (a sqrt per edge). No limiter read it: the field is gone (2026-09-26).

### P1.3 Geometry for real coastlines
- [x] Per-node isoparametric geometric factors (`[K]` → `[K × n_nodes]`), parallelogram-only panic removed (2026-09-26). `GeometricFactors2D::compute(mesh, ops)` evaluates the bilinear map at every node: metric, J, mass weights w·J, and per face node the normal and sJ from the same contravariant vectors (`sJ n = ±J∇r, ±J∇s`), so the discrete metric identities hold to round-off.
  - Kernels: the collocated SWE, tracer, advection and BR1 diffusion volume terms are in conservative form `J⁻¹[Dr(J∇r·F) + Ds(J∇s·F)]` with the per-node lift scale sJ/J; the split forms use the curvilinear Wintermeyer et al. (2017) form (`{{Ja}}` metric averages, bed term included); the `WetDry` subcells use the telescoping interface metrics (Fisher et al. 2013; Hennemann et al. 2021) plus a metric balance term in the bed source (zero on parallelograms and flat beds). Limiter, wet/dry and diagnostic means are mass-weighted (`LimiterContext2D` and the limiter / wet-dry functions take `&GeometricFactors2D`).
  - Gates on distorted periodic meshes (`tests/curvilinear_2d_test.rs`): free stream, lake at rest over rough and shoreline beds, mass/momentum/tracer conservation, entropy; convergence N+1 for advection and the split forms, 2 for the subcells (`convergence_test.rs`, ACCURACY.md).
  - Parallelograms take a fast path from a dense per-element copy of the node-0 geometry (`ElementGeometry`): the per-node fields are strided and cost ≈ 9 cache lines per element. Frøya (all parallelograms, `profile=80`, mains power, CPUs 0–7, five alternating runs, min/median): RHS 1 thread 4.44/4.51 ms against 4.36/4.38 on `main` (+2–3 %), 24 threads 1.22/1.26 against 1.17/1.20 (+4–5 %); `dt` 0.246/0.251 against 0.259/0.262 (−5 %); step at 24 threads +4–5 %. Before the fast paths the RHS was ≈ 10–20 % slower. [ ] Find the remaining few percent with a profiler (AMD uProf is installed).
  - [x] The 3D kernels on general quadrilaterals (2026-10-03, `tests/curvilinear_3d_test.rs`). `Hydrostatic3D::new` no longer asserts parallelograms.
    - The PGF takes the metric per node and per face node, the 3D limiters weight with `w_i J_i`, and the 3D step takes each node's length (now the narrow side of elongated elements, as in 2D: 10:1 rectangles get 1.74× shorter steps).
    - The 3D divergences take the 2D module's metric form (`MetricForm`): the split forms' mass flux is `{{J∇r}}·{{hu}}`, not the collocated `D(J∇r·hu)`. Without the match the splitter's nodal identity failed everywhere, and a distorted seamount at rest jumped to 0.9 m/s. The PGF takes the averaged metric as the advection's adjoint.
    - Gates: rest, exactness, constancy, inventories, momentum, PGF and advection convergence (see CHANGELOG).
  - [ ] `batched` SIMD and the Burn prototype still use one metric per element (`affine_metric`); `compute_volume_terms_batched` and `BurnGeometricFactors2D::from_cpu` assert parallelograms.
  - [ ] Run the Frøya coastline mesh (`data/froya_coast.msh`) in 3D: a stratified spin-up at rest, then a tide. Measured 2026-10-03 with `froya_real_data mesh=data/froya_coast.msh levels=20` (GLS, 20 stretched levels, P2, 2.18 M points, 24 threads):
    - Cost: 1.06 s per step, of which the barotropic pass (35 substeps) ≈ 0.25 s and the 3D stages ≈ 0.8 s (≈ 370 ns per point per step). The step is 5.3 s at rest (internal waves on the smallest quads) and ≈ 0.8 s with 1 m/s currents: ≈ 12 min per model hour at rest and ≈ 75 min with tidal currents, so 15 days take ≈ 3–18 days of wall time. The elements' own steps would save 4–5.5× with local time stepping in 3D; the 2D run takes ≈ 38 s per model hour.
    - **Blocker, before any optimisation: a stratified fluid at rest did not stay at rest on the real bed**, on the coastline mesh and on the structured 500 m grid alike (so not the general-quad change). Unstratified water holds 1e-12 m/s. Diagnose with `froya_real_data levels=20 debug_3d=trace[,linear,uniform,balanced,unbalanced,nolimiter,constant] [dt_3d=]`.
      - [x] Fixed 2026-10-03: shoreline elements flattened the stratification. Elements the 2D pass balances only as a whole carried their tracers as the mean per level at every node. Now each node keeps its departure from the mean (`from_inventory`; gate `a_steep_stratified_shore_keeps_its_stratification`). Linear N² at rest without diffusion: 1e-13 m/s on the coastline mesh (it jumped to 0.3 m/s). With GLS: 8.5e-4 m/s after 1.2 h, mixing at the cliffs.
      - [x] Done 2026-10-03: `Hydrostatic3D::with_reference_profile` (Mellor et al. 1998's reference-profile subtraction, `BalancedReference::from_profile`). The force at rest of the summer pycnocline on the coastline mesh drops from 2.2e-2 m/s² (σ-pairs) and 1.7e-4 (state reference, the constant-depth form's error at the cliff shores) to 6.5e-7.
      - [ ] **Still open: an instability of the stratified rest state at steep elements.** Reproduced in 90 s on the 1 km grid (`froya_real_data nx=60 ny=45 levels=20 steps_3d=400 debug_3d=trace[,…]`):
        - Pycnocline, profile reference, GLS and limiter: 6e-4 m/s at 0.7 h, 0.06 at 2 h, 0.36 at 3 h. On the coastline mesh the same within 0.3–0.6 h. Constant mixing without the limiter: round-off for 3.5 h, then 1e-6 → 3e-2 m/s in 3.5 h. The growth is exponential, e-folding ≈ 15–17 min, in every variant. GLS and the limiter only seed it earlier. Linear N² with GLS and the limiter grows too, more slowly (1.5e-3 m/s at 4.3 h); with constant mixing and no limiter it does not (7e-8 m/s after 8 h).
        - **Diagnosed 2026-10-03: hydrostatic inconsistency within elements, not the thin columns.**
          - It is in the semi-discrete scheme: dt 73.7, 36 and 18 s grow at the same rate. The first elements to grow are fully wet, e.g. element 335, 304 m beside 14 m within one element, where the bottom σ-level crosses the pycnocline between nodes.
          - Not the cause (each varied alone): the discontinuous projected bed (`bed=point` grows too); the vertical exchange (centred vertical tracer advection with a slope-free trapezoidal pressure integral, the exact energy partner, grows the same); viscosity (`nu=10` changes nothing; `nu=50` slows it and adds a steady 1.6e-2 m/s at a 0.3 m node); Coriolis (`f = 0` grows in the slice below).
          - It needs a curved profile, and an x–z slice reproduces it, in two gates (≈ 2 s each in the ci profile): `constant_stratification_over_a_cliff_stays_at_rest` (1.8e-11 m/s after 6 h) and `a_pycnocline_over_a_cliff_stays_at_rest` (ignored, failing: 2.4e-8 m/s after 6 h, e-folding ≈ 45 min). The slice is 8 P2 elements of 1 km, with the bed falling from 15 to 300 m inside one of them.
          - Mechanism. The split form exchanges potential energy with the σ-pairs force exactly for any ρ, and it also conserves the tracer variance `Σ H_z ρ²`. For a linear `ρ̄(z)`, energy plus `g/(2|∂_z ρ̄|)` times the variance has its minimum at rest (an energy–Casimir argument), so rest is stable. For a curved profile no conserved functional has a minimum there. The linearised exchange would need each node's weight `1/|∂_z ρ̄|` to equal `Δz/Δρ̄` for every pair the node is in. Where a σ-level crosses the pycnocline between two nodes of one element, the pair's chord is far steeper than the stratification at either node.
          - `rx0` smoothing helps only partly. `rx0=0.2 rx0_min_depth=0.5` gives 1e-2 m/s at 2.4 h, against 1.6e-1 without. Cliffs at the shore remain, since r_x0 against a dry node is 1 by definition. In that run a shoreline element with one wet node (68.6 m deep) grew too: wet–dry pairs exchange density in the advection with no PGF partner, a second leak.
        - **Tried: an entropy-conservative pair density.** For `C′(ρ) = −g Z(ρ)`, with `Z` the reference profile's height of density ρ, Tadmor's two-point flux is the reference's mean density between the two densities' heights. Both the σ-pairs force and the split-form advection of T and S used it (`T* = {{T}} + θ[[T]]`).
          - In theory it makes rest a constrained energy minimum for any stable profile, and the force is exact at rest.
          - It holds the slice for 30 h. On Frøya it blows up within 0.1 h at dt 36 s. At dt 4 s it still grows, e-folding ≈ 150 s, at a shoreline element with dry nodes.
          - It is stiff: `∂ρ*/∂ρ_i` is the chord over the local gradient, up to ≈ 100 where a pair straddles the pycnocline closely over weakly stratified water (effective wave speeds ≈ 10 m/s against 0.5).
          - Prototype on the local branch `exp/reference-pair-density` (`DGRS_EC=1`).
        - **Followed up 2026-10-04: the pair density with a wet-side rule and a regularised reference. It does not hold the production setup.** Local branch `exp/pair-density-wet-dry`; its ignored `probe_beach` runs the slices.
          - Wet-side rule: a pair with a thin column carries the wet column's tracers (θ = ±½), so the wet node gets no anomaly from pairs the PGF ignores.
          - Without the pair density, wet–dry pairs alone are harmless: a steep shore to land under linear N² holds 1e-11 m/s. Under the pycnocline it is worse than the same cliff ending at a wall: 4.1e-7 against 2.8e-8 m/s after 6 h.
          - With the pair density: the cliff and the wall hold 1e-10 m/s for 48 h. The cliff to land grows with e-folding ≈ 9 min without the wet-side rule and ≈ 5.7 h with it.
          - The shoreline element there is not marked for element means (`from_inventory` is not involved). Left over: the wet node's layer volume exchange with the dry node, which has no pressure-work partner in the PGF.
          - Frøya 1 km, at the 36 s step:
            - The default thin depth (0.1 m) blows up, from pairs of shallow wet nodes (0.2–0.4 m) beside 50–100 m ones: ∂ρ*/∂ρ ≈ 50.
            - `thin=2` needs dt ≤ 12 s.
            - A minimum reference gradient of 0.01 kg/m⁴ (`DGRS_EC_MIN`) caps the stiffness: `thin=2` then holds 5e-9 m/s for 6 h with constant mixing and no limiter. After that, shoreline elements with dry nodes (553, 554) grow.
            - At 0.05 kg/m⁴ the reference no longer matches the deep water, and element 335 grows at the old rate.
            - With GLS and the limiter, every variant reaches 0.5–0.9 m/s within 1.5–3 h, worse than the unchanged scheme (0.16 m/s at 2.4 h).
        - **Also tried 2026-10-04: horizontal diffusion of T and S about the reference profile** (ROMS `TS_MIX_CLIMA`; local branch `exp/anomaly-diffusion`, `with_tracer_anomaly_diffusion`). It is zero at rest and conservative, and it slows the growth without stopping it. On the 15 → 300 m cliff (1 km P2 elements) the e-folding is 38 min at κ = 0, 50 min at 10, 1.6 h at 30 and ≈ 4.4 h at 100 m²/s. So the growing mode is not a grid-scale anomaly that a Laplacian removes cheaply.
          - The constant-depth PGF on the same cliff is worse: 1e-2 m/s at 2 h, NaN by 12 h.
          - Bed slope: in the slices, a beach of slope 0.005 (≈ 5 m of depth change per 1 km element against a pycnocline ≈ 8 m thick) stays at rest. A 15 → 60 m step over one element grows with e-folding ≈ 1.7 h.
        - **Literature and analysis 2026-10-04: `docs/stratified-rest-over-steep-beds.md`.** In short:
          - The literature knows the instability. Beckmann & Haidvogel 1993 found z-interpolated PGFs less stable, as the constant-depth form is here. Zängl 2012: unstable when neighbours' heights differ by much more than the layer spacing. Shepherd 1993: energy alone is not enough, rest needs a Casimir (the APE). Ricardo, Duru & Lee 2024 saw the same failure in thermal shallow water.
          - Operational answers are geometric: NorKyst smooths to rx0 0.34 with Haney ≈ 6 and keeps levels near the surface; SCHISM LSC² uses z-based gradients on per-node grids; multi-envelope levels follow smoothed virtual bottoms; ROMS wet–dry masks the flux and the PGF together.
          - With pairs by level index, conservation and exact energy, a stable rest forces the pair density's linearisation, so its stiffness (≈ chord/2G: up to 65 at the slice's cliff, half its levels > 10) is unavoidable. An entropy-*stable* pair (clipped toward upwind) equals `ρ*` at Q = 0, so it is stiff too, unless discontinuous in Q (Coulomb friction ≈ 3e-3 m/s² at the cliff).
          - Anomaly diffusion can feed the APE across a straddling pair (two nodes at G = 0.25 and 4e-4 kg/m⁴: `sym(G⁻¹L)` has an eigenvalue of +516). That explains why it only slowed the growth.
        - **Tried 2026-10-04: the pair density at steps small enough for its stiffness, with GLS. It fails at the shorelines.** Frøya 1 km, 20 levels:
          - `thin=2`, 8 s step, GLS and the limiter: 0.32 m/s at 0.5 h. Without the limiter: 0.70 m/s at 0.63 h.
          - `thin=0.1`, 4 s step, GLS and the limiter: 0.16 m/s at 0.31 h.
          - Every run grows in shoreline elements with dry nodes beside 40–135 m ones (553, 393, 77). That is far faster than the unchanged scheme (6e-4 m/s at 0.7 h, first at the fully wet element 335).
          - Also: the limiter alone changes the rest state's force from 1e-9 to 3e-6 m/s² at the first step.
        - **Tried 2026-10-04: a pressure-work partner for wet–dry pairs (`DGRS_DRYBANK=1` on `exp/pair-density-wet-dry`): worse.** It is the σ-pairs difference with the wet column's density, matching the wet-side rule. On the cliff-to-land slice the e-folding drops from ≈ 5.8 h to ≈ 3.1 h. A dry node holds no volume, so the wet node's exchange with it ends in its own Ω, whose pressure work no horizontal PGF term can balance. The flux has to be blocked instead, in the 2D `WetDry` mass flux and the 3D layers alike, as ROMS does.

It is volume-preserving, and the coastline does not move (calibration in the CHANGELOG).

        - **Done 2026-10-04: 3D wetting and drying on the 2D kernel's subcells** (see CHANGELOG). The layers of a `WetDry` subcell element move through the 2D kernel's subcell interfaces (its hydrostatic reconstruction closes a dry bank), the PGF's volume term there is their adjoint, and a thin column exchanges no baroclinic transport across faces. Masking the shore elements' PGF first confirmed the leak: the exponential growth stopped.

          - Frøya 1 km at rest with `slopes3d=on` (5511 nodes changed, volume kept): constant mixing without the limiter holds 5.8e-10 m/s for 24 h. With GLS and the limiter: 1.1e-3 m/s at 8 h, 4.6e-3 at 16 h, 1.1e-2 at 24 h, at the bed of shallow elements (the mixing at the slopes).
          - Coastline mesh with GLS (23k nodes changed by up to 210 m): ≈ 1e-3 m/s per hour for 1.5 h, then 8e-3 m/s at 2 h in shore element 11223 (0.2–0.6 m beside 28.5 m and dry nodes) at a wetting front. Before: 0.16 m/s within 15 min.

              - The mode is real and non-oscillating: dumps 1 h apart correlate to 0.997, e-folding 34.5 min. The time step does not change it (half: 34.6 min), nor does the stratification below the pycnocline (×½, ×2: 34.4–34.8). It is grid-scale at one terrace-edge vertex (30.6 m beside 23 m) shared by shore (subcell) element 91 and its wet neighbours 90 and 92, in bed layers 0–2 crossing the pycnocline's lower flank, with δρ alternating from level to level.
              - Seeded, the e-folding by vertical tracer scheme: upwind 26–36 min, TVD 29, LimitedAkima (the default) 34.6, Akima ≈ 50, Hermite mean (the exact energy partner of the PGF's pressure integral) ≈ 75 (transient: the spectrum below has nothing faster than 7.2 h), centred none (≥ 10 h). From round-off, centred and the Hermite mean grow nothing exponential in 8.8 h (1e-9 m/s).
              - Why: at rest Ω̄ = 0, so to first order a scheme acts only through the surface value it gives the *background* times the perturbation's δΩ. Upwind adds `½|δΩ|ΔC̄`, mixing the background at the rate of the perturbation's own vertical flow: flow → mixing → density anomaly → PGF → flow, a feedback with no continuum counterpart. The limiters clip at the pycnocline's foot and act partly the same way. Akima moves the value off the mean by a curvature term and grows (an oscillatory pair); centred and the exact energy partner (the Hermite mean, half Akima's term) both hold.

            - [x] The reconstruction about a reference is a real option (2026-10-04): `with_vertical_reference(state)`, `with_vertical_reference_profile(state, |z| (T, S))`, `set_vertical_reference`, `with_vertical_reference_timescale(τ)` (relaxed towards the state every `Simulation3D` step; `relax_vertical_reference` for hand-written loops).
              - The form: `C_s = V(C)_s − ψ[V(C_ref)_s − ½(C_ref,l−1 + C_ref,l)]`, `ψ = clamp(ΔC/ΔC_ref, 0, 1)`, the correction moving the value towards the column's centred one and never past it. Centred at the reference, uniform tracers stay uniform (`ψ = 0`), a column at a fraction of the reference's stratification is exactly centred, and fronts beyond the reference keep the limiter. The prototype `centred(C_ref) + V(C − C_ref)` broke constancy (a mixed column under a stratified reference, restratified by `½|Ω|ΔC_ref`).

With the 3D viscosity's mirrored walls and extrapolated open faces, the operator had eigenvalues with positive real parts (+1.1e-2 /s on the terrace fixture at ν = 10, +3.4e-3 /s at open faces of a small distorted basin). The kernel now takes a boundary flux matching the exterior state, `F* = ½(I − R)(F·n)` for an exterior state `R u`: at a mirrored wall the normal stress only, at a zero-gradient face none. The largest real part is now round-off (≤ 3e-16 /s, ν = 10 and 50), and the patch's seeded mode decays under ν = 10 and 50. Gates: `shear_viscosity_is_dissipative_at_walls_and_open_faces`, `shear_viscosity_is_dissipative_on_the_coastline_fixture`. The 2D viscosity takes the same wall flux (its open boundaries keep the interior flux, matching a prescribed exterior state).

          - (b) The pair density with a cap on its stiffness, or the stiff pairs implicit.
          - (c) A geopotential (rotated) tracer diffusion, strong enough at cliffs: the growth, ≈ 1e-3 /s at the 250 m node scale, needs κ ≈ 6 m²/s.
          - ~~(d) Make the wet–dry pairs consistent first.~~ Done 2026-10-04 (above): the layers move on the 2D kernel's subcells, which block a dry bank.
          - (f) Reconstruct the vertical tracer advection about a reference profile (found 2026-10-04, above): it removes the 36-min mode of the smoothed coastline mesh, but not the straddle growth of unsmoothed cliffs, which still needs (a)–(c).

          - [ ] `SigmaGrid` ignores `SongHaidvogelStretching::hc`: levels are pure σ (`z = η + σD`, `vertical/sigma.rs` `z_at_levels_into`), not ROMS's `Vtransform=2` hybrid. With `norkyst=` the same file, read with its profiles, sets the initial T and S at every node and nests u, v, T, S in the band (`Nesting3D` + `OceanModelColumns`); without it the open faces relax to the state at rest. The PGF, the limiter and the vertical reference use the domain's mean profile. GLS, log-layer drag, Smagorinsky; stations, progress lines and a 3D snapshot file as in 2D.

The 3D snapshot replays in the viewer.

      - The nesting's bed blend deepened shore nodes to NorKyst's smooth bed: a 0.4 m node beside land went to 32 m, and the cliff burst (3.9 m/s and η −1.0 to +4.2 m within 3 min, in 2D as well). Shores now keep their bed (P1.5).
      - The nesting's reference level (+0.27 m for NorKyst here) was added after the ramp, so a 0.27 m step stood at the boundary from t = 0 and flooded in as a bore (the mean level +5 cm in 3 min, a 1.4 m/s burst 2.6 km inside at 15 min). It ramps with ζ now (P1.5). 2D nested runs before this had the bore too.
      - The thin-column depth of a 3D tide is 1 m (`TIDE_3D_THIN_DEPTH`; `debug_3d=thin=`). At 0.1 m the `|Ω|/H_z` bound of columns just over it took the step from 4.6 s to 0.7 s within 20 min.
      - NorKyst's profiles held below its bed ran its bottom water down the child's deep holes (NorKyst 10–59 m over a 90–140 m hole): a bed jet of 0.4 m/s within 5 min at rest. `OceanModelColumns::with_deep_reference` (`DeepReference`): below the parent's bed, the mean profile plus the bottom anomaly decaying over 10 m; now 0.12 m/s at mid levels, which is NorKyst's own density field adjusting without its currents.
      - The mean profile per height over the columns that reach it was statically unstable in places (the set changes with depth): as the state at rest it overturned (0.26 m/s). Now pooled to stability (pool-adjacent-violators on ρ); every column at every height instead warmed the deep holes by 2 °C.

Columns deepening past 1 m bring it into the resolved water (6.1 °C there at 2.7 h). The inventory does not follow the volume in thin columns, likely the 2D positivity limiter and wet/dry correction changing nodal depths with no tracer flux (P4.2: only the element balance holds there).
