  - [x] Prescribed 3D velocity and T/S profiles at open faces, with a relaxation band. Done 2026-09-30 (`boundary::Nesting3D`, `Hydrostatic3D::with_nesting`): any `ParentColumns3D` gives the parent's u, v, T, S at the child's layer centres per node and time. At the nested tags' open faces the layer fluxes take the central average of the interior's and the parent's `H_z u` (corrected to the 2D flux) and inflow brings the parent's velocity and tracers (`Exterior3D`). Within the band (the 2D nesting's distance, `boundary::band`, shared) the shear relaxes to the parent's with no depth mean (the 2D module owns ū; nothing enters G) and T/S relax in inventory form. Gates: a uniform tracer stays uniform (3.8e-12 °C) under a nested exchange flow with the same water; a stratified exchange flow (ΔT = 4 °C) holds the boundary columns to 2.3 % of ΔT and 5.2 % of the shear after 12 h; unit tests for the face-flux profile, the inflow budgets and constancy with a parent.
    - [x] The parent's 3D fields. Done 2026-09-30: `OceanModelReader::from_file_with_profiles` keeps the levels (`io::ProfileSeries`: z-levels at their depths, ROMS s-levels at the fraction of the column of their layer centres, which does not depend on ζ with either Vtransform; grid-relative velocity rotated per level), and `boundary::OceanModelColumns` is the `ParentColumns3D` of such a reader: horizontal stencils per node on first use (wet corners, else the nearest wet point within `max_snap`), linear or cubic in time, linear in depth at the child's layer centres with the deepest valid value held below (NorKyst's 300 m), rotated to the mesh axes. Gate: the nested exchange flow read on z-levels drives the same run as the analytic parent to 1.9e-5 of ΔT (the relative η/D).
    - [ ] The relaxation is explicit (`τ` well above the baroclinic step); a point-implicit form as for the 2D band (P1.5) if FRS-like `τ ≈ Δt` is wanted.
    - [ ] Cost: every band node's parent column is evaluated at every stage time (cached per time and η): for `OceanModelColumns` four parent columns × snapshots × layers of linear searches. Precompute each node's vertical interpolation weights for z-levels (they change only with η), or the time-interpolated profiles once per baroclinic step.
    - [ ] Memory: `from_file_with_profiles` holds every snapshot of four 3D fields (a 3-day hourly 100 × 100 subset on 16 levels: 46 MB each). Months of forcing need snapshots read lazily around the current time (as for the 2D reader, P1.5).
    - [ ] `G`'s advection uses the interior's values at nested faces (both in the 3D and the one-layer mean-flow run, so unsheared flow still cancels exactly); the parent's shear dispersion at the boundary face is left out of the depth mean.
    - `TracerBoundaryCondition3D` still has no time argument; time-dependent boundary tracers go through the nesting instead.
  - [x] Baroclinic radiation: measured 2026-09-30 (`an_internal_wave_leaves_through_a_relaxed_open_boundary`). A mode-1 internal-wave pulse (0.24 °C, P2, 50 m, N = 0.02 s⁻¹, c₁ ≈ 0.32 m/s) meeting an open end:
    - **Extrapolation (open faces without nesting, as merged in P4.2's open boundaries) is unstable for stratified flow**: the pulse starts an exchange flow at the boundary that displaces its isopycnals without bound (6 °C, 0.26 m/s within 8 h). `Hydrostatic3D` now warns once when a stratified state has open 3D faces that no nesting relaxes.
    - Boundary values of the state at rest without a band: stable, reflect the pulse completely.
    - A relaxation band to the state at rest (`boundary::ReferenceColumns`, ROMS's nudging to climatology), amplitude reflected at 16 h: 2 km 27 % (30 min) / 32 % (1 h); 4 km 22 % (30 min, 1 h) / 31 % (2 h); 6 km 18 % (1 h). The gate (P1, six levels) measures 36 %.
      - **Re-measured 2026-09-30 with Akima vertical advection (P4.5): most of that was not reflection.** Upwind's mixing left a 0.2 °C anomaly along the pulses' path (the middle of the channel from hour 4 on, before anything could return from the ends); Akima leaves 0.02 °C. With Akima at 16 h: 2 km 4.9 % (30 min) / 12 % (1 h); 4 km 4.5 % (30 min) / 4.4 % (1 h) / 14 % (2 h); 6 km 2.5 % (1 h); 1 km 18 % (30 min). The gate measures 4.5 %.
    - [ ] A radiation condition that absorbs better than a sponge, and makes a band unnecessary: a characteristic (Flather-type) pressure flux at open faces per vertical mode, `p* = p_ext + ρ₀c(u − u_ext)·n` with the local mode speed, or an Orlanski-type radiation of the shear and tracers (ROMS `u3d`/`t3d` RadNud). Less pressing since the re-measurement above: bands of 2–4 km at 30 min already leave ≈ 5 %. A radiation condition would matter for narrower bands (1 km: 18 %) or long waves.
  - [x] The 3D classification must match the 2D module's boundary condition. Done 2026-09-30: `SWEBoundaryCondition2D::is_wall(tag)` (`Reflective2D` a wall; the characteristic OBC, tidal, discharge, extrapolation and fixed-state conditions open; `MultiBoundaryCondition2D` forwards by tag; `None` = cannot tell), and `Hydrostatic3D::new` derives its walls from it (`Boundaries3D::matching`). `with_wall_tags` remains as an override for conditions that cannot tell.
- [x] Step Hz·C (inventory form), then divide by the new Hz. Done 2026-09-30: `ModeSplitIntegrator` carries the tracers as `H_z C` through the 3D stages. Gates `uniform_tracers_stay_uniform_under_a_tide_over_a_sloping_bed` (drift 9.3e-13 in one period; before 12.8) and `tracer_inventories_are_conserved_under_a_tide` (3.1e-15; before 1.1e-2). The seiche/wind/Ekman gates run with the T/S-dependent EOS again (the side effect below is gone).
  - Measured side effect (2026-09-26, fixed by the above): with a T/S-dependent EOS, the drift of T and S with η fed a spurious baroclinic PGF into the mode-split G. It damped a barotropic seiche by 0.6 % per period (P1), or amplified it by 0.4 % per period (P2), independent of amplitude and dt.
- [x] Step Hz·u as well. Done 2026-09-30 (`apply_momentum_transport_3d`): `ModeSplitIntegrator` steps `H_z u`, `H_z v` as inventories with the tracers, advected by `−∇·(Q_l u↑) − δ(Ω u)` on the layer transports (upwind horizontally, centred vertically), the column sum of the stage tendency set to the rate of the barotropic transport `D̄ū`. `G` takes the same operator on the state's own layer transports (`LayerTransport::compute(state, None, …)`) minus a one-layer run on `ū`, so unsheared flow still gives exactly the stresses. Gate `layer_momentum_anomalies_are_conserved_across_a_tide` (Ω = 0 by construction, so each layer's anomaly `∫ H_z,l (u_l − ū)` is invariant): 9.6e-16 of the layer transport over a period; the velocity form drifted by 7.4e-3.
  - [ ] `G`'s advection builds its own layer transports and a one-layer mean-flow run every step (an extra `LayerTransport::compute`, two advection passes). The last stage's transports are at `tⁿ⁻¹ + Δt/2`, not `tⁿ`; if the cost shows in a profile, consider evaluating G's advection from the stage-1 transports of the previous step, extrapolated with the AB3 history as G already is.
  - [ ] `with_thin_columns_at_rest` copies the whole `Solution3D` every stage of a domain with any thin column (every realistic wet/dry run). Pass a thin-column mask into the transport kernels instead.
  - [x] `tracer_to_inventory` / `inventory_to_concentration` now convert the velocity too; rename them (e.g. `to_inventory` / `from_inventory`) when the P4.5 kernel work touches them. Done 2026-10-01 with the turbulence's advection (P4.4): `to_inventory` / `from_inventory`, taking the cells' σ-thicknesses.
- [ ] Cost of the inventory form (noted 2026-09-30): each 3D stage copies the whole stage state to convert the inventory fields (u, v, T, S since 2026-09-30) to concentrations (`Buffers::concentrations`), and `post_stage` converts back and forth. Cheap next to the 3D RHS today; with the P4.5 kernel work, have the RHS read `H_z φ` and divide on the fly instead.
  - Measured 2026-10-02 (`profile_3d`, 8192 P2 elements × 20 levels, 24 threads): ≈ 75 ms per step of copies and conversions around the 3D physics calls, ≈ 10 % of the step, before the copies were trimmed (P4.5, barotropic pass). What is left per stage: the inventory → value conversion twice (`to_values` for the RHS and in the post-stage hook, ≈ 8 ms each per step), `last` copied into the state and back (≈ 12 ms), `to_inventories` (≈ 5 ms). The post-stage hook could convert in place (value where wet, `last` where dry) instead of converting into `last` and copying.
  - [x] `Solution3D`'s SSP-RK3 stage combinations were ≈ 30 ms per step at that size: `copy_from` + `axpy` + `scale` as separate passes over every field. Done 2026-10-02: `Integrable::combine(base, a, terms)` (default: the old sequence), used by `SSPRK3` and `SSPRK43`; `Solution3D` overrides it with one cache-chunked pass per field (`blocks::combine_values`), bit for bit the sequence (gate `solution_3d_combine_matches_the_operation_sequence_bit_for_bit`). A/B, alternating: pass + splitter 25–35 ms less per step at 8192 elements in each of 4 pairs, 3–6 ms at 1024. `w` and `rho` (zero rates) are still combined; skipping them would change the bits of `w` (`¾w + ¼w ≠ w` in floating point).
    - [ ] The other `Integrable` types keep the default sequence: `SWESolution2D`'s `scale`/`axpy` are serial. The 2D `Simulation` runs SSP-RK3/SSP-RK(4,3) through `MultirateStepper`'s fused stages when the physics supports local time stepping, so only the other paths (and `Simulation3D`'s tracer types) would gain; override `combine` there if a profile shows them.
  - Measured 2026-10-01 (`examples/profile_3d.rs`, 1024 P2 elements × 20 levels, 24 threads, after the parallel kernels): of the 44 ms the 3D stages take per step, the kernels are ≈ 37 ms and the rest (≈ 7 ms) is copies and stage combinations: `concentrations.copy_from` copies all eight 3D fields where five are needed (η and the four inventory fields), and the SSP-RK3 combinations of `Solution3D` (parallel since 2026-10-01) also combine `w` and `rho`, which the stage RHS zeroes. Combine only the prognostic fields, or give the splitter its own stage state.
- [x] The `w` output of `Hydrostatic3D::post_process` was Ω of the 3D velocities alone from a separate kernel (`compute_vertical_velocity`, allocating, affine metric only). Done 2026-09-30: `Hydrostatic3D::update_vertical_velocity` takes Ω of the end state's own layer transports (`LayerTransport::compute(state, None, …)`, the stages' DG divergence), whose column sum is `Dū` = the filtered barotropic transport after a step; `compute_vertical_velocity` and `compute_strong_divergence` are gone.

### P4.3 Pressure gradient and vertical grid
- [x] Balanced PGF. Lift pressure/η jumps at element faces. Done 2026-09-30 (`solver::rhs::baroclinic`): ∇p|_z directly, as the DG derivative of pairwise pressure differences at a common depth (Stelling & van Kester 1994 in a flux-differencing form), each column's ρ a monotone Hermite cubic in z integrated exactly (the Shchepetkin & McWilliams 2003 ingredient), one-sided pairs where a node lies below the other column's bed, and the pressure jump at every face lifted with a central flux.
  - Constant N² at rest over a 30→400 m slope: round-off (≤ 1.5e-14 m/s²) at P1–P4, uniform and stretched levels; the σ form gave 1.3e-5 – 2.5e-3.
  - The review's pycnocline fjord on 30 stretched levels (θs 5): 2.0e-7 / 1.5e-6 / 3.3e-6 m/s² at P1 500 m / P3 250 m / P3 100 m, against 1.6e-3 / 1.1e-4 / 2.2e-5 for the σ form and ≈ 5e-5 of real estuarine forcing.
  - Mode-split fjord at rest (10→150 m over ≈ 400 m, linear N²): 2.8e-11 m/s after 1 h; on `main` it blew up within 16 steps.
  - Since 2026-10-01 (P4.6, the seamount) the default form is σ-pairs, consistent in energy with the split-form tracer advection; the constant-depth form above stays as `PressureGradientForm::ConstantDepth`, and its balance at rest is reached by σ-pairs with a balanced reference state. σ-pairs alone on the pycnocline fjord: 6.8e-4 / 1.5e-4 / 1.7e-5 m/s².
  - [ ] On 30 *uniform* levels the pycnocline case stays at 2–4e-4 m/s² (σ form 4e-4 – 1.6e-3): 13 m apart at 400 m, the deep columns do not sample a 3 m pycnocline. Resolve it in every column (stretched levels, as NorKyst), or subtract a finely tabulated horizontal-mean reference profile ρ̄(z) before integrating (Mellor et al. 1998) — an option to add if real stratification needs it.
  - [ ] Second order for smooth fields at P2 (measured 1.7–1.8 in the max norm, pre-asymptotic); first order in the one-sided pairs at the bottom of steep slopes. Higher-order vertical reconstruction (unlimited slopes where monotone) is the lever if needed.
  - [x] Cost: n_nodes²/2 pair evaluations per level per element (81/2 pairs at P2). Done 2026-10-01: parallel over elements, and each pair evaluated at all levels in one upward walk of both columns (no binary search) with the own-level pressures tabulated, bit-identical; 1.8 ms per call at 1024 P2 elements × 20 levels on 24 threads (serial before: 45 ms). Next lever if it shows: the cubic integrals vectorised across levels (structure of arrays per pair).
  - [ ] Curvilinear elements: the pairs use the affine metric (`Hydrostatic3D` asserts affine); the collocated chain rule `rx_i Dr + sx_i Ds` extends it.
- [ ] rx0/rx1 diagnostics and ROMS-style bathymetry smoothing. r_x0 is done in 2D (`Bathymetry2D::max_rx0`, volume-preserving `smooth_rx0`, 2026-09-29, P1.3). Still open: r_x1 (Haney number), which needs the σ levels, and an r_x1 bound for the 3D grid.
- [ ] Vtransform 2 + the true Vstretching 4. `ROMSVstretching4` is not ROMS Vs4, and `hc` is unused; Ω must use ∇·(Hz·u) once Hz varies horizontally.

### P4.4 Vertical mixing and boundary layers
- [x] GLS k-ε (NorKyst's closure) as a per-column solve reusing the tridiagonal solver (Umlauf & Burchard 2003; Warner et al. 2005). Done 2026-10-01 (`physics::GlsMixing`, module `physics::gls`): k-ε, k-ω and Umlauf & Burchard's generic model with Canuto A/B quasi-equilibrium stability functions, following GOTM (`tkeeq`, `genericeq`, `cmue_d`): `k`, `ψ` at the w-points in `Solution3D::tke`/`gls`, one backward-Euler step per baroclinic step before the implicit vertical diffusion (Patankar sinks, log-layer flux conditions, Galperin's length limit), `c₃⁻` from Ri_st = 0.25, κ from log-layer consistency, `c₃⁺` from k-ε's `c₃ε⁺ = 1`.
  - Gates: Kato–Phillips entrainment within 5 % of Price's `1.05 u*√(t/N₀)` at 12–30 h for all presets (k-ε 1.004–1.014, independent of Δt 10/60 s); open channel: bottom stress u*², `k` in local equilibrium to 3 %, the wall length `κz` at the first w-point (0.96 at 80 levels); homogeneous shear turbulence steady at Ri_st; an unstable column overturns in < 3 h; the 3D step bit-identical on 1 and 4 threads with GLS.
  - Found on the way: ROMS's `c₃⁺ = 1` for every model makes k-ω's implied ε equation `c₃ε⁺ = 2`, and k-ω then never started convection from weak turbulence (0.45 of a 0.5 °C inversion left after 12 h). The presets use GOTM's `(3/2 − c₃ε⁺)n + m` (k-ω 0, generic 0.665).
  - [x] Advect `k` and `ψ` (ROMS does; GOTM, being 1D, does not): horizontally with the layer transports and vertically with Ω, both at the w-points. Matters where turbulence is carried faster than it decays: tidal fronts and the lee of sills, the farm wake. Done 2026-10-01 (`Hydrostatic3D::with_turbulence_advection`, on by default with limited Akima vertically): the splitter carries `tke`/`gls` through the 3D stages as inventories over the w-cells (half the end layers at the bed and surface, `w_cell_thicknesses`), advected by the layer transports averaged to them (`LayerTransport::stagger_from`), so constancy and conservation are the tracers'; the closure then steps the sources in each column (operator splitting, as ROMS).
    - Gates: uniform `k` under a tide over a slope 2.5e-14 (with a river too), `ψ`'s inventory 3.4e-15; the wet/dry beach 1.7e-9 (interior `k`) and 7.9e-11 (`ψ` inventory); a turbulence patch in a 0.5 m/s current moves 900.01 m in 30 min (U·t = 900), stays put without advection. Cost: +7 % of the 3D step with GLS (`profile_3d mixing=gls turbulence=local|advected`: transport RHS 16 → 25 ms at 1024 P2 × 20 levels).
    - Found on the way: the end w-points hold the closure's boundary values (the wall's `l = κz₀`), not half-layer means; advected at their own values they cut the top viscosity to the background wherever Ω pointed down and decoupled the surface layer (`profile_3d` surface current 0.28 against 0.175 m/s after 20 steps). They are advected at their neighbours' values now. And a non-positive `ψ` from an undershoot made the closure reset the whole column (NaN through the implicit solve, then the final `max`); such a point is now taken as having no turbulence.
    - [ ] Positivity: horizontally the advection is the tracers' upwind DG, not positivity-preserving at P ≥ 1; a non-positive point is reset to no turbulence by the closure (`turbulence_that_is_not_positive_is_taken_as_none`), and a tiny positive `ψ` still floors `ε` at `ε_min`, a length `k^(3/2)/ε_min` of km (ROMS has the same floor, `gls_Pmin`). A Zhang–Shu scaling of each w-cell level towards its element mean (as the 2D positivity limiter), or a length cap (`l ≤ κ` × the distance to the bed or surface, or the column depth), would make undershoots harmless. Not seen in the gates or `profile_3d`; check on a real run with fronts.
    - [ ] Cost: `turbulence_transport_rhs` staggers a second `LayerTransport` (n_levels + 1 per column, faces included) and copies each field into a scratch to set the end points, every stage. The w-cell fluxes are linear in the layer ones: the transport kernel could read the layer transports and average on the fly (no second transport), and the end-point override could be a boundary condition of the vertical reconstruction instead of a copy.
  - [ ] Cost: `vertical_implicit` 2.1 ms per step with GLS against 0.8 ms with Pacanowski–Philander (1024 P2 elements × 20 levels, 24 threads, `examples/profile_3d.rs mixing=gls`; serial 23 against 8 ms; ≈ 2 % of the step). It is `powf`: `GlsMixing::dissipation`, `psi` and `coefficients` are evaluated two to three times per w-point per step. A k-ε fast path (`ψ = ε`, no `powf`) and one ε per w-point.
  - [x] Surface waves: TKE injection by breaking (Craig & Banner 1994; GOTM's `injection` boundary type) and a Charnock roughness `z₀ₛ = α u*ₛ²/g` instead of the constant 0.02 m. Done 2026-10-01: `GlsMixing::with_wave_breaking(c_w)` and `with_charnock_roughness(α)` (the constant roughness as its minimum, as ROMS's `CHARNOK`). The flux `c_w u*³` makes the model's shear-free layer `k ∝ s^(−a)`, `l = L s` (`GlsMixing::shear_free_layer`: k-ε 4.97 / 0.087, k-ω 2.53 / 0.24, generic 2.00 / 0.19); the `k` and `ψ` fluxes of that layer at the top layer's centre, the `ψ` one blended with the log layer's by how deep the centre lies in the wave-affected layer (≈ 2–4 z₀ₛ).
    - Measured before choosing (2026-10-01): with the log layer `u*` sets only the diagnostic boundary `k`, `ψ`, and from rest the top interior w-point needs ≈ 36 h/u* for 90 % of the log layer's `k` (3 h at 3 m top layers and u* = 1 cm/s, 20 min at 0.3 m). With the shear-free `ψ` condition imposed regardless of depth, a roughness of 2 cm at 0.5 m layers (Charnock's at u* = 1 cm/s) *cut* the top diffusivity 10–100× and the surface current ran to 2.8 m/s (k-ε): the condition shrinks the length inside the log layer. A blend by the local `P/ε` (Burchard 2001's σ_ψ interpolation) fed back on itself (k-ε's mixed layer collapsed after 30 h). The geometric blend has no feedback: with a 2 cm roughness the column is the log layer's (k to 0.6 %); with a wave height's (z₀ₛ = 0.5 m) k-ω and generic hold 4.3–4.4× the log `k` and 2.3–2.9× the diffusivity at the top interior w-point, the mid-depth unchanged, the spin-up one step instead of 15–31 min, and the Kato–Phillips depth unchanged (1.000–1.014 × Price; the entrainment is shear-driven).
    - Gates: `breaking_waves_make_the_shear_free_layer` (still water: `k` to 10 / 7.3 % at 0.1 m layers with k-ω / generic, first order in the layer thickness from the blend; k-ε to 2.7 % at 5 mm), `the_shear_free_layer_balances_both_equations`, `breaking_waves_raise_the_near_surface_mixing_under_wind`, waves in the Kato–Phillips gate.
    - [ ] k-ε with waves: its shear-free layer (`L` ≈ 0.09) is too thin for metre layers, and there it *lowers* the near-surface diffusivity (0.35× the log layer's at z₀ₛ = 0.5 m). Burchard (2001) makes σ_ε a function of `P/ε` (GOTM's `sig_peps`), which gives k-ε a realistic shear-free layer; until then use k-ω or the generic model with waves.
    - [ ] The wave height: Charnock's water-side roughness (cm) leaves the wave-affected layer unresolved at metre layers, so breaking changes nothing there; Terray et al.'s `z₀ₛ ≈ 0.6 H_s` (ROMS `ZOS_HSIG`) needs `H_s`: a reader for MET Norway's wave model (MyWaveWAM, 800 m along the coast) next to `AtmosphereReader`, or a fetch-limited `H_s(U₁₀, fetch)` for fjords. The roughness is one value per model now; per column it would come with `H_s`.
  - [ ] Bed roughness is set twice: `GlsMixing::with_roughness` and `BottomDrag3D::log_layer(z0)` (whose κ is 0.41, the closure's 0.416 for k-ε). Let `Hydrostatic3D::with_bottom_drag` hand its `z₀` to the closure, or take both from one bed description (and the spatially varying `z₀` below).
  - [ ] Thin columns keep their last `k`, `ψ` while thin and start from them when they deepen again; reset them to the minimum (or the neighbours') on re-wetting if shoreline mixing looks wrong.
  - [ ] `PacanowskiPhilanderMixing` keeps its own `g`, `ρ₀` though `Column` now carries the model's; drop the fields (API change). It also still gives unstable, shear-free columns only background mixing (use GLS there).
  - [x] `Solution3D::eddy_viscosity`/`eddy_diffusivity` held `n_levels` values per column, the closures' w-points 0..N−1 labelled as layer centres, without the surface one. Done 2026-10-01: stored at all `n_levels + 1` w-points like `tke` (ROMS's `AKv`/`AKt`); the 3D particle walk reads them.
  - KPP as an alternative: not needed for now.
- [x] Convective adjustment (low-shear unstable columns currently get background mixing). Not needed with GLS: buoyancy production overturns an unstable column within hours (`an_unstable_column_overturns_by_buoyancy_production`). Pacanowski–Philander still has none.
- [x] Per-column surface stress for the 3D model. Done 2026-10-01: `Hydrostatic3D::with_surface_stress(impl SurfaceStress3D)` adds a per-node field `τ(x, t)` to the uniform `Forcing::surface_stress`, in `G` (at `tⁿ`), the vertical diffusion's surface flux and the closure's `u*` (at `tⁿ + Δt/2`). `GriddedAtmosphere2D::split_for_3d` gives the 2D module the pressure gradient and the columns the wind (`GriddedWindStress`, sharing stencils and snapshots); `AnalyticSurfaceStress` takes a function. Gates: `a_varying_wind_reaches_each_columns_drag_balance` (τ varying 3× across a periodic ocean, every column at its own drag balance to 1.1e-13, shear 1.9e-13), `a_time_varying_surface_stress_reaches_the_depth_mean_at_second_order` (rates 1.94, 1.98), `each_column_feels_its_own_surface_stress` (GLS columns bit for bit as lone columns), `gridded_wind_stress_varies_from_node_to_node`.
  - [ ] The surface buoyancy flux is still one domain value (`Forcing::surface_buoyancy_flux`), and the weather readers carry no heat fluxes: a per-column heat/freshwater flux (bulk formulae from `AtmosphereReader` air temperature, humidity, radiation) is the next step for a real 3D run beyond momentum.
  - [ ] Thin columns (`with_min_column_depth`) get no surface stress at all: `G` is zero there and the 2D module carries no wind in a 3D run, so tidal flats under a few dm of water are not wind-driven. Give thin columns the stress through `G` (their depth mean is the 2D module's only, so `τ/ρ₀` alone, without the shear terms).
  - [ ] `GriddedWindStress` is evaluated twice per baroclinic step (`tⁿ` for `G`, `tⁿ + Δt/2` for the columns) into a full `[element][node]` buffer each time; cheap next to the 3D step, but a farm mesh of 10⁶ nodes could keep the `tⁿ⁺¹` evaluation for the next step's `G`.
- [x] Implicit quadratic bottom drag with a log-layer Cd; consistent with 2D friction. Done 2026-09-30 (`physics::bottom_drag`, `Hydrostatic3D::with_bottom_drag`): `τ_b/ρ₀ = C_d|u_b|u_b`, constant or log-layer `C_d` bounded to [0.0025, 0.1]; rate `r = C_d|u_bⁿ|` frozen over the step, `−r·ū` point-implicit in the barotropic pass, `−r·(u_b − ū)` in G, `r·u_bⁿ⁺¹` implicit in the vertical diffusion. Without shear it is the 2D quadratic friction exactly (converges at first order in Δt); wind against drag reaches `C_d u_b² = τ/ρ₀` to 1e-13; the P4.5 beach runs with it.
  - [ ] Second order in time: `r` lags a step (first order, as the 2D point-implicit friction). Re-evaluate `r` from the pass's `ū` per stage, with the frozen shear deviation `u_b − ū` (Thetis-style), if the drag's time error matters.
  - [ ] A spatially varying `z₀` (sediment map, as the 2D roughness-map item in P1.2), and a drag coefficient consistent with the 2D module's Manning n for runs that compare 2D and 3D.
  - [ ] ROMS's `LIMIT_BSTRESS` is not needed (the drag is implicit), but thin columns take `C_d,max = 0.1` from their thin bottom layer: check the wet/dry front speed on a real beach against the 2D `WetDry` with Manning.
- [ ] Surface heat flux Q_net/(ρ₀c_p) (currently fed a buoyancy flux). The momentum stresses use the model's ρ₀ since P4.1 PR 2; the heat flux still needs it.
- [ ] Surface heat-flux budget (shortwave, longwave, sensible/latent) for multi-day SST evolution (formerly "P3.1 Surface Heat Flux Budget").

### P4.5 Remaining 3D physics and numerics
- [x] Higher-order vertical advection. Done 2026-09-30 (`VerticalAdvection`, `Hydrostatic3D::with_vertical_advection`): fourth-order Akima (ROMS `TS_A4VADVECTION`) is the default for the tracers, and upwind remains an option. The mode-1 internal seiche on 10 levels: upwind 1.06 % slow, 1.2 % amplitude loss per period; Akima 0.37 %, 0.14 %, second order in the level spacing (gates `akima_vertical_advection_keeps_internal_waves`, `a_mode_1_internal_seiche_has_the_internal_wave_speed`).
  - [x] Akima is not monotone. Done 2026-09-30: `VerticalAdvection::Tvd`, third-order upwind with a TVD limiter (Koren 1993; the spatial part of HSIMT, Wu & Zhu 2010, whose Courant-number terms the SSP-RK3 stages replace). A front advected 16 layers: TVD stays in [0, 1] over 6 layers, upwind smears it over 18, Akima overshoots 16 %; on a tanh halocline TVD is 60× more accurate than upwind, Akima 800× (`vertical_schemes_on_a_front_and_a_smooth_profile`). Out of an end layer it is linear through the two end layers (first order there froze the bed layer at a wall, and a seiche over a thick bed layer drifted by a third of its amplitude per period).
    - The beach's 1.0e-3 °C undershoot was not a defect of Akima's: the layer means start linear in z, so the water at the bed is 0.26 °C colder than the bed layer's mean, and rising, it cools the layer (TVD: 1.6e-3 °C). Upwind stayed in range only because it freezes the bed layer at a wall.
  - [x] Akima in index space. Done 2026-09-30: Akima and TVD reconstruct on the layers' σ-thicknesses (`LayerTransport::d_sigma`), exact for linear profiles on any spacing. On Song–Haidvogel θs 5, θb 0.4 levels the divergence error of layer means of a smooth profile at 160 levels: index-space Akima 9.2e-3 (stalling), thickness-aware 3.2e-4; TVD 9.7e-4 against 3.8e-6 (third order).
    - The internal seiche on stretched levels is no judge of it: its sampled sine is not the discrete mode on uneven layers, and the index-space form's period error is smaller there at 10–40 levels (+0.23 % against +0.33 % on 20), both converging to −0.03 % at 80.
  - [x] The momentum's vertical advection. Measured 2026-09-30, and it stays second-order centred (`VerticalAdvection::Centred`, linear and non-dissipative); `Hydrostatic3D::with_momentum_vertical_advection` selects Akima. On a tanh profile Akima is 100× more accurate (error 5.0e-5 against 5.1e-3; at a front 16 % against 28 % overshoot), but in a sheared flow it barely shows: a nonlinear internal seiche (a = 3 and 5 m of 20 m, u ≈ 0.15–0.25 m/s) on 20 levels is 0.098 % against 0.111 % (a = 3 m) and 0.086 % against 0.103 % (5 m) off its 80-level period. All 3D gates pass with either.
- [ ] Rotated/geopotential horizontal diffusion via BR1 (no 3D horizontal diffusion of the tracers exists; `viscosity_3d` is the momentum's, along σ).
- [x] Split-form (kinetic-energy-preserving) momentum advection. Done 2026-10-02 (`MomentumAdvectionForm::Split`, the default; `Hydrostatic3D::with_momentum_advection_form`): the P2 grid-scale shear instability was the conservative form's aliasing, as the split forms of under-resolved DG flows suggested (Gassner, Winters & Kopriva 2016). With centred `Ω` fluxes the split form changes the kinetic energy only by the upwind faces' `−½ ∮ Σ_l |F_l| |Δu_l|²` (to 1e-15; the conservative form makes 5.5e-3 of that back, gate `split_momentum_advection_only_dissipates_kinetic_energy`).
  - Without any horizontal viscosity, conservative against split: the P2 lock exchange NaN within 2 h against fronts at Fr 0.464 / 0.464, T in range to 2e-12 °C (gate `a_p2_lock_exchange_runs_without_horizontal_viscosity`); split also holds at 62–500 m, P3, 20 levels, 10–40 s steps, over 3 h (Fr 0.43–0.48). The fjord estuary at ν_h = 5: NaN at step 21 against a run with the same exchange, also at ν_h = 1 and 0. The balanced tanh seamount at `r_x0` 0.32: NaN at 54 h against bounded over 4 days (rms 1.5–1.8 cm/s). Smagorinsky `C_s` 0.2 holds the lock exchange (conservative: 0.5).
  - The earlier "changed flow" in the fjord's volume budget was the gate's sampling: it took `H_z u` from one side of the element face at each section, and the split form's face jumps are larger (5e-4 of the river's against 1e-4). Averaged over both sides, 3–6e-5 in either form.
  - Cost: none measurable (`profile_3d mform=split|conservative`: transport RHS 11–14 against 11–12 ms per step, noise).
  - [x] Without viscosity the fronts run slower (Fr 0.44–0.48 against 0.497 at ν = 10) and drift apart on finer meshes (125 m: 0.467 / 0.434; 62 m: 0.479 / 0.447): an interface left to the grid mixes more. Measured 2026-10-02 with Ilıcak et al.'s (2012) reference potential energy (`solver::PotentialEnergy3D`: the water sorted by density into level layers over the basin's own hypsometry, exact per GLL column; RPE grows only by diapycnal mixing, and the lock exchange has no diffusivity of T, so all of it is spurious):
    - Exact against the discrete energy budget: under a constant vertical κ a stratified basin at rest gains `g κ A Δt (ρ_bed − ρ_surface)` per step (Winters et al. 1995) to 4.2e-11 (gate `vertical_diffusion_raises_the_reference_potential_energy_at_its_exact_rate`). Energies are of `ρ − ρ_r`: with full ρ the round-off of `g ρ z V` was 3.6e-10 of a step's change.
    - **The spurious mixing is set by the grid Reynolds number `Re_Δ = ΔU·Δx/ν`** (ΔU = 0.4 m/s, Δx the node spacing), as Ilıcak et al. found for z-, σ- and isopycnal models: at `Re_Δ` = 10 it is 5.1–5.7e-4 W/m² from 500 m to 62 m (hours 1–3), and over hours 1–2 3.818e-4 against 3.812e-4 on 250 and 125 m (gate `spurious_mixing_is_set_by_the_grid_reynolds_number`).
    - Without viscosity it grows as the mesh is refined, levelling off: 1.07 / 1.38 / 1.68 / 1.69e-3 W/m² at 500 / 250 / 125 / 62 m (20 levels: the same; re-measured after the end-layer bound, which moved only these, from 1.07 / 1.35 / 1.64 / 1.72e-3). At a fixed ν = 10 it converges: 5.7 / 3.3 / 2.3 / 2.0e-4, levelling off at the 10 levels' floor; on 20 levels 1.45e-4 at 125 m, 7.9e-5 at 62 m. On 250 m it falls monotonically with ν: 13.8 / 9.7 / 7.0 / 5.1 / 3.3 / 2.2e-4 at ν = 0 / 1 / 2.5 / 5 / 10 / 20, the fronts 0.458 → 0.497.
    - Smagorinsky: `C_s` 0.2 gives 8.9 / 7.9 / 6.6 / 5.9e-4 at 500 / 250 / 125 / 62 m, 0.5 gives 6.1 / 4.0 / 3.2 / 2.8e-4 (1.2–1.4× ν = 10). P1 without viscosity 6.0e-4 (its own dissipation). The gates' values: P2 inviscid 8.4e-4 (hours 1–2), ν = 10 2.7e-4, `C_s` 0.7 2.5e-4.
    - So for the advection's mixing alone: keep `Re_Δ = ΔU·Δx/ν` ≲ 10 for the velocity jumps ΔU across the fronts that matter (ΔU = 0.4 m/s: ν ≈ 0.04·Δx, ≈ 1 m²/s at 30 m node spacing), or `C_s` ≈ 0.5.
    - The farm channel (`examples/farm_3d.rs`, now reporting each 3D run's RPE growth; 3 h, ν = 1, GLS k-ε): `C_s` does not matter there. With cages 7.3 / 6.6 / 7.3e-6 W/m² at `C_s` 0 / 0.2 / 0.5, without −1.97e-5 in all three: 20–100× below the lock exchange, ≈ 0.2 J/m² over 3 h against ≈ 2400 J/m² of stratification anomaly. The thermocline is barely touched in 3 h, and the cages' wake is not a sharp front. Keep the default 0.2.
    - [x] **RPE falls in the open farm channel** (−1.97e-5 W/m² over 3 h, the same at every `C_s`). Measured 2026-10-02 (`farm_3d` now reports RPE, the range of T and the volume and heat inventories every half hour; `limiter`, `tvadv`, `runs=open`, `closure=none` switches): it is the **vertical** advection's, and it is not a defect. The horizontal suspect was wrong: P1 and P2 give the same curve (−1.25 / −4.47e-5 W/m² over the half hours to 1.5 / 2 h against −1.23 / −4.39e-5), and the horizontal Kuzmin limiter changes nothing (−1.958e-5); TVD vertically is no better (−2.45e-5); upwind vertically turns it into +1.5e-5 (at 1.5 h). Volume and heat are conserved to 5e-13, so nothing leaks. Without any tracer diffusivity (`closure=none`) the same dip (−0.33 J/m² over hours 1–3), then RPE alternates in sign with the tide and grows on balance: 7.6e-5 W/m² over 12.5 h, upwind 4.5e-4 (monotone growth). Not a coarse-layer sampling effect: 32 levels dip as much (−0.2 J/m², hours 1.5–4.5), 8 levels not at all (their dissipation dominates). Only first-order upwind is a doubly stochastic map, which cannot lower RPE; a low-dissipation scheme can, temporarily (Ilıcak et al. 2012). With GLS the dip is ≈ 1–2 % of the mixing after hour 3, when the bottom boundary layer reaches the thermocline (2.7e-3 W/m² at 10 h).
      - So measure mixing over a tidal cycle (or longer), not over hours: over windows shorter than the internal and tidal periods the advection's reversible exchange can have either sign.
      - [ ] With `closure=none` (no diffusivity) the P1 farm channel's T undershoots 10 °C by up to 0.07 °C (also with upwind vertically, so horizontal; there is no tracer limiter by default), gone with `limiter=kuzmin`. Consider making the horizontal Kuzmin limiter the example's (and `Hydrostatic3D`'s) default once a realistic run confirms it does not cost accuracy over slopes (P4.6).
    - [x] T leaves its range in some configurations the gates do not cover (hours 1–3 or 1–2): 7.4e-5 °C with `C_s` 0.1 on 250 m, 1.8e-5 °C with `C_s` 0.5 on 62 m, 6.0e-5 °C inviscid on 125 m over hours 1–2 (1–3: 4.7e-12). Done 2026-10-02: the **vertical advection out of an end layer** let it through. Out of the surface (bed) layer there is no layer beyond for the TVD bound, and both `LimitedAkima` and `Tvd` took a value between the two end layers' means. Under a light gravity current's head the surface layer sits at 12.5 °C over cooler water, sinking: the water leaving it was cooler than its mean, and the warm water flowing in along it carried the layer's element mean past the range (the nodes followed; the Kuzmin limiter cannot change element means). TVD was worse: 2.4e-2 °C in the 125 m run. Now the increment out of an end layer is bounded by the end layer's own gradient, extrapolated linearly from the gradients between the three end layers' centres (`end_layer_limited`): linear and smoothly curved profiles keep their linear value (so the seiche over a thick bed layer, the reason first order was rejected, is unchanged), a plateau over a front leaves at its own mean. All three configurations in range to 3–8e-12 °C (TVD too); gate `vertical_values_out_of_an_end_layer_at_a_plateau_are_its_mean`. Fronts and mixing barely move (P2 ν = 10 TVD Fr 0.497/0.495 → 0.496/0.494; inviscid 125 m over hours 1–3 0.473/0.445 → 0.467/0.434, mixing 1.64 → 1.68e-3 W/m²; `Re_Δ` = 10 the same to 3 digits). The GLS turbulence's advection (limited Akima by default) is unchanged bit for bit: its end w-points are advected at their interior neighbours' values, so the difference out of an end cell is zero under either rule.
      - [ ] P3 on 250 m without viscosity (20 s steps, hours 1–3) ends 3.3e-9 °C out of range, where every P1/P2 run ends at 2–8e-12 (found re-measuring after the bound; not checked whether it predates it). No gate runs P3; find whether it is a layer's element mean (the vertical advection) or nodes the horizontal Kuzmin limiter leaves (its P3 bounds).
      - [ ] The inviscid 125 m TVD run still has a transient nodal excess of 1e-8 °C (hour 1.4, surface layer, element mean in range): the Kuzmin bounds extrapolated past the end layers (`TracerLimiter3DConfig` at constant height, clamped to the column's nodal range) allow it. Gone by the end of the step sequence; look at it if a gate on the intermediate states is wanted.
    - [ ] Cost of the diagnostic: one serial sort of every wet layer (`n_elements × n_nodes × n_levels` parcels; 1.8e8 for 1e6 P2 elements × 20 levels; not measured at that size). Fine for occasional output; for a time series on a large mesh, a parallel sort, or a density histogram with exact moments per bin (binned RPE, error bounded by the bin width), is the lever.
    - [ ] Report RPE in the 3D runs' output (a `Simulation3D` callback or the NetCDF writer's global attributes), so long runs' spurious mixing is visible without a test.
  - [ ] `apply_momentum_transport_3d` takes 12 arguments; the form and the vertical scheme (and `Rhs3DConfig`'s two fields) could be one `MomentumAdvection { horizontal, vertical }`, as the tracers' could carry their vertical scheme and limiter.
  - [x] **Horizontal viscosity for the 3D momentum is needed at P2** (found 2026-09-30 with the lock exchange, P4.6): the interface's shear instability grows at the grid scale, fastest in a hydrostatic model, and P2 blows up after ≈ 1500 s (the same at 10 and 2 s steps); P1's numerical dissipation holds it. 1e-2 m²/s of vertical viscosity also holds it, at Fr 0.20. Ilıcak et al. (2012) run the benchmark with horizontal viscosity for this reason. (Since 2026-10-02 the cause is removed: in split form P2 holds with no viscosity, above; the viscosity remains as physics.) Done 2026-10-01 (`Hydrostatic3D::with_horizontal_viscosity`, `solver::rhs::viscosity_3d`): BR1 per layer on the shear `u − ⟨u⟩` along σ, `∇·(νD∇u′)/D`; with constant ν its column sum is zero to round-off, so the 2D module's own viscosity keeps the depth mean and `G` gets nothing twice.
    - Gates: the P2 lock exchange on 250 m with ν = 10 m²/s and TVD vertical advection, Fr 0.500/0.494, T in range to 2e-12 °C (`a_p2_lock_exchange_runs_with_horizontal_viscosity`); a divergence-free shear mode decays to 1.1e-6 of `e^(−νk²t)` under the mode split with the depth mean at rest (`a_shear_mode_decays_at_the_viscous_rate_and_leaves_the_depth_mean`); the decay rate converges at 2N.
    - The ν that holds the P2 lock exchange shrinks with the spacing: 5–10 m²/s at 500 m, 5–7 at 250 m, 2.5–5 at 125 m, ≤ 2.5 at 62 m (grid Reynolds numbers `ΔU·Δx/ν` 7–10 at the node spacing, 10–20 at 500 m). At ν = 5 on 250 m the mid-depth interface grows at ≈ 6e-3 s⁻¹ (`kΔU/2` at the node spacing).
    - [x] Smagorinsky (or a shear-dependent ν) for the 3D shear: a constant ν that holds the interface also damps everything else at that scale. Done 2026-10-01 (`Hydrostatic3D::with_smagorinsky_viscosity`, `HorizontalViscosity3D`): `ν_l = ν₀ + (C_s Δ)²|S_l|`, the strain of the layer's own velocity, Δ the node spacing. The column sum of the shear's stresses is no longer zero; `G` carries it to the depth mean, and the 2D module's own viscosity differentiates only ⟨u⟩, so nothing counts twice (gate `smagorinsky_shear_stress_reaches_the_depth_mean_and_conserves_momentum`: total momentum to 9e-10 of what moved). `compute_dt` bounds the step from the current strain.
      - P2 lock exchange on 250 m with `C_s` = 0.7 and no background: Fr 0.504/0.499, ν 19 m²/s at the heads, ≈ 5 along the interface, ≤ 0.1 in the water at rest (gate `a_p2_lock_exchange_runs_with_smagorinsky_viscosity`). `C_s` 0.5 holds, 0.4 does not; on 125 m 0.4 holds (ν ≈ 1–3 along the interface at 0.5). The coefficient is above the usual 0.1–0.2: the instability is in x–z, and only its horizontal strain is seen.
      - [ ] Cost: two more BR1 gradient passes per RHS (the depth mean's) and a strain per node and layer; `compute_dt` repeats the gradient passes once per step and allocates its per-element ν. (Parallel since 2026-10-01; the allocation, one `Vec` per step, is left.)
      - [ ] Not yet measured on a realistic case (a fjord with a sill, tidal shear): the coefficient that holds the lock exchange may over-damp a tidal mixed layer. A vertical-shear-aware ν (Leith, or the Ri-dependent forms of Ilıcak et al. 2012) is the alternative if it does.
      - Data point (2026-10-01, the fjord estuary gate): a P2 fjord on 500 m with a fresh outflow (0.5 m/s, g′ ≈ 0.2 m/s²) blows up at the face of the river's element after 1.5 h with a constant ν_h = 5 m²/s, at any step from 40 to 240 s; 20 m²/s holds it. Smagorinsky not yet tried there. In split form (2026-10-02) it runs at 5, 1 and 0 m²/s.
    - [x] The horizontal Kuzmin limiter bounds T only along layers: with Akima vertical advection a P2 lock exchange at 125 m and ν = 10 undershoots by 0.23 °C next to the interface (TVD: 4e-12). Done 2026-10-01, with a flux limiter rather than a tracer limiter: the undershoot was in the layers' **element means** (one element layer at 7.27 °C in a 7.5–12.5 °C range), which a conservative slope limiter cannot change whatever neighbours it bounds by. `VerticalAdvection::LimitedAkima` (now the tracers' default) clips Akima's surface values to Koren's TVD bounds from the layers below and above: 4e-12 °C (gate `a_sharp_lock_exchange_interface_stays_in_range`), and on smooth profiles exactly Akima (tanh halocline: same error to the last digit, 13× below TVD's; internal seiche unchanged).
      - Akima's undershoot comes and goes: at 2 h it was ≤ 2e-8 °C in every 125–250 m run tried, and 0.016 °C with ν = 15; with Smagorinsky (`C_s` 0.5, 125 m, 3 h) 3e-11 °C.
      - [ ] `TracerLimiter3DConfig::vertical_column_bounds` scales each node's whole column towards its column mean until it fits the global bounds (`TracerBounds`): it does move inventory between layers, but to fix one layer it flattens the column's whole stratification, and only against global bounds. With the flux limiter it has no known use; revisit or drop it.
    - [x] The P2 gate took ≈ 60 s in a debug build (2 s in release), three lock-exchange gates > 60 s each. CI and `cargo test --profile ci` (opt-level 1) take 9 s for the lib suite. Since the parallel kernels (2026-10-01) the four lock-exchange gates take 34 s together in a debug build.
- [x] 3D wetting/drying (any dry node gave NaN). Done 2026-09-30:
  - Thin columns (D < `Hydrostatic3D::with_min_column_depth`, default 0.1 m, ROMS's Dcrit) carry no shear (u = ū, zero 3D momentum tendency, no vertical diffusion), get no stress through G, and exert no baroclinic pressure. The 3D momentum advection and the mean-flow part of G see them with zero velocity, so films at the 2D velocity cap (20 m/s) stay the 2D module's.
  - Tracers are element means per level for the step in elements where the 2D pass broke the nodal identity (`WetDry` subcells, positivity limiter): constant and conservative. Nodes (or marked elements) with essentially no water keep their last concentration.
  - Gates (beach from −4 to +2 m, 0.3 m slosh, wind, stratified, ≈ 4 periods): no NaN, 0 clips, T within its initial range; uniform T/S drift 5.3e-9 (round-off amplified by nearly dry elements); inventories 7.4e-12; a stratified lake with dry land at rest 2.0e-13 m/s. Before: NaN in the first step.
  - [ ] The shoreline elements' element means flatten the tracer along σ within them while the pass is inconsistent there (a band one element wide at moving shorelines). A subcell (GLL-cell) finite-volume tracer update, matching the 2D module's subcells, would keep the nodal structure; it needs the 2D subcell mass fluxes in DU_avg2.
  - [ ] Films: ū = hu/h in columns of a few mm reaches the 2D cap (20 m/s). Harmless to the 3D fields now, but it sets output statistics; consider reporting speeds only where D ≥ the minimum column depth (as `froya_real_data` does with its open-water column).
- [ ] EOS: delegate the physics trait to the fixed UNESCO EOS (P0.17) with a linear fast path; TEOS-10 later.
- [x] `compute_dt`: internal-wave speed from stratification (not a hardcoded 2 m/s) and the vertical CFL. Done 2026-10-01: the WKB first-mode speed `c₁ = (1/π)∫N dz` of each column (floored at `MIN_INTERNAL_WAVE_SPEED` = 0.1 m/s so a run from rest has a finite step), `|Ω|Δt/H_z ≤ 1` and `|f|Δt ≤ 1`; gate `the_3d_step_follows_the_internal_wave_speed`. In the weakly stratified farm channel (`examples/farm_3d.rs`, 3 h) the 2 m/s set the step: 14,320 steps (Δt 0.6–0.8 s, 17 min) before, 2,008 (Δt 2–17 s, 2.6 min) after.
  - [ ] The advective part still divides by `(N+1)²` with `h = √J` (half an element side): far below the DG–RK3 limit the 2D module measures with its per-node metric (`compute_dt_swe_2d`, P2.1). Porting the 3D bound to that form changes what `cfl` means for every 3D run (the 3D gates run at `cfl` 10 for that reason); do it with a linear-stability measurement as in `tests/linear_stability_test.rs`.
  - [ ] Relabel or convert Ω vs w in output (`Solution3D::w` is Ω at the layer centres).
- [x] Parallelise the 3D kernels; no `Vec` allocation in inner loops. Done 2026-10-01 (`solver::core::blocks`): every kernel writes its outputs' disjoint element blocks (`for_each_block`, exact reductions only) with per-thread scratch from a thread-local cache (`Pooled`), on rayon's pool with `parallel` and in order without it, so the result is bit-identical to the serial code and independent of the thread count (gate `the_3d_step_does_not_depend_on_the_thread_count`). Converted: PGF, Coriolis, `LayerTransport::compute`, tracer and momentum advection, horizontal viscosity, vertical diffusion (`VerticalMixing::compute_mixing_into`, no per-column `Vec`s), the 3D tracer limiters, the inventory conversions, the slow forcing's sums, `compute_dt`, `Solution3D`'s stage combinations.
  - Measured with `examples/profile_3d.rs` (a stratified basin with every 3D term on, each `ModeSplitPhysics` call timed; 24 threads): 1024 P2 elements × 20 levels 378 → 107 ms per step, 8192 elements 3.19 → 0.87 s (the 3D kernels 2.5 → 0.28 s, ≈ 9×).
  - [ ] **The barotropic pass is now two thirds of a 3D step** (587 of 872 ms at 8192 elements, n_bt = 128, i.e. 384 2D RHS evaluations). At 1024 elements the 2D RHS costs 0.26 ms per evaluation on 24 threads against 0.35 ms on one: at that size it is bound by rayon dispatch (several parallel regions per evaluation, each a wake-up of the pool), not by work. Levers, cheapest first: fuse each barotropic stage's work (RHS, `+ G`, `BarotropicTransport::accumulate`, the drag's damping, the stage combination) into one element-block pass; run 2D passes below a size threshold on one thread; the forward-backward fast mode (P4.1 design decisions), which needs fewer RHS per substep.
    - [x] Fuse the stage work. Done 2026-10-02 (`barotropic_stage`, bit-identical; gate `fused_barotropic_stages_match_ssp_rk3_bit_for_bit`). Instrumented first: at 8192 elements the serial sweeps were ≈ 300 of the pass's 670 ms per step (`+ G` and DU_avg2 110 ms, the stage combinations 125, the drag 73), and the first two were 1.5× slower on 24 threads than on one. After: pass + splitter 655–687 → 456–515 ms (step 937–968 → 739–806) at 8192, 64–77 → 48–50 ms (step 100–120 → 85–87) at 1024; one thread unchanged.
    - Not worth it now: a serial size threshold. At 1024 elements the pass is 48–50 ms on 24 threads against 78 on one.
    - [x] Where the rest goes (measured 2026-10-02, instrumented, per step at 8192 elements, 24 threads; the "≈ 360 ms of RHS" first noted here had counted the warm-up step too): the pass 335 ms = the 2D RHS 239 (edge fluxes 76, elements 162) + the fused stages 96; the splitter outside the pass ≈ 126 ms, almost all of it in the 3D stages (≈ 75 ms of copies and inventory conversions around the physics calls, ≈ 30 of `Solution3D` stage combinations). At 1024 elements: pass 29 ms (RHS 21, of it edges 8.8), splitter ≈ 19.
      - The 2D RHS alone (`SWEFormulation2D::EntropyStable`, P2, standalone): 1.95 ms on 1 thread, 0.35 on 12, 0.41 on 24 at 8192 elements (edges 0.41 → 0.11 → 0.13, elements 1.54 → 0.25 → 0.28); 0.28 / 0.094 / 0.116 ms at 1024 (edges 55 / 36 / 48 µs). So it scales to the 12 cores and loses on their SMT siblings, and at 1024 elements each parallel region costs ≈ 30–40 µs of fork-join on 24 threads, more than the edge pass's work. The whole 3D step is no faster on 12 threads, though (the 3D kernels gain from SMT): 1024 elements 80 against 85–95 ms, 8192 806 against 740–780.
      - [x] Done 2026-10-02: the splitter's 3D-stage bookkeeping moves fewer bytes, bit-identical. Each stage copied the whole stage state into `concentrations` and then overwrote its six inventory fields; it now copies the others only (`copy_non_inventory_fields`). The first stage takes `u, v` of `R₃D`, not the whole state. `InventoryFields`' copies, `set_column_sums`, `depth_average`, `shift_columns` and the stage's zeroing of `w`, `rho` are parallel. A/B, alternating runs, 8192 elements: pass + splitter median 495 → 473 ms.
    - [ ] Fewer parallel regions per barotropic stage: run the fused stage as the RHS's per-element continuation (as `compute_rhs_swe_2d_subset_then` does for local time stepping, which would need the face mass fluxes and a whole-mesh form without the subset's edge collection). Three regions per stage now; at 1024 elements one region less is ≈ 4 ms of the 85 ms step, and the continuation keeps `k` in cache (the fused stage is 96 ms at 8192, bandwidth-bound).
      - Tried and dropped 2026-10-02: the split form's edge fluxes inside the element pass (each element computing its own four edges into a per-thread buffer, interior edges twice; bit-identical). The RHS alone at 1024 elements: 0.120 → 0.086 ms on 24 threads, but 0.278 → 0.318 on one; at 8192: 0.415 → 0.439 ms on 24 threads, 0.356 → 0.395 on 12, 2.01 → 2.43 on one. It wins only on meshes small enough for the fork-join to dominate, and real runs are ≥ 10k elements.
    - [ ] Then the forward-backward fast mode (fewer RHS per substep, P4.1).
    - [ ] Two fused SSP-RK3 stage implementations now exist: the pass's `barotropic_stage` (with `+ G`, DU_avg2 and the filter's sums) and `Simulation`'s one-level `MultirateStepper` (P2.3). Running the pass on `MultirateStepper` with hooks for the sums would leave one, and would give the barotropic substeps local time stepping on refined farm meshes, where the 20 m elements set `n_bt` for the whole domain (P2.5). DU_avg2 must then weight each element's fluxes by its own substeps' stage weights.
  - [ ] **At the real domain's steps the splitter's fixed work, not the pass, is the largest per-step cost** (measured 2026-10-06, `profile_3d`, P2, 20 levels, GLS, 24 threads). At 12,288 elements, dt 60 s (171 substeps) is 965 ms of pass + splitter, and dt 4 s (12 substeps) is 262 ms. So ≈ 4.4 ms per substep plus ≈ 210 ms per step outside every physics call, 27 % of the 776 ms step at dt 4 s. At 2,800 elements and dt 4 s it is ≈ 52 of 171 ms. The Frøya and Mausund 3D tides step at 0.8–5 s with 4–35 substeps, so the "two thirds" above holds only for long steps in deep water. The fixed part is the stages' data movement: per stage `to_values` (6 fields), `copy_to_state` / `copy_from_state` (6 each), `to_inventories` (6), `copy_non_inventory_fields`, `to_inventory` of the tendencies, and `Solution3D`'s combination of 11 fields, including `w` and `rho` whose rates are zero. That is ≈ 60 single-field streaming passes per stage. Levers: let the kernels read inventories and divide by `H_z` on load (H_z from η and Δσ), so the stage hook and the RHS closure stop converting back and forth; skip `w` and `rho` in the combination; replace `with_thin_columns_at_rest`'s whole-state copy (taken whenever any column is thin, so every stage and `G` of a coastal run) with a thin mask that the momentum advection reads.
  - Profiling note (2026-10-06): AMD uProf (TBP) on the `profiling` build of `froya_real_data` (Mausund 3D tide) put 19 % of CPU time in the 3D Kuzmin limiter. Alternating A/B runs with `debug_3d=nolimiter` showed no difference beyond the ±10 % run-to-run noise, and `profile_3d`'s `post_stage` (which includes the limiter) is 3–6 %. The function names on monomorphised rayon helpers are unreliable in this build, possibly from the linker's identical-code folding (unverified). Trust the timers and A/B runs, and alternate runs: identical runs on this laptop differed by 345 against 416 ms per step.
  - [ ] Not converted (serial, cheap at present): the nesting's band relaxation (`Nesting3D` columns, `relax_shear`, `relax_tracers`), `with_thin_columns_at_rest` and `zero_thin_momentum`, `update_density`, the splitter's 2D loops (the constant rates, the filtered barotropic state, `to_transport`, the element-means marks: ≈ 3 ms per step together at 8192 elements). The splitter's column loops (`set_column_sums`, `depth_average`, `shift_columns`) are parallel since 2026-10-02.
  - [ ] How parallel code is written (noted 2026-10-01): the 2D kernels have separate serial and parallel entry points (`compute_rhs_swe_2d_into` / `compute_rhs_swe_2d_parallel_into`, the wet/dry and damping pairs), chosen with `cfg` at the call sites in `physics/builder.rs`, and their own thread-local workspace guards (`WorkspaceGuard`, `FaceFluxGuard`, `ViscousGuard` in `swe_2d.rs`). The 3D kernels have one entry point with the `cfg` inside `solver::core::blocks`. Moving the 2D kernels onto `blocks` (or `blocks` onto a small trait both use) would remove the duplicated public API (P6) and give the 2D module the same thread-count gate. `DisjointChunks` (local time stepping) and `blocks` are the same idea; keep one.

  - [ ] Three Thomas solvers: `solver::algorithms::tridiagonal::solve_tridiagonal` (vertical diffusion), `physics::implicit_advection::ColumnSolve::solve` (implicit vertical advection, from fluxes, with zero-gradient ends) and the waves' `thomas` (`waves/model.rs`). One in-place solver over `(a, b, c)` closures would serve all three.
    - [ ] The vertical diffusion (`vertical_diffusion.rs`, `solve_diffusion_column`) solves u, v, T and S separately and rebuilds the matrix for each, but u/v share `A_v` and T/S share `K_t`: share one elimination per pair, as the waves' `thomas_pair` does (scalar, first). Then solve 8 columns at once (`[level][lane]` scratch in L1, the wet mask as lanes): the recurrence runs along the column, so lanes must be columns (SIMD survey 2026-10-08).
    - [ ] `UnescoEOS::update_density` (`physics/eos.rs`) is serial and calls `s.powf(1.5)`: `s * s.sqrt()` and a flat parallel loop would let it autovectorise.
  - [ ] The implicit vertical advection and the vertical diffusion are separate column solves (the advection after every stage, the diffusion once per step). ROMS folds the implicit advection into the diffusion's matrix; here that would need the advection on the diffusion's step only (the last stage), losing the per-stage constancy. Measured cost of the separate solves on Mausund: none visible (367 against 363 ms per step), since only ≈ 20 columns solve.
  - [ ] `Hydrostatic3D::time_step_limits` allocates the per-element viscosity (`vec![0.0; n_elements]`) every step; keep it in `ViscosityScratch3D`.
  - [ ] `GriddedAtmosphere2D::split_for_3d` panics on a clone, so a caller that shares the atmosphere (the open boundary's inverse-barometer level) must split first (`froya_real_data`'s `tide_setup` does). Splitting through the shared inner state, or a `split_for_3d(&self)` that clones the `Arc`, would remove the ordering trap.

### P4.6 3D validation (before any NorKyst 3D comparison)
- [x] Stratified lake-at-rest, linear N² ≤ 1e-12: the PGF to ≤ 1.5e-14 m/s² over a 30→400 m slope; the mode-split model to 2.8e-11 m/s after 1 h (P4.3 gates, 2026-09-30).
- [x] Stratified seamount at rest (Beckmann & Haidvogel 1993). Done 2026-10-01 (`examples/seamount_3d.rs`), and it found an instability: **a stratified fluid at rest over a slope was unstable**, even with constant N², which the PGF balances to round-off. The round-off velocity grew tenfold every 6 h (16 × 16 P2 elements of 4 km, 20 stretched levels, bed 40–400 m, `r_x0` 0.32; eighteenfold on 2 km elements) and blew up within 3 days, also at `r_x0` 0.08, at any time step, with horizontal viscosity (10 m²/s), vertical viscosity (1e-2), any vertical advection scheme, or a penalty on the layer velocities' face jumps.
  - Cause: the PGF and the density advection did not exchange energy (the PGF's `ρ∇z` must be the negative adjoint of the advection of the stratification along the sloping σ-levels). Growth per 6 h: constant-depth PGF + conservative advection × 11, + split advection × 5; σ-pairs + conservative × 3.5; σ-pairs + split none.
  - Fix: the tracers' horizontal advection in split form (`advective_divergence_element`), the PGF as σ-pairs `(p_j − p_i) + ½(ρ_i + ρ_j)(z_j − z_i)` (`PressureGradientForm::SigmaPairs`, the default; under GLL summation by parts the adjoint of the split form, and with the total potential energy `Σ gρz H_z` exact for any ρ horizontally), and `Hydrostatic3D::with_balanced_reference` to take back the σ form's error at rest (`F_z(ρ_s) − F_σ(ρ_s)` of a reference state added as a fixed field). Gates `a_stratified_seamount_at_rest_stays_at_rest` (7.4e-11 m/s after 36 h; constant depth 8.6e-8 and growing), `a_balanced_reference_gives_the_constant_depth_force_at_rest`. Cost: none (the step 117 → 111 ms in `profile_3d`; σ-pairs need no vertical search).
  - [ ] Tanh pycnocline (3 kg/m³ across 10 m at 30 m, 20 levels): at `r_x0` 0.08, 5 days, the balanced σ-pairs run spins up 0.38 → 1.5 cm/s (σ-pairs alone 4.1, constant depth 4.0 and accelerating). Still growing slowly: run 30 days, refine (nx, levels) to see whether it converges, and compare with Shchepetkin & McWilliams (2003, Fig. 10). At `r_x0` 0.32 it needed horizontal viscosity (Smagorinsky `C_s` 0.5: ≈ 7 cm/s, bounded over 4 days; without, the shear across the pycnocline blew up at 54 h). With the split-form momentum (2026-10-02) it is bounded without (rms 1.5–1.8 cm/s, max 0.2–0.3 m/s over 4 days), and Smagorinsky still lowers the error flow.
  - [ ] The vertical half of the energy exchange is not exact for curved profiles: the PGF integrates the pressure with Hermite-cubic means between levels, the tracers' vertical advection uses limited-Akima face values; both are exact for linear profiles only. Matching them (the face value of the vertical advection as the Hermite mean the pressure uses, or the pressure integrated with the advection's face values) would make the exchange exact for any ρ; check whether it removes the residual growth above.
  - [ ] The balanced reference is computed at the reference state's η: a tide moves the levels by `η(1 + σ)` and the reference's sampling with them (a relative error `η/D`). Refresh it (`set_balanced_reference`) when a parent model's stratification changes, or interpolate the correction to the current level depths; measure with a tide over the seamount.
  - [x] **The horizontal Kuzmin tracer limiter breaks the rest state over a slope**: with `kuzmin=1` the constant-N² seamount reached 0.15 m/s within 15 min and NaN at 2 h (flat bed: round-off). Done 2026-10-01 (`solver::limiters::tracer_3d`), in parts, each found by the one before failing:
    - Bounds at constant height: each node is bounded by its patch's columns of layer means interpolated linearly in `z` to the node's height (extrapolated past the end layers, clamped to the column's nodal range there, so a pit's bed node stays exact and a shoreline film cannot extend a step over metres). A linear `T(z)` is then within its bounds to round-off (unit gate `horizontal_kuzmin_leaves_a_linear_stratification_over_a_bump`).
    - That alone still blew up (2.8e-2 m/s at 2 h): Kuzmin scales the layer's whole deviation from its mean by one α, which over a slope is mostly the stratification along the tilted layer. A round-off clip at a node near the mean height (the P2 centre) set α well below 1 and flattened the layer: growth 2× per 300 s step. Now only the departure from the element's own column at each node's height (`T̂_i`) is scaled, about its inventory-weighted mean `m`.
    - `m` keeps the inventory and must go somewhere when α < 1. As a factor on the column structure (the same ill-conditioning) or as a fallback to the layer mean (it flattened the stratification once perturbations grew: blow-up on day 3), it destabilised the rest state. Now it goes into each node's room within its bounds in proportion, then into the element's widest bounds, then uniformly (without the second tier the P2 lock exchange overshot by 2.4e-9 °C).
    - Curved stratification: layer means cannot represent a pycnocline at the nodes' heights where σ-layers cross it steeply. `TracerLimiter3DConfig::with_reference_profile(TracerReferenceProfile)` limits `T − T_ref(z)` instead; at rest in `T_ref` that leaves 1.5e-5 °C, the profile's sampling error, against 1.8 °C of 17 without (gate `the_kuzmin_limiter_leaves_a_pycnocline_at_rest_with_its_reference_profile`).
      - [x] **The reference's own interpolation error drives a flow at rest under a sharp step.** Found 2026-10-07 with the viewer's 3D fjord (F.3): S 25 over 33 in a 3 m halocline, `from_fn` with 4001 samples over 161 m (4 cm apart) gave 4e-5 m/s within 10 min over the fjord's gentle slopes (r_x0 ≲ 0.05); without the limiter, 9e-11. The error fell 100× per 10× samples (4.8e-7 at 4 mm, 4.2e-9 at 0.4 mm): the limiter sees `T_ref`'s linear error (∝ spacing²) as a departure. Done: `TracerReferenceProfile::from_smooth_fn`, cubic Hermite between the samples with fourth-order slopes of the function (error ∝ spacing⁴; ≈ 10⁻⁸ of the step at 6 cm). The fjord at 2 cm: 3.6e-10 m/s after 1 h (gates `a_smooth_reference_is_exact_for_a_cubic`, `a_smooth_reference_converges_at_fourth_order`; the viewer's `the_stratified_fjord_stays_at_rest`). `from_fn` stays linear (a cubic overshoots at a piecewise-linear profile's kinks).
        - [ ] Move the smooth references to it and measure: Frøya's summer pycnocline without NorKyst (`froya_real_data` `tidal_run_3d` and the `levels=N` timing, `from_fn(…, 4001, profile)`; 4 m thick, so ≈ 40× less curved than the fjord's halocline, but over cliffs), `examples/seamount_3d.rs kuzmin=2`, and the gate at `src/simulation/simulation_3d.rs:3359` (8001 samples over 400 m). Frøya's NorKyst mean profile (`MeanProfile::at`, piecewise linear between its levels) should keep `from_fn`, or sample at its own levels with `new`.
    - 3D-patch bounds: each column also contributes the means of its two layers bracketing the node's height, as Thetis's vertex patches on prisms include the cells above and below. Without them the limiter cut smooth vertical extrema between thick layers (a displaced pycnocline's departure peaks where `∂T/∂z` does): at `r_x0` 0.32 it changed 55–75 % of the element layers every stage by up to 0.2 °C; with them half as many, and the rms error flow after 4 days 7.8e-3 m/s instead of 1.0e-2. The lock exchanges are unchanged (in range to 5e-12 °C).
    - Gates: `a_stratified_seamount_at_rest_stays_at_rest_under_the_kuzmin_limiter` (constant N², 36 h: 7.9e-11 m/s, 7.2e-11 without the limiter); the lock exchanges stay in range to 2–5e-12 °C, their fronts within 0.005 in Fr (P1 0.483 → 0.488: the bounds now follow `η`). `examples/seamount_3d.rs` (16 × 16 P2, 20 levels): constant N², `kuzmin=1`: 1.1e-10 m/s at 3 days (the same without the limiter), 3.6e-10 at 10 days. Tanh pycnocline at `r_x0` 0.08, balanced, `kuzmin=2` (the initial profile as reference): 5.1e-3 / 1.05e-2 m/s at 2 / 5 days, no limiter 7.0e-3 / 1.5e-2; `kuzmin=1` NaN by 24 h.
    - [ ] At `r_x0` 0.32 (balanced tanh, Smagorinsky `C_s` 0.5) the limiter still adds to the error flow: rms 7.8e-3 m/s after 4 days against 6.1e-3 without it (the flow is eddying: compare rms, not the noisy maximum). The exact solution is rest; at this `r_x0` the σ-pairs PGF is ≈ 30× off for departures from the balanced pycnocline, so every non-advective change of T over the flanks is a spurious force, while the advection exchanges energy with the PGF consistently (P4.6).
      - Literature (2026-10-01): Marchesiello et al. (2009, Ocean Modelling 26): any dissipation along σ over slopes is spurious diapycnal mixing, and "the accurate application of strictly monotonic schemes to the sigma-coordinates remains an unsolved and largely unacknowledged issue"; remedies are dissipating only a departure from a reference (Mellor et al. 1998: our `TracerReferenceProfile`) or dissipation rotated to geopotentials (RSUP3; the limiter's reset target `T̂_i` is at constant height). Lemarié et al. (2012, Ocean Modelling 42): only isoneutral dissipation damps the error growth over steep topography. Thetis (Kärnä et al. 2018): Kuzmin's vertex limiter in 3D on prisms (the 3D-patch bounds above). Aizinger et al. (2017, IJNMF 84): anisotropic limiting of each gradient component.
      - Tried and not kept: Kuzmin's (2010) hierarchical limiting at P2 (the departure's linear part and curvature limited separately, the curvature by the patch's mean gradients): rms 8.1e-3 alone, 1.1e-2 with the 3D-patch bounds; with Kuzmin's rule α₁ = max(α₁, α₂) the P1 lock exchange overshoots 2 °C (a front over two elements has a smooth gradient), without it the P2 one 2.6e-7 °C. Troubled-cell limiting (only layers whose patch has a face jump of the departure > θ × the bounds' spread, after Krivodonova et al. 2004): the best seamount yet (rms 4.2e-3 at θ = 0.3, 5.4e-3 at 0.1, both below no limiter), but the lock exchanges overshoot 0.003–2 °C at either θ: the overshoots grow where the field is steep but continuous, so face jumps do not find them. Wider bounds (`relax=3`): 7.3e-3.
      - Candidates: a detector that sees steep continuous fronts as well as jumps (e.g. the jump of the gradient, or the ratio of the highest modal coefficients, Persson & Peraire 2006) tuned against both the seamount and the lock exchanges; with T and S both stratifying, bounds and the reset target along isopycnals for the spice.
    - [ ] A source for the reference profile: a horizontal-mean profile of a state (binned in `z`) or of the parent model's fields (`OceanModelColumns`), refreshed as the nesting's stratification changes. Only `TracerReferenceProfile::new`/`from_fn` exist; a realistic run with fronts and a pycnocline needs one before it uses this limiter over slopes.
    - [ ] Periodic meshes: `Mesh2D::elements_at_vertex` has no periodic images, so a vertex on a periodic boundary has half its patch (a corner of a fully periodic mesh: one element). Kuzmin bounds there come from one side only; this affects the 2D limiters too (`limiters/swe_2d.rs:260`, `tracer_2d.rs:593`). Found with the seamount's corners.
    - [x] Cost (`examples/profile_3d.rs nx=64 ny=16 levels=20`, 24 threads): the limiter's hooks (`post_stage` + `post_process`, which also computes `w`) went 4.4 → 12 ms per step with the 3D-patch bounds, the step 108 → 114 ms. Done 2026-10-02, bit-identical (the run's state checksum is unchanged): the node heights are computed once per call, one search per column gives both the interpolated value and the bracketing means, and a layer whose nodes are all within their own column's bounds is left before its patches are read (they contain those bounds, so `α = 1`). The Kuzmin pass is 2.2 → 1.2 ms per call for `T` and `S`, the hooks 12 → 7.2 ms per step. The same run's largest speed is 0.056 m/s against 0.111 before (a sill and a pycnocline: the old limiter's clipping drove flow).
      - [ ] What remains per call (1.6 ms): the setup (node heights and layer-mean heights, 0.25 ms), each field's layer means and end ranges (0.25 ms, each recomputing `layer_thickness`, as does the main pass: one buffer of inventory weights per call would serve all three), and the layers that do need their patches. `T` and `S` share every height and search position: limiting them in one pass would halve the searches.
- [x] Constant-T preservation under tide over a sloping bed; Hz·T inventory conservation (P4.2 gates, 2026-09-30).
- [x] Wind setup τ/(ρgD); Ekman transport τ/(ρ₀f); column momentum after one implicit diffusion step = Δt·τ/ρ₀ (P4.1 gate tests).
- [x] Temporal convergence of the coupled scheme; barotropic energy decay (P4.1 gate tests).
- [x] Mode-1 internal-wave speed. Done 2026-09-30 (`a_mode_1_internal_seiche_has_the_internal_wave_speed`): a standing mode-1 seiche (5 km × 20 m, N = 0.05 s⁻¹, P2) against the free-surface hydrostatic speed (`tan(NH/c₁) = Nc₁/g`, 0.05 % below the rigid lid's `NH/π`): +0.04 % on 20 levels, +0.37 % on 10, second order in the level spacing; 0.13 % amplitude loss per period. At N = 0.1 and 0.2 s⁻¹ the model follows the free-surface correction (0.21 %, 0.83 %).
- [x] Lock exchange. Done 2026-09-30 (`a_lock_exchange_runs_at_the_gravity_current_speed`): an 8 km × 20 m channel, ΔT = 5 °C, P1 on 250 m with the horizontal Kuzmin limiter: both fronts at Fr = U/√(g′H) = 0.483 (0.475 at 125 m on 20 levels, 0.473 at 62 m), a little below Benjamin's ½, T bounded. At the middle the layers move at ±0.20 m/s = ½√(g′H); the interface thickens to ≈ 8 m (Ri ≈ ¼), so the exchange transport is only 0.40 of `(H/2)·½√(g′H)`: measure fronts, not transport. P2 needs horizontal viscosity (P4.5): done 2026-10-01, Fr 0.500/0.494 at ν = 10 m²/s (`a_p2_lock_exchange_runs_with_horizontal_viscosity`).
  - Fixed on the way: the horizontal Kuzmin tracer limiter checked only the vertices, so P2 edge and centre nodes overshot (0.46 °C in the P2 lock exchange; 7e-4 °C after). Every node is now bounded by the element's vertex patches (`horizontal_kuzmin_bounds_every_node_at_p2`).
- [x] Idealised fjord estuarine circulation. Done 2026-10-01 (`a_river_drives_an_estuarine_circulation_in_a_fjord`): a 10 km × 500 m × 10 m fjord, 200 m³/s of fresh water at the head, open to a sea of S = 30 through a characteristic OBC with a 2 km band relaxing only the tracers (a tracer-only parent, so the exchange flow passes freely). In 24 h: brackish outflow on top (0.2 m/s, surface S from 0.6 at the head to 25 at the mouth) over a compensating inflow at depth (Q_in 70–126 m³/s at 2.5–7.5 km), outflow growing seaward by entrainment, S in [0, 30]. Knudsen's relations with the storage landward of three sections close to 1e-5 (volume) and 1–3 % (salt; the section fluxes are nodal, not the DG face fluxes). 3.5 s.
  - [ ] Sognefjord-like: deep (hundreds of metres) behind a sill, stretched levels, GLS instead of constant κ (entrainment by the outflow's shear), Coriolis (the internal radius ≈ the fjord's width). Run to a steady state (the 10 m case is still freshening at 48 h: Q_in 76–176 m³/s against Knudsen's steady 86–198) and compare the brackish layer with Stigebrandt's (1981) two-layer theory: the layer thickness at the mouth from critical flow, the entrainment.
  - [ ] Exact section budgets: the model's own fluxes through a section (the layer face fluxes and tracer face fluxes of every stage, combined with the RK weights over a step) as a diagnostic. Through the open boundary it would make the salt budget exact (now 1–3 % from nodal `H_z u S`, averaged over the two sides of the face since 2026-10-02; one side alone missed the volume by 5e-4 in split form), and it is what transport-through-a-section output (P5.2, farm-site exchange) needs.
- [ ] Comparison with NorKyst-800 3D fields.

## Priority 5: Operational Features

### P5.1 River forcing
- [ ] NVE database (1,760 rivers in NorKyst-800) and a ROMS river-file reader onto `source::RiverSources` (vertical shapes: `RiverProfile`, done 2026-10-01; see P1.6). `Discharge2D`/`ConstantDischarge2D` (weak boundary inflow) and `RiverTracerSource` (tracer nudging, no volume) are superseded by `RiverSources`: delete them with the P6 cleanup.

### P5.2 ROMS-compatible output
- [ ] `NetCDFWriter` produces CF-1.8 but not ROMS-compatible output; add a ROMS variable-name mapping.

### P5.3 Restart / checkpointing
- [ ] HDF5 or NetCDF restart files (operational daily forecasts must hot-start).
  - [x] 3D restarts, for the multi-day 3D runs (15 days of the Frøya coastline mesh in 3D take ≈ 3–18 days of wall time, P1.3). Done 2026-10-06 (`io::Restart3D`, see CHANGELOG). A restart holds the whole `Solution3D`, the splitter's AB3 history of `G` (`SlowForcingRecord`) and the vertical reference, plus a domain fingerprint checked on resume. The barotropic pass starts from the state, and the forcing and clock are functions of time, so nothing else carries over. `froya_real_data tide3d=1 restart_hours=H` writes restarts, and `resume=<file>` continues one, with the station records and the snapshot file. Gate: `a_restarted_run_ends_bit_for_bit_where_the_uninterrupted_run_does`. Mausund, resumed in a new process: outputs byte-identical.
    - [ ] Only one restart file is kept (each replaces the last, atomically). For spin-up branching keep numbered copies (`restart_keep=`), or let the driver name the file per time.
    - [ ] The physics' configuration is not in the file: a resume with other options (mixing, limiter, nesting, wind) runs on silently from the restart's state. The example records the command line (`command` metadata) and prints it on resume. Compare the options that change the physics and refuse a mismatch, or store a configuration hash.
    - [ ] 2D runs (`Simulation`, `SWESolution2D` plus the multirate stepper's state) have no restart yet; the 15-day 2D runs take ≈ 4 h, so less pressing.
    - [ ] A NetCDF export of the restart (ROMS-like `ocean_rst.nc`) for other tools, once P5.2's output conventions are settled. The binary format stays the restart itself (exact `f64`, no native dependency, runs in CI).

### P5.4 Operational pipeline
- [ ] Automated run scripts, monitoring and alerting, THREDDS/OPeNDAP serving.

## Priority 6: Tech Debt and Structure (`REVIEW.md` §6.2)

- [ ] Move the remaining examples (`profile_cpu`, `quick_profile`, `high_res_benchmark`; Frøya is done) from the legacy `ssp_rk3_swe_2d` stepper and its `SWE2DTimeConfig` onto `Simulation` + `SWEPhysics2D`. This is the precondition for deleting the legacy integrators below. The two paths currently duplicate limiter and wet/dry dispatch.
- [ ] Delete the legacy integrators (`ssp_rk3*.rs`, three `coupled_swe_tracer` variants, `burn_ssp_rk3.rs`; ~2.4k lines). Make `CoupledState2D` implement `Integrable`; one time loop (fold `Simulation3D::run_with_callback`). `Simulation3D` still fires callbacks at the first step past each interval (drift); `Simulation` now lands on them exactly.
- [ ] Collapse the duplicate config enums (`SWEFluxType2D` vs `StandardFlux2D`; three limiter enums).
- [ ] Prune ~200 root re-exports to a `prelude`; export `Simulation3D`.
- [ ] Error handling:
  - `SimulationResult` → `Result<SimulationStats, SimulationError>`;
  - reachable panics (`timeseries_reader.rs:198`, `geotiff.rs:239-245`);
  - library `eprintln!` in `tide_gauge.rs`.
- [ ] Feature-gate `tiff`/`shapefile`/`geo` behind `geodata`.
- [ ] `solver/simd/` is mostly dead (2026-10-08): `compute_volume_terms_batched`/`BatchedVolumeWorkspace` (faer GEMM) has no caller, the SIMD `combine_derivatives` has none, and the rest serves only the `Standard` kernel slated for retirement. Retire the module with `Standard` (P1.2). (The `simd` feature's `pulp 0.18` is gone since 2026-10-08: its two kernels are on fearless_simd.)
- [ ] faer 0.23's own pulp paths (everything except matmul) stop at AVX2 on stable; its matmul already uses AVX-512 through `private-gemm-x86`. faer 0.24's `nightly` feature is now just `pulp/x86-v4` and builds on stable. It only matters for setup-time solves (harmonic analysis, bathymetry fits, the test spectra), so upgrade when convenient, not for speed.
- [x] ~~`cargo clippy` reports 103 warnings (2026-10-07, Rust 1.98) and CI runs it without `-D warnings`.~~ Done 2026-10-09 (branch `chore/clippy-pass`): none in any feature set (none, `parallel`, `simd`, `parallel,simd`, default with NetCDF; `--all-targets`, 157 warnings and one error before). `needless_range_loop` and `too_many_arguments` are allowed crate-wide in `Cargo.toml` `[lints.clippy]` (the 79 per-site allows removed). CI runs `--all-targets -- -D warnings`. Two lessons from `cargo clippy --fix`: it sees one feature set, so it removed an import that other feature sets needed and left a `#[cfg]` hanging on the next item (`examples/high_res_benchmark.rs`); and it rewrote five `for v in &mut x { *v = c }` loops as `&mut x.fill(c);`. Check every feature set after it.
- [ ] Build the `netcdf` feature in CI. It is excluded because it needs native HDF5/netCDF-C, so the NetCDF reader and nesting tests (P0.20) only run locally. `cargo check --features netcdf/static` builds HDF5 and netCDF-C from source with CMake and no conda (verified on Windows 2026-09-25: ~5.5 min cold, cached afterwards); use it for a CI job, and consider it as the documented local alternative to conda.
- [ ] Characteristic-based limiting for the 2D SWE system (1D version exists but is dead code; was P2.4). Likely subsumed by P1.2.
- [ ] Consolidate the root markdown files into `docs/`. Decide crate vs repo name; `dg-core`/`roms-rs` workspace split.

## Resolved (history)

### Priority 0 from the 2026-07-08 review — resolved (P0.11 deferred, above)
Details are in `CHANGELOG.md` and git history. Caveats found on 2026-09-25 are noted in brackets.

- **P0.5** Hardcoded 100 m depth in vertical diffusion: FIXED. Layer thicknesses come from η − B via `&Bathymetry2D`. Regression: `diffusion_layer_thickness_is_depth_dependent` (weak: it only checks that the profiles differ).
- **P0.6** Barotropic PGF double count: FIXED. `rho_ref` selects the baroclinic-only PGF. The endpoint/flat-average bug was fixed; Hann time filter centred at t+dt with correct moments. Regressions: `baroclinic_only_pgf_excludes_surface_slope`, `seiche_period_matches_analytic`, `cosine_filter_is_centred_at_baroclinic_endpoint`.
  - [Still open: the subcycle-in-every-stage structure, Forward Euler substeps, and the advection/Coriolis double count in G. See P4.1.]
- **P0.7** 3D walls transmissive: FIXED for Ω and momentum via the shared `boundary::reflect_velocity`.
  - [Tracer flux still leaks: P0.22.]
- **P0.8** NorKyst read the seabed layer: FIXED for s_rho files. `ubar/vbar` preferred; size guard skips staggered variables.
  - [Wrong for z-level files, and no rotation/transport conservation: P1.5.]
- **P0.9** Tidal phase sign (A·cos(ωt − G)): FIXED. Plus the `src/tides/` Doodson/Schureman astronomy (V₀, f, u for 15 constituents), epoch-aware BC builders, and harmonic nodal-correction inference (`reference_constants`). Verified correct in the 2026-09-25 review.
  - [Constituent periods are truncated: P0.15.]
- **P0.10** `--no-default-features` build: FIXED. `faer_par()` helper; CI (check matrix, nextest, clippy, fmt).

### Priority 0 from 2026-02-11 — resolved
- **P0.1** Depth-formula inconsistency (`h = η_tidal − B` in `HarmonicFlather2D`/`TSTOBC2D`): FIXED.
- **P0.2** RHS heap allocations inside element loops: FIXED for the element loops only.
  - [Per-step allocations remain: P1.1, P2.3.]
- **P0.3** Added `ROMSVstretching4`.
  - [It is **not** the standard Shchepetkin & McWilliams (2005) Vstretching 4, contrary to the original note: P4.3.]
- **P0.4** Affine-element assumption documented for cell averages and `GeometricFactors2D`.

### Priority 1–2 items from 2026-02-11 — done
- **P1.1** Tests: SWE convergence (linearised: P1 1.67, P2 3.02; see P1.7), Radiation2D unit tests, exact dam-break Riemann solver, 50-period periodic conservation, "multi-day" stability run (316 s).
- **P1.2** Horizontal viscosity: constant + Smagorinsky; conservative BR1 face coupling now shared with tracer diffusion; diffusive CFL helper.
- **P1.3** `SpongeLayer2D`; `BCContext2D::dt` plumbing (but `with_dt` has no callers: P1.4); binary search in `InterpolatedTidalBC`.

### Completed phases
- Phase 0: core 1D DG (Legendre, Vandermonde, operators, SSP-RK3).
- Phase 1: 1D SWE (Roe/HLL/HLLC, well-balanced, limiters, BCs).
- Phase 2: 2D tensor-product quads, 2D SWE, convergence verified.
- Phase 3: Norwegian-coast features:
  - tidal forcing, Chapman/Flather/TST-OBC, nesting;
  - wind, Coriolis, friction, atmospheric pressure;
  - wetting/drying, harmonic analysis;
  - tide-gauge and ADCP infrastructure.

- 3D scaffolding: sigma grid, stretching, `Solution3D`, mode splitter, Ω diagnostic, implicit vertical diffusion, baroclinic PGF (standard form), EOS.

- Audusse et al. (2004), hydrostatic reconstruction; Xing & Shu (2006); Xing, Zhang & Shu (2010).
- Zhang & Shu (2010), positivity-preserving limiters; Kuzmin (2010), vertex-based slope limiting; Vater, Beisiegel & Behrens (2019).
- Flather (1976); Chapman (1985); Kärnä et al. (2011), weak Flather in DG; Davies (1976), flow relaxation.
- Shchepetkin & McWilliams (2003), density-Jacobian PGF; (2005), ROMS split-explicit stepping.
